# BUGS

Real defects only — in our code or the engine. Not setup/housekeeping. Move to GitHub
Issues once public.

```
## B-NNN — title
status: open | fixed | wontfix   ·   severity: blocker | high | medium | low   ·   area: …
repro / expected / actual / cause / fix
```

## Known engine constraints (design around these — not bugs)

Refreshed 2026-09-02 (docs-reconciliation pass) — three items previously listed here are
solved and removed:
- ~~Render entry points are Tauri-coupled~~ — fixed by **D-014** (`render_core` extraction,
  done 2026-09-01); the grade path is callable headless (export, the eval harness).
- ~~Masks are static per image — no keyframe/tracking model~~ — SAM 2 video tracking shipped
  (**D-018**) and mask keyframes shipped (**D-034**, done 2026-09-02).
- ~~Depth Anything is wired for stills only; per-frame video flickers~~ — a real temporal
  depth track shipped (**D-036**, Video Depth Anything-vits, done 2026-09-02); the
  single-frame ONNX bake is still used for stills and pre-track, by design, not as a gap.

Still real:
- `process_and_get_dynamic_image` bypasses the GPU and returns the source unprocessed if
  `w|h > max_texture_dimension_2d` — watch at 8K.

## Open

## B-058 — The Motion tab said "No project open" for the whole session, with a project genuinely open — it inferred that from one read it made at boot, before any project existed
status: fixed (2026-09-05, D-150) · severity: high (the Motion tab was unusable for the entire session with no recovery path an owner would find; Edit and Colorist worked against the same project throughout) · area: `packages/motion/src/{useMotionManifest.ts,MotionTab.tsx}`, `app/src/main.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-05, owner-reported live with a screenshot — "Projects ▸ &lt;name&gt;" breadcrumb showing, Edit and Colorist both working against that project, Motion showing "No project open — Open a project in the Colorist tab to edit and render a motion manifest" with a Retry button.
- **repro:** launch the app, open any real `.chroma` project from the launcher, switch to the Motion tab. Nothing about the project matters.
- **expected:** the Motion tab loads that project's manifest (or the engine's sample manifest, for a project that has never saved one).
- **actual:** the "No project open" empty state, for the rest of the session.
- **cause:** two halves, and the second is the one that matters.
  1. **The read happened before there was anything to read.** `Shell.tsx` mounts every tab from boot and merely hides the inactive ones (`{tabs.map(…)}`, L308–315 — deliberate; B-007's note in `app/src/main.tsx` says so), so `MotionTab` → `useMotionManifest` ran its `load()` under the launcher with no project open. `chroma_motion_get_manifest` correctly rejected: `motion.rs::current_project_dir` (L35–39) returns `"no project open — open one in the Colorist tab"` when `state::current_project()` is `None`.
  2. **That failure was then recorded as a fact about the app.** `useMotionManifest`'s `catch` matched that exact string (via `manifestIO`'s `NoProjectOpenError`) and set `loadState = 'no-project'` — an answer with no input that could ever change it. Nothing pushed the app's real "a project is open" signal into this tab (`@chroma/editor` gets exactly that, `setProjectOpen` from the composition root — B-034/D-112), and the one thing that re-read was a window `focus` listener. **Opening a project from the in-window launcher blurs nothing**, so that listener never fired for the real case; only alt-tabbing out of the app and back happened to clear it, which is why the bug could read as intermittent.
- **not a D-148 regression**, though the timing suggested one: `state::set_project(Some(ProjectRef{…}))` still runs at `project.rs` L199, at the end of `open_manifest`, on every open/new/relink/add-shot/remove-clip path, and D-148's diff touches `set_project` only inside test code. It is provable from the screenshot alone, too — the frontend sets `projectPath` (hence the breadcrumb and the tab bar) only from the `ProjectOpenDto` that `open_manifest` returns *after* that line, so a visible tab bar means `set_project` had already run. This bug has been present since the tab shipped (D-046).
- **fix (D-150):** the readiness state moves out of the hook into `packages/motion/src/motionProjectStore.ts`, and the app pushes the signal in (`main.tsx` → `useMotionProjectStore.setProjectOpen`), mirroring B-034/D-112's shape. `load()` refuses to call the backend at all while the signal is false, so there is no error to misread; a read that fails while a project *is* open is `status: 'error'` with the real backend text; "No project open" now has exactly one cause. The signal is deliberately `!!projectPath`, narrower than the shell's own `projectOpen`: an in-memory "Untitled" loose-clip session has no project directory and therefore nowhere to keep a manifest sidecar, so the empty state (reworded to say so) is the truth there rather than a lie.
- **regression tests:** `packages/motion/src/motionProjectStore.test.ts`, 9 tests. Two encode this exact fault and were **confirmed to fail against the pre-fix behaviour** (the old inference + unguarded mount read temporarily restored inside the new store): `never runs at all while the app says no project is open` and `even the backend's own "no project open" text does not flip the signal`.
- **verification gap (honest):** proven by test and by tracing, not by driving the real Tauri window — this pass ran in a sandboxed worktree that cannot launch the app (and must not touch the owner's live dev server). Same constraint D-125/D-130 recorded for their timing fixes.
- **adjacent hole found, deliberately NOT fixed here:** `useSessionStore.closeProject` leaves the Rust `state::ProjectRef` pointing at the closed project (its own comment calls this harmless). That is not harmless for a loose-clip "Untitled" session opened afterwards — every `current_project_dir()` consumer (`edit.rs`, `motion.rs`, `export.rs`) would resolve into the *previous* project's directory. Motion no longer reaches that path (its signal is false for an Untitled session); the Edit tab's exposure is pre-existing and out of this pass's scope. Filed here so it is not re-derived from scratch later.
## B-059 — A camera keyframe's `ease` is supported by the engine and silently stripped by the schema, so every camera move in every manifest is stuck on the default easing
status: fixed (2026-09-05, D-159) · severity: low (a real engine capability is unreachable from a manifest; the fallback is the sensible `design.ease.inOut`, so nothing looks broken — it just cannot be changed) · area: `packages/motion-engine/src/engine/schema.ts` L32–37 (`cam2dKey`)
- **found:** 2026-09-05, writing `docs/notes/motion-tab-audit.md` (D-151) — while fact-checking that audit's own draft claim that Motion's camera interpolation was "linear, no easing control." That claim was **wrong**, and checking it turned up this instead.
- **repro:** put an `ease` on a 2D camera key in a manifest — `{"at": 1.6, "zoom": 1.5, "ease": [0.4, 0, 0.2, 1]}` — and parse it through `manifestSchema`. Verified live via a scratch script: the parsed key comes back `{"at":0,"zoom":1}`, i.e. `'ease' in key === false`.
- **expected:** the `ease` reaches `Camera`, which has genuinely supported it since the primitive was written — `CameraKey.ease?: readonly [number,number,number,number]` (`Camera.tsx` L20–26), applied as `Easing.bezier(...)` at L30 and consumed at L73. `Video.tsx` L29 already spreads the key through verbatim (`{ ...k, at: … }`), so the plumbing between schema and primitive is complete and correct on both sides — the schema is the only thing in the way.
- **actual:** `cam2dKey` is a plain `z.object({at, x?, y?, zoom?})`, and zod strips unknown keys by default, so `ease` is removed during validation before `Video.tsx` ever sees it. Every camera move in every manifest therefore runs on `design.ease.inOut` (`Camera.tsx` L46's `k.ease ?? design.ease.inOut`), with no way to say otherwise.
- **cause:** the sibling `layer` schema *is* `.passthrough()` (`schema.ts` L64) precisely so per-primitive props survive without being re-declared centrally — the author knew about the distinction and applied it one object over, but not to the camera keys. `cam3dKey` (L39–43) has the same strict shape — this bug's own original text guessed that was "harmless... since Scene3D has no ease concept," which **D-159 found to be wrong**: `Scene3D.tsx`'s `CameraRig` reads `CamKey.ease` the identical way (`cb(b.ease ?? design.ease.inOut)`), so `cam3dKey` had the exact same live bug, just never separately filed.
- **fix (D-159, applied exactly as proposed below):** `cam2dKey` (and, per the finding above, `cam3dKey` too) now declares `ease` as a real, typed `easeCurve` (`z.tuple([z.number(),z.number(),z.number(),z.number()])`) field rather than reaching for `.passthrough()` — visible to `propCatalog.ts`'s `CAM2D_KEY_FIELDS`/`CAM3D_KEY_FIELDS`, which now expose it as a real `[x1,y1,x2,y2]` JSON-tuple control in the Inspector's existing camera keyframe editor, completing this bug's own proposed fix text verbatim. The same `easeCurve` shape is also used by the new `transformKey` (D-159's per-layer keyframes), so a THIRD potential instance of this exact gap never gets a chance to exist.
- **verification:** `packages/motion/src/schema.test.ts` (5 tests) — an authored `ease` round-trips through `manifestSchema.parse` on a 2D camera key, a 3D camera key, and a new layer transform key; a key with no `ease` still parses correctly (no regression); and one test reconstructs the EXACT pre-fix `cam2dKey` shape inline, proves it strips `ease` (this bug's own repro, reproduced), then proves the current schema does not — a test that would have failed against the old schema, not merely one that passes against the new one. See D-159's own decision entry (`docs/08-decisions.md`) for the full write-up.

## B-061 — the Motion Inspector labels camera keyframe times in frames; the manifest stores seconds
status: open · severity: low (a mistyped value, not a crash — but a silently wrong one) · area: `packages/motion/src/propCatalog.ts` (`CAM2D_KEY_FIELDS`, `CAM3D_KEY_FIELDS`)
- **found:** 2026-09-05, reading the Motion tab for the D-152 visual-builder research pass. Not owner-reported.
- **repro:** Motion tab → select a scene's `Camera` row → the keyframe list editor (`InspectorPanel.tsx` L327–341) renders `CAM2D_KEY_FIELDS`, whose first field is labelled **"At (frame)"**. Type `48`, intending frame 48 of a 30 fps composition.
- **expected:** the key lands 1.6 s in. Every other timing field in the same Inspector is labelled "(s)" (`timingFields`' "Start (s)" / "Duration (s)", `SCENE_FIELDS`' "Duration (s)"), and the manifest is uniformly seconds.
- **actual:** the key lands at 48 **seconds** — past the end of most scenes, so the camera move silently never happens.
- **cause:** `propCatalog.ts` labels both `CAM2D_KEY_FIELDS[0]` and `CAM3D_KEY_FIELDS[0]` as `'At (frame)'`. The manifest's unit is seconds — `schema.ts`'s header says "All times are in SECONDS" and its own example uses `{"at":0.3,…}`; `Video.tsx` converts on the way in with `Math.round(k.at * fps)`. The stored value and the engine agree; only the label is wrong.
- **fix:** relabel both to `'At (s)'`. One word, one file, no data or schema implication. Worth doing alongside a step/precision pass on the field — a `1` step on a seconds value is a whole-second jump per keystroke, the same class of defect D-136 fixed on `ClipInspectorPanel`'s Position X/Y after the normalisation migration.

## B-060 — the ambient drift's rate is computed from a hardcoded 30 fps token, so the same manifest is not the same motion at two frame rates
status: open · severity: low (±3 px, deterministic, invisible at the default fps — a consistency defect, not a visible break) · area: `packages/motion-engine/src/primitives/Camera.tsx`, `packages/motion-engine/src/lib/draw.ts` (`ambientDrift`)
- **found:** 2026-09-05, reading `Camera.tsx` in full for the D-152 research pass — the camera transform had to be written out exactly to answer the coordinate-space question, and the drift term does not survive that. Not owner-reported.
- **repro:** render any manifest twice, identical but for `"fps": 30` vs `"fps": 60`. Compare the same wall-clock instant in each.
- **expected:** frame rate is a render-quality/timing knob; the *motion* — where things are at a given second — is the same. Every other time-driven value in the engine already honours this: `registry.ts`'s `f(sec, fps)` converts through the real fps, `Video.tsx` sizes each `<Series.Sequence>` from it, and every primitive reads `useVideoConfig().fps`.
- **actual:** the drift oscillates twice as fast in wall-clock at 60 fps, and 20 % slower at 24 fps.
- **cause:** both drift implementations divide the frame number by **`design.fps`** — a design *token* fixed at `30` (`design.ts` L32) — rather than by the composition's own fps. `Camera.tsx` L81–85: `design.ambientDriftPx * 2 * Math.sin((frame / design.fps) * 0.6)`. `draw.ts`'s `ambientDrift` (L36–39) repeats the same expression. `Camera` already calls `useVideoConfig()` for `width`/`height`, so the correct value is in scope and unused; `draw.ts` is a pure helper and would take `fps` as a parameter, the way `pop(frame, fps, delay)` in the same file already does.
- **fix:** use the composition's fps in both. `design.fps` stays as the *authoring default* (what a new manifest gets) and stops being read as a runtime constant. One consequence to accept before doing it: this changes rendered pixels for any non-30 fps manifest — correct, but a real look change, so it wants its own commit and a before/after frame rather than riding along with something else.

## B-062 — a key's `ease` had no validation anywhere against `Easing.bezier`'s own hard `x∈[0,1]` requirement, so an out-of-range hand-authored value renders fine right up until it crashes
status: open · severity: low (requires hand-editing the manifest text or a foreign tool to produce; the Inspector's own new curve widget, D-164, cannot produce this value by construction) · area: `packages/motion-engine/src/engine/schema.ts` (`easeCurve`), `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/bezier.js`
- **found:** 2026-09-05, building the Phase 5b curve/easing editor (D-164) — confirming `Easing.bezier`'s real signature against `remotion`'s own bundled source (not assumed) surfaced its own hard guard: `bezier(mX1,mY1,mX2,mY2)` THROWS `'bezier x values must be in [0, 1] range'` unless `0 <= mX1,mX2 <= 1`. `y1`/`y2` carry no such constraint at all (`design.ease.anticipate` already overshoots to `y1:-0.55`/`y2:1.55`).
- **repro:** hand-edit a manifest's `ease` to `[-1, 0, 2, 1]` (via the Inspector's manifest-text `</>` toggle, or any other tool/script that writes the JSON directly) and render/preview any key that reaches it.
- **expected:** either the schema rejects an `x1`/`x2` outside `[0,1]` at parse time (a clear, early error), or the engine clamps defensively before calling `Easing.bezier`.
- **actual:** `schema.ts`'s `easeCurve` is `z.tuple([z.number(),z.number(),z.number(),z.number()])` — any four finite numbers pass. Nothing between the schema and `interpolateKeys.ts`'s `Easing.bezier(c[0],c[1],c[2],c[3])` call checks the `x` range, so the manifest validates and the render throws only when playback/rendering actually reaches that key's own frame — a late, confusing failure mode (looks like an engine crash, not a data problem).
- **cause:** the schema was written (D-159/B-059) to fix a *different* gap — `ease` being silently STRIPPED — and correctly declared the field's SHAPE (a 4-tuple of numbers), but never encoded the one real semantic constraint the consuming function (`remotion`'s own `bezier()`) enforces at its own call boundary. A `z.tuple` has no built-in way to say "elements 0 and 2 specifically must be in `[0,1]`"; that needs a `.refine()` or a narrower per-position schema, neither of which existed at either B-059 or this pass.
- **not fixed here, deliberately:** this pass (D-164, the curve/easing editor) was explicitly scoped to a NEW INPUT WIDGET for an already-plumbed field, not a schema change — a real widget-side mitigation exists instead (`easeCurve.ts`'s `pixelToCurve` unconditionally clamps a drag's `x` to `[0,1]`, so the new Inspector control can never itself author an invalid value) — but that only protects the ONE authoring path this pass touched. The manifest-text editor (`ManifestEditor.tsx`'s raw JSON view) and any external tool/script remain unguarded. A real fix is a `schema.ts` change (a `.refine()` on `easeCurve`, or splitting it into a stricter tuple type) — flagged here rather than smuggled into a pass whose own task brief explicitly said "if you find yourself needing a manifest/schema change, stop and reconsider."
- **fix (proposed, not applied):** `easeCurve = z.tuple([z.number(), z.number(), z.number(), z.number()]).refine(([x1, , x2]) => x1 >= 0 && x1 <= 1 && x2 >= 0 && x2 <= 1, 'ease x1/x2 must be within [0,1]')` — a real parse-time rejection instead of a render-time crash, at the one place (`schema.ts`) already responsible for this field's shape.

## B-056 — `edit::PROBE_CACHE` never invalidates and is keyed on path alone, so a source file replaced in place serves stale `VideoInfo` for the rest of the session — defeating the staleness guarantee the disk cache directly beneath it was built to provide
status: fixed (2026-09-05, D-146) · severity: medium (wrong duration / frame count / resolution / fps everywhere downstream; recovery is an app restart, with no indication that is what is needed) · area: was `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` L93–128 (`PROBE_CACHE`, `probe_cached`); now `crates/chroma-media/src/probe.rs`
- **found:** 2026-09-05, reading `probe_cached` for the D-141 crate-extraction scoping pass (`docs/notes/crate-extraction-plan.md` §2.2) — `probe_cached` has to move out of `edit.rs` before `chroma-media` can exist, and it does not survive being looked at closely. Not owner-reported.
- **repro:** open a project with a clip. Scrub it, so `probe_cached(path)` populates. Without quitting the app, replace that file at the same path with a different encode of it — a re-export from another tool over the same filename, a re-render, an `ffmpeg -i … -c:v … same.mov`. Anything that changes duration, resolution or frame rate is enough. Now interact with the clip again.
- **expected:** the app re-probes and picks the new file's real facts up — which is precisely what the disk layer one line below is designed to do. `media_cache::source_key` is `blake3(path ‖ mtime_nanos ‖ len)` specifically so that "a re-encoded or replaced file never serves a stale probe" (`edit.rs` L106–108; `media_cache.rs` L89–104 argues the choice at length).
- **actual:** the in-memory layer *in front of it* returns the old `VideoInfo` and the disk layer is never consulted. Every consumer gets stale facts for the process lifetime: `edit::composition_size` (so D-136's whole composition-space model, and therefore every layer's normalised position and scale), `edit::build_from_shots` (clip lengths in frames), `filmstrip::chroma_clip_thumbnails` (strip extent — and the stale `info` is handed to `extract_thumb_chunk`, while `media_cache`'s own chunk key correctly misses, so it re-decodes with wrong metadata), `audio::cached_peaks`, `project`'s `MediaItem`/`MediaVideoInfo` DTOs, and `decode_pipe`'s `-ss (start-0.5)/fps` seek arithmetic.
- **cause:** `static PROBE_CACHE: Lazy<Mutex<HashMap<PathBuf, VideoInfo>>>` is **insert-only**. `grep -rn PROBE_CACHE` finds four sites: the declaration and three inside `probe_cached` (one read, two inserts). There is no `clear`, no eviction, no TTL, and — the actual defect — no re-validation of the key against the file's current mtime/size, even though the very next lines compute exactly that value for the disk lookup. The two-layer cache therefore has two different keys for the same question, and the weaker one is in front. `chroma_project_relink` looks like it should paper over this and does not: it calls `video::probe` directly for the manifest, and relinking points at a *new* path anyway (a new key), so it never touches the stale entry for the old one.
- **fix (D-146, applied in the `chroma-media` commit-2 move exactly as this entry proposed):** validate on hit. `media_cache::source_key(path)` is now computed **first**, before the memory lookup rather than after it, and `PROBE_CACHE` stores it beside the `VideoInfo` (`HashMap<PathBuf, (String, VideoInfo)>`). A hit whose stored key differs from the file's current one falls through to the disk layer and then to a real `ffprobe`, overwriting the entry; both layers now answer the same staleness question with the same key instead of the weaker one shadowing the stronger. Cost is one `stat` per hit — the same order as the `HashMap` lookup it guards, and orders of magnitude below the `ffprobe` spawn it avoids (measured: an unchanged file is still >5x cheaper warm than cold, asserted by `an_unchanged_file_is_still_served_from_memory`).
- **one deliberate decision the entry did not cover:** when `source_key` fails outright — the file cannot be `stat`ed, i.e. the media went **offline**, which `project::media_item_is_online` treats as a real supported state — the remembered `VideoInfo` is served *unvalidated* rather than becoming an error. Re-probing a file that cannot be opened would fail anyway, and reporting a clip's known duration beats reporting nothing. Pinned by `an_offline_file_still_serves_its_remembered_facts`. Correspondingly, a probe with no `source_key` is **not** memoised at all — an unkeyed entry would be precisely the insert-only cache this bug was about.
- **regression test:** `chroma_media::probe::tests::a_source_file_replaced_in_place_is_re_probed_not_served_stale` — synthesises a real 320x240/1s clip with `ffmpeg`, probes it, overwrites the same path with a real 640x480/2s clip, and asserts the second probe reports 640x480. **Confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code** (the old body temporarily restored in place): `left: 320, right: 640`.

## B-057 — `filmstrip::CHUNK_LOCKS` leaks its entry whenever a chunk extraction fails, because the cleanup sits after the `?`
status: fixed (2026-09-05, D-146) · severity: low (a bounded leak of small entries; no user-visible symptom found) · area: was `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/filmstrip.rs` (`load_chunk`); now `crates/chroma-media/src/filmstrip.rs`
- **found:** 2026-09-05, reading `load_chunk`'s locking for the D-141 crate-extraction scoping pass. Not owner-reported, and filed as low rather than dressed up: it is a real defect with a one-line fix, not a performance problem.
- **repro:** request a filmstrip window for a clip whose extraction fails — a media-offline clip (`project::media_item_is_online` is a real supported state), a codec `ffmpeg` refuses, or the class of failure B-055 documents. Each *distinct* failing `(source, level, index)` adds a permanent entry.
- **expected:** `CHUNK_LOCKS` holds an entry only while a chunk is genuinely being worked on, which is what the `remove` at the end of `load_chunk` exists to enforce.
- **actual:** on the failure path `let bytes = extract(...).await?;` returns early, so `CHUNK_LOCKS.lock().remove(&key)` never runs and the `String` key plus its `Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>` stay in the map for the process lifetime.
- **cause:** the cleanup is placed after the fallible call rather than tied to the guard's lifetime. Growth is bounded by the number of distinct chunk keys that have ever failed, not unbounded — which is why this is low and not medium — but `CHUNK_LOCKS` is the one map in this module with no bound at all (`CHUNK_MEM` has `MAX_CHUNK_MEM`, `KEYFRAME_MEM` is per-path, the disk cache has `CACHE_BUDGET_BYTES`), so it is the one place the module's own discipline is missing.
- **fix (D-146, applied in the `chroma-media` commit-3 move):** a `ChunkLockGuard { key }` whose `Drop` removes the entry, taken immediately after the `tokio` mutex guard and never released early. Drop order is the reverse of declaration, so the entry is removed **while that lock is still held** — byte-for-byte the ordering the old success path had, which this entry's own "checked and explicitly NOT a bug" note already traced as safe. The trailing `CHUNK_LOCKS.remove(&key)` statement is gone entirely; there is now no path out of `load_chunk` (early `Ok` from either `CHUNK_MEM` re-check, the `?` on a failed `extract`, or the normal return) that leaves an entry behind.
- **regression test:** `chroma_media::filmstrip::tests::a_failed_chunk_extraction_does_not_leak_its_chunk_lock` — writes a real file whose contents `ffmpeg` refuses (so it has a real `source_key` and therefore genuinely takes the lock path), calls `load_chunk`, asserts the call really failed and that `CHUNK_LOCKS` is back to its prior size and no longer holds that chunk's key. **Confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code** (the guard removed and the old trailing `remove` restored in place): `left: 1, right: 0`. Counted as a delta, not an absolute, because `CHUNK_LOCKS` is a process-global and `cargo test` runs the binary's tests in parallel. Paired with `a_successful_chunk_load_also_releases_its_chunk_lock` (env-gated on `CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO`) so the guard cannot regress the path that already worked.
- **checked and explicitly NOT a bug:** the neighbouring question — whether removing the entry *while still holding the lock* lets a second task start a duplicate `extract` — was traced and does not reach. A third caller arriving in that window hits `CHUNK_MEM` (`remember` runs before the `remove`), and even if `remember`'s wholesale `mem.clear()` had just evicted it, `media_cache::write_blob` has already run before `remember`, so that caller takes the disk hit and never re-decodes. Recorded here so it is not re-derived as a suspicion later.

## B-053 — A lone clip's transform was silently discarded by the preview: the single-layer fast path skipped compositing unconditionally
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-132) · severity: high (an Inspector control that writes the file and changes nothing on screen) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`timeline_frame`)
- **found:** 2026-09-04, while wiring D-132's per-clip crop through the Edit-tab compositor. Not reported by the owner — found by asking "what happens when there's only one clip," which is the first thing anyone would try a crop on.
- **repro:** one video clip on one track, nothing above or below it. Select it, set Opacity `0.5` (or Scale `2`, or Rotation `45`) in the Edit-tab Inspector.
- **expected:** the preview shows the change, the way it does the moment a second visible video track exists.
- **actual:** nothing changes on screen, at any playhead position or preview quality. The value really is written to `project.json` and really is read back into the Inspector field — only the picture never reflects it.
- **cause:** `timeline_frame`'s `match layers.as_slice()` had `[(track, clip, source_frame)]` as an **unconditional** arm — "exactly one visible layer needs no compositing at all" (D-088). True for a *plain* clip, and a real, worthwhile fast path (it skips a full-frame RGBA clone, an alpha pass and an `overlay`); false for a clip carrying any of the D-082 transform fields, which only `composite_layer_onto` ever applies. So the transform was reachable in the UI, persisted, keyframeable — and invisible, for the entire single-track case, which is most projects.
- **fix (D-132):** the arm is now guarded by `resolve_clip_transform(clip, *source_frame).is_identity()`. A genuinely untransformed clip still takes the plain-decode path byte-for-byte (the common case costs exactly what it did before); anything else falls through to the real compositor. `is_identity()` deliberately shares `crop_pixel_rect`'s clamp semantics so the predicate can never disagree with what would actually be painted.
- **remaining caveat at the time this was written, since closed:** this makes a lone clip's `position_x`/`position_y` visible for the first time — and at the time, those were still **B-043**-affected (absolute pixels in a canvas whose size follows the preview decode scale), so a lone clip's *position* would still shift between scrubbing and playing until Phase 0a of `docs/notes/on-canvas-transform.md` landed. Opacity, scale, rotation and D-132's crop were already ratio-based and correct at every preview scale. **B-043 closed the same night (D-136)** — position is now ratio-based too, so this caveat no longer applies to any of the five fields.

## B-054 — A `chroma-timeline` test pinned to the owner's live `project.json` asserted a premise that expired the moment they used the feature it was testing
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-132) · severity: low (test-suite only — but it fails on `main`, on correct data) · area: `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs` tests
- **found:** 2026-09-04, the first `cargo test -p chroma-timeline` run of the D-132 fork, before any of that fork's own code existed.
- **repro:** `cargo test -p chroma-timeline` on any machine where `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma/project.json` exists and has had a clip dropped into it since D-129 shipped.
- **actual:** `tests::the_owners_real_project_json_loads_with_every_clip_unlinked` fails — `left: Some("lg-54a79586-…"), right: None`.
- **cause:** the test read the owner's **live** project file and asserted `link_group == None` on every clip, as D-129's backward-compatibility evidence. True when written, but its actual premise was "the owner has not used A/V linking yet" — and D-129 shipped the feature that makes the app write `lg-…` groups into that very file. The test was therefore guaranteed to start failing, on correct data, on the owner's own machine, as soon as the feature worked.
- **fix (D-132):** the test keeps its live-file evidence and its skip-if-absent guard, but now asserts only claims that stay true as the user works — that every clip in the real file loads with the migration defaults of a field that file genuinely predates (D-132's four crop insets), and that `backfill_legacy_positions` resolved every `start_frame`. Renamed `the_owners_real_project_json_loads_with_migration_defaults`.
- **standing lesson:** a test pointed at live user data may only assert properties that are monotone under the user using the app. "This file has no X yet" is not one; "a field added after this file was written defaults correctly" is.
## B-055 — A filmstrip window that runs past the end of the source loses its *whole* strip, not just the out-of-range tail
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-134) · severity: medium · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/filmstrip.rs`
- **found:** while fixing B-049, by running the env-gated filmstrip tests against the owner's own `Screen Recording 2026-08-10 at 9.21.13 PM.mov`. Not owner-reported. Reproduced identically on `main`, so it is D-128's, not D-134's.
- **repro:** `chroma_clip_thumbnails` for a window whose end is past the source's duration — the 8.023s screen recording is enough on its own, because an 8-second chunk boundary lands at exactly 8.000s with 0.023s of empty tail behind it.
- **expected:** the tiles that exist, and nothing for the part that doesn't.
- **actual:** `Err`, and the clip renders no filmstrip at all.
- **cause:** `ffmpeg -ss` at or past the last frame reads no packets, so its encoder is never opened and it exits non-zero (`ff_frame_thread_encoder_init failed` / `Nothing was written into output file`). `extract` turned that into an `Err` and D-128's chunk loop propagated it out of the whole request. Latent under a serial loop; D-134's concurrent `join_all` would have made it worse, since one such chunk would abort every sibling decode with it.
- **fix (D-134):** drop chunks with no real frame in them before the request (`chunk_start + 1/fps < duration`), and downgrade a genuinely failing chunk to a `warn` + its own missing tiles rather than the request's failure — with the real error still returned when nothing in a request worked at all, so a broken file is still loud (D-124's standing lesson). Regression test: `a_window_running_past_the_end_of_the_source_still_returns_its_real_tiles`.

## B-049 — Timeline filmstrip zoom (in or out) recomputes thumbnails very slowly, even though normal scroll/load is now fast (D-124/D-128)
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-134) · severity: medium (D-124/D-128 fixed load/scroll but zoom itself regressed) · area: `packages/editor/src/Filmstrip.tsx`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/filmstrip.rs`
- **found:** owner, live, 2026-09-04: "i do zoom in and zoom out its takes like forever to calculate the thumbnail also thumblain rest looks amazing... very fast now" — so this is scoped specifically to the zoom action, not general scroll/load, which the owner confirms is fixed.
- **repro:** open a project with a loaded clip on the Edit-tab timeline, click the zoom +/- controls (or the `%` readout) repeatedly.
- **expected:** near-instant re-render at the new zoom level, same speed as the now-fixed scroll/load path.
- **actual:** a long stall before thumbnails redraw at the new zoom level. Measured on the owner's `A001_08302215_C019.MOV` over one fixed 32s window of source: 9.2s, 9.6s and 12.5s for the three zoom-in rungs after a warm 0.5s window — **31.3s for one fine-band sweep**.
- **cause, confirmed by measurement (D-134):** the hypothesis below was right about the mechanism and understated the size. Yes, D-128 keys a chunk by `(source, level, index)`, so a zoom step is cache-cold. But the reason that costs so much is that it is cold **for every chunk of the viewport at once** and D-128's request loop `await`ed them one at a time — a 5-chunk window at the default zoom paid 5 × ~2.2s serially while two of `EXTRACT_SEMAPHORE`'s three permits sat idle. And the whole re-decode was avoidable: a fine chunk costs the same to decode at 0.0625s spacing as at 0.5s (8s of that file is ~2.15s either way — ffmpeg decodes every frame regardless below the keyframe gate), so the four fine levels were paying full price four times for the same seconds. The two rival explanations B-049 listed were checked: a main-thread decode was disproved by reading the path (`chroma_clip_thumbnails` is `async`, `extract` goes through `spawn_blocking`), and the request pile-up is a consequence of the cold keys rather than a separate throttle bug.
- **fix (D-134):** decode and store every fine level **once**, at the finest rung, and serve the rest by decimating it — exact, because the LOD ladder is powers of two; plus load a request's chunks concurrently, and derive a coarse chunk for free from cached finer ones. **Fine-band zoom sweep: 31.3s → 0.001s**, and the cold first window 9.45s → 7.15s. Regression test: `a_zoom_step_over_an_already_decoded_range_costs_no_decode` (fails on `main`).

## B-050 — Player's seek bar and the volume-hover slider are effectively invisible: only a lone white dot floats above the transport row, no track/line, and the volume control shows nothing at all on hover
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-131) · severity: high (the Edit tab's transport, again — a seek control you can't see the state of is unusable) · area: `packages/ui/src/components/ui/slider.tsx`, `packages/player/src/Player.tsx`
- **found:** owner, live, three screenshots. "no white visible thingy line for how much is done" (seek bar) and "no slider for player voice" (volume).
- **repro:** open the Edit tab preview with a clip loaded; look above the transport button row.
- **expected:** a horizontal track showing playback progress (seek), and a volume slider revealed on hovering the mute icon (D-126 built this as a hover-expand `w-0 → w-16` container).
- **actual:** the seek slider's **thumb** renders as a bare white circle with no visible track under it; the volume slider shows nothing at all, track or thumb, even on hover.
- **cause, confirmed by compiling this app's real Tailwind build, not guessed:** the CSS token theory was wrong — `--color-muted`/`--color-primary` are real, defined, visible colours in this build (checked directly). The real bug: `slider.tsx`'s Track/Control/Root/Indicator used the shorthand `data-horizontal:`/`data-vertical:` Tailwind variant, which compiles to a presence check on a literal `[data-horizontal]` attribute. Base UI's Slider never sets that attribute — it stamps `data-orientation="horizontal"` (confirmed by reading `@base-ui/react`'s `getStateAttributesProps`) — so those classes never matched anything. Track/Indicator got no explicit size, collapsed to `auto` height (0, since their only content is absolutely positioned and doesn't contribute to layout), and a 0-height `overflow-hidden` div renders nothing regardless of its background colour. The Thumb's classes (`bg-white`, `size-4`) are static, not gated behind this broken variant, so it alone stayed visible — the exact "lone white dot" reported. The volume slider's total blankness is the same collapse one level further in: its `w-0 overflow-hidden group-hover/volume:w-16` wrapper had nothing to give it height either, so even on hover the Thumb inside it was clipped by the wrapper's own `overflow-hidden`.
- **fix:** `data-horizontal:`/`data-vertical:` → `data-[orientation=horizontal]:`/`data-[orientation=vertical]:` throughout `slider.tsx` (the correct form, matching upstream shadcn's own source for this component). Also fixed in the same file/pass: `Player.tsx` passed both sliders a bare number (`value={frame}`) instead of an array (`value={[frame]}`) — harmless in effect (Base UI's real internal state still had one value; the wrapper just mis-rendered 2 stacked `Thumb` DOM nodes instead of 1) but a real correctness bug, fixed at the call site. See D-131 for the full compiled-CSS evidence and an honest gap on live-window verification (not possible in this sandbox).

## B-051 — Sources-panel toggle icon still visually sits on top of the "Timeline" title text
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-131) · severity: low (cosmetic, but D-126 claimed this fixed) · area: `packages/shell/src/Shell.tsx`, `packages/player/src/Player.tsx` (title strip)
- **found:** owner, live: "timeline and window button for source still kinda on top of each other" — screenshot shows the Sources toggle chip directly over the "T" of "Timeline", reading "◫neline".
- **repro:** open the Edit tab with the Sources panel closed.
- **expected:** D-126's "elevated chip" styling was supposed to stop this crowding (bg+border+shadow, distinguishing it from the title strip).
- **actual:** the chip is opaque now but its position is unchanged — it still overlaps the title text itself, not just sits near it.
- **cause, confirmed:** D-126 fixed *legibility* (an opaque chip over transparent-ghost) but never touched *position*. The Sources toggle's real footprint (`Shell.tsx`, `absolute top-2 left-2 h-6 w-6`) is an 8px inset, 24px square — right edge at x=32px. `Player.tsx`'s title strip used a uniform `px-3` (12px both sides), so "Timeline" started at x=12px, squarely under the chip.
- **fix:** `Player.tsx`'s title strip padding changed from `px-3` to `pl-10 pr-3` (40px left, clearing the toggle's real 32px edge with an 8px gap), reserved unconditionally rather than only when Sources happens to be open — the toggle is shell-level and floats over this same corner for any tab embedding `<Player>`, not just Edit. Deliberately did not move the toggle itself: D-118/D-120 place it there specifically so it stays reachable regardless of active tab/panel state. See D-131.

## B-052 — Pressing Play/Pause restarts just the audio, even with a real linked audio track present on the timeline
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-133) · severity: high (audio played the wrong part of the media on every Play, on the owner's main footage) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs`
- **found:** owner, live, on the build with D-129 (linked audio tracks) and D-130 (session-restart fix) both merged: "play nad pause restart the audio just audio even though its showing timeline and this is here" — screenshot shows a real `Audio 1` track with waveform, linked to `Video 2`.
- **repro:** open `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma` (its `Audio 1` clip points at `A001_08302215_C019.MOV`), press Play, then Pause, then Play again.
- **expected:** audio resumes from the playhead, in step with the picture.
- **actual:** audio started from **0:00 of the source file on every Play, at any playhead** — so Play/Pause/Play made the take begin again from the top each time, while the picture correctly carried on from the playhead. Only the audio, because the video preview seeks through `ffmpeg`, which has no such problem.
- **cause:** `open_source` and `decode_mono_range` asked `symphonia` to seek and threw the result away (`let _ = format.seek(...)`, commented "worst case playback starts from wherever the reader already is"). That worst case is the actual case for this file, every time. `symphonia-format-isomp4-0.6.1`'s `IsoMp4Reader::seek` (`SeekTo::Time` arm) seeks every *other* track to the requested time with `?` before the one it was asked for — under a comment claiming it discards the result, which it does not. That MOV carries an ordinary camera-original metadata track (timebase `1/1_000_000_000`, total duration `41_666_667` ns = one 24 fps frame), so any seek past 0.042 s is out-of-range for *that* track and the whole call fails, leaving the audio track un-seeked at sample 0. Pre-existing since D-050; D-125 (sessions became audible immediately) and D-129 (a visible waveform to compare against) are what made it noticeable, not what caused it.
- **not** a D-130 regression: D-130's causes were real and its tests still pass. This is a third, older defect that looked the same from the outside.
- **fix:** D-133 — sources reach their start time by trimming on the packets' own timestamps (`packet_skip` / `StartTrim`), correct whether the seek succeeds, fails, or lands early; the seek is kept only as the fast path and its failure is logged rather than swallowed. Measured catch-up cost on the same 2.3 GB file: 56 ms to 60 s, 249 ms to 300 s (packets before the target are never decoded).
- **verification:** 4 pure unit tests on `packet_skip`; a real-decode test on an `ffmpeg`-synthesized `.mov` whose video track is shorter than its audio track — reproducing the identical upstream seek failure without needing a camera original — asserting a source opened at 3.0 s starts in the tone rather than the silent head of the file; and an env-gated test on the owner's own `A001_08302215_C019.MOV` asserting 0 s and 60 s no longer decode to identical samples (they did, byte-for-byte, before the fix — that measurement is what root-caused this). **Honest gap:** not verified by clicking Play in the assembled app (same environment constraint D-125/D-130 disclosed), and nobody has *listened*.

## B-047 — The audio session was restarted mid-playback by an unrelated object change, and D-125 removed the ordering that made restarts safe — so the voice repeated a couple of seconds, or vanished
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-130) · severity: high (the Edit tab's transport again — audio that repeats itself is worse than audio that is merely late) · area: `packages/editor/src/PreviewPane.tsx`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs`
- **found:** owner, live, on a binary rebuilt **after** D-125 merged: "the voice is lagging or just loops", then "yeah voice is overlapping couple of seconds also".
- **repro:** open `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma` and press Play in the Edit tab's preview, especially with the window not already focused (that click also fires `EditorTab`'s `focus` → `load()`).
- **expected:** one audio session, started once, from the playhead Play was pressed at.
- **actual:** audio starts, then a fraction of a second later starts again from a different point — the same words twice — and intermittently does not start at all.
- **cause, two layers.** (1) `PreviewPane`'s audio effect was keyed `[playing, timeline]`, and `timeline` is a **new object** on every `timelineStore.load()` (including `EditorTab`'s window-`focus` refetch, which fires on the very click that starts playback) and on every `applyOp`. Each replacement ran the effect's cleanup (`chroma_audio_stop`) and body (`chroma_audio_play` at whatever the playhead had reached) — a real teardown-and-restart of the session, for a change the session does not depend on at all (`chroma_audio_play` resolves its sources in Rust, from the open project). (2) D-125 changed both audio commands from `#[tauri::command]` to `#[tauri::command(async)]`. Read from the source, not assumed: `tauri-macros-2.6.3`'s `command/wrapper.rs` routes `ExecutionContext::Async` on a sync `fn` to `body_async` → `tauri-2.11.5`'s `ipc::InvokeResolver::respond_async_serialized` → `async_runtime::spawn` → **`tokio::spawn` on the multi-threaded runtime**, whereas `Blocking` runs the body inline on the main thread as IPC messages drain. So the FIFO ordering *and* mutual exclusion the play/stop protocol depends on silently disappeared. With (1) firing two plays and a stop within a few hundred ms, that lands constantly: if the **older** play's task reaches a worker last it wins the generation and playback starts from a stale `start_frame` (audio replaying a stretch the picture has passed — "overlapping a couple of seconds"); if the pause's stop lands after the resume's play, the new session is killed and there is no voice at all.
- **why it appeared only now:** before D-125 a session spent its first 157-635 ms emitting silence while it warmed up (B-040's cause 2), so a session superseded within a few hundred ms had never made a sound and the double-start was inaudible. D-125's prefill + skew compensation makes a session audible essentially immediately — correct, and exactly what exposed this.
- **fix:** key the audio effect on `timeline !== null` rather than the object; and make the ordering explicit instead of inherited — both commands carry a monotonic `seq` stamped by the frontend at issue time, and `begin_request` drops anything a newer request has overtaken (the same request-token pattern `timelineStore.ts`'s `load()` uses — B-034/D-112). Reverting to `Blocking` was considered and rejected: it puts the thread join back on the main thread and leaves the invariant implicit and untestable. See D-130.
- **verification:** 4 new tests, each confirmed to **fail** against the pre-fix behaviour (re-run with the staleness check removed) and pass with it — including one that runs 8 stamped transport commands from 8 threads in whatever order the OS gives them and asserts the highest stamp owns the session at the end. **Honest gap:** cause (1) is a React effect-dependency change argued from the real call graph, not observed firing in a running window — `packages/editor` has no React-render test harness. The owner pressing Play is what closes it.

## B-048 — A clip's audio kept playing after the picture cut away from it: sources streamed to the end of the *file*, ignoring the clip's out-point
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-130) · severity: medium (audio that is not on the timeline at that position; on a stacked timeline it plays under a picture that has already cut elsewhere) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{audio.rs,edit.rs}`
- **found:** while root-causing B-042, by reading `run_session` against the owner's real `New.chroma` timeline.
- **repro:** a timeline where a short clip sits above a longer one — the owner's is exactly this: a 166-frame screen recording on track 0 over a 12414-frame camera take on track 1, both at `start_frame: 0`. Play from frame 0.
- **expected:** at frame 166 the picture cuts to the clip below and the screen recording's audio stops with it.
- **actual:** the screen recording's audio carried on underneath, for as long as its source file lasted.
- **cause:** `AudioSourceSpec` carried only a source path and a start second. `open_source`/`DecodedSource` had no idea the clip under the playhead had a length, so a session streamed each source until the **file** ended. Pre-existing since D-050; unrelated to D-125.
- **fix:** `resolve_video_position`'s caller and `resolve_audio_track_positions` now report the clip's out-point measured forward from the playhead; `DecodedSource::remaining` counts down to it, `take` pads silence past it, `is_done` respects it. `Clip::end_frame` made `pub` so both callers use the model's own arithmetic rather than re-spelling it.
- **scoped out:** re-resolving *which* sources are active as the playhead crosses a clip boundary mid-session — D-050's documented deliberate limitation and a real feature, not a drive-by. Until it exists the correct behaviour past a clip is silence, not the wrong clip's audio. On the roadmap.
- **verification:** two real-decode tests through the real `open_source`/`take` path (a synthesized 3-second tone opened as a 1-second clip: audible inside, **exact** `0.0` past the out-point; and a source with no out-point still playing on), both confirmed to fail against the pre-fix behaviour.

## B-040 — Clicking Play took 2-3 s and then the audio lagged: the multi-layer preview respawned an `ffmpeg` process per layer per frame, and the audio clock started late and never caught up
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-125) · severity: high (the Edit tab's transport — the app's most basic interaction — was unusable on a real multi-track project) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{decode_pipe.rs,edit.rs,audio.rs}`, `packages/editor/src/PreviewPane.tsx`
- **found:** owner, live, two messages: "play is not smooth or after clicking it takes like 2-3s lag and no voice comes", corrected moments later to "voice comming but laging".
- **repro:** open `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma` (three video tracks all with content at the playhead, two of them the same 517s 4K HEVC file) and press Play in the Edit tab's preview.
- **expected:** picture starts promptly at roughly the timeline's frame rate, audio in step with it.
- **actual:** several seconds before anything moved, then a slideshow, with audio audibly behind the picture.
- **cause, measured, four things — and *not* the one that was suspected.** The leading hypothesis was contention with D-121/D-124's filmstrip thumbnail generation. Disproved: the regression test written for this fix builds a fresh throwaway project and decodes only preview frames — no `Filmstrip`, no `chroma_clip_thumbnails`, no competing `ffmpeg` — and still measured **618-660 ms per displayed frame**. D-124's 105-second filmstrip decodes were real and compounding on the owner's machine, but they are a separate defect; removing them would not have made Play responsive. The real causes: (1) `decode_pipe` kept **one** process-global `ffmpeg` pipe, while D-088's compositor decodes *every visible layer* through it — and a pipe respawns on a path change *or* a backward frame step, both of which every layer after the first triggers, so three visible layers cost three process spawns plus three keyframe seeks into a 2.3 GB file **per displayed frame**. (2) `chroma::audio`'s `run_session` started the `cpal` stream *before* opening its sources, so the callback emitted silence for the whole warm-up; the ring buffer is a plain FIFO with no timestamps, so that head silence is never made up and audio stays permanently that far behind — measured at **157 ms warm / 431-635 ms cold**, against a ~45 ms broadcast tolerance. (3) `chroma_timeline_frame` was a plain `#[tauri::command] fn`, which Tauri runs **on the main thread** (confirmed by reading `tauri-macros`' `command/wrapper.rs`), so a decode stalled the whole event loop and delayed `chroma_audio_play` — whose latency *is* the A/V offset in (2). (4) Scrub and play requested different preview long edges, which changes the scaler arguments and so respawned every pipe on **every** Play/Pause toggle (a measured 550-650 ms), and the play loop additionally re-requested the frame already on screen, a backward step that respawned them again.
- **fix:** a `PipeSlot`-keyed pool of decode pipes (one per Edit-tab video track, one for Colorist's single clip) with per-frame release of slots that are no longer visible; `-hwaccel videotoolbox` with a real software fallback in `decode_pipe` (extending D-121's thumbnail-only application, byte-identity re-verified for this path on both of the owner's sources); real start-skew compensation plus a 150 ms prefill before the audio device starts, capped at 2 s so it can never silently skip audible content; the heavy commands moved off Tauri's main thread; and one `PREVIEW_LONG_EDGE` for both scrub and play.
- **verification:** **618 ms/frame → 23-31 ms/frame** through the identical Rust code path against a timeline shaped like the owner's real project, before and after run back-to-back under identical machine load (660 ms → 17 ms on an idle machine) — the "before" produced by temporarily collapsing every slot back onto one shared pipe and re-running the same test, not inferred. `cargo test -p RapidRAW chroma::` 173/173; clippy and `cargo fmt` introduce nothing new; `tsc` clean; `packages/editor` vitest 166/166. **Honest gap:** not verified by clicking Play in the assembled Tauri window — this environment could not launch it and the main tree was the owner's live dev server. The owner pressing Play on `New.chroma` is what actually closes this. Full writeup: D-125 in `docs/08-decisions.md`.

## B-041 — `chroma_audio_play` fails outright when a genuine `TrackKind::Audio` clip points at an audio-only file
status: open · severity: medium (blocks D-057's Phase C multi-track audio the moment anything populates an audio track; no user impact today because nothing does yet) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`resolve_audio_track_positions`)
- **found:** while verifying D-125's audio work. Two existing env-gated tests, `chroma_audio_play_mixes_a_genuine_audio_track_with_the_video_track` and `chroma_audio_play_with_a_muted_audio_track_still_plays_the_video`, fail whenever `CHROMA_TEST_AUDIO_VIDEO` is set. **Confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to D-125** by stashing that work and re-running against a clean tree — both fail identically there.
- **actual:** `chroma_audio_play with a video track + a genuine audio track: "probe /…/tone.m4a: no video stream in /…/tone.m4a"` — the whole play call errors, so nothing plays at all, not even the video track's own embedded audio.
- **cause:** `resolve_audio_track_positions` (D-057) calls `edit::probe_cached` → `video::probe` on an **audio-only** source, and `video::probe` requires a video stream by construction. It then converts the clip's `source_start` to seconds with `VideoInfo::frame_to_secs`, i.e. the *source's* frame rate — which an audio-only file does not have either.
- **why it is not fixed here:** the fix is not a guard, it is a semantics decision — an audio clip's frame positions have to be interpreted against the **timeline's** rate rather than a source video's, and `probe_cached` needs an audio-only path that returns something other than a `VideoInfo`. That is a real design call belonging to D-057's Phase C/D, not a drive-by inside a playback-lag fix, and it cannot affect the reported symptom because no project in the app can populate an audio track yet. Logged rather than half-fixed.

## B-043 — The Edit tab's multi-layer composite geometry is preview-resolution-dependent: a PIP overlay moves AND resizes when you press Play
status: fixed (2026-09-04, **D-136**) · severity: medium (real, visible, and had been a hard blocker for on-canvas transform handles — see `docs/notes/on-canvas-transform.md` Phase 0a) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`composite_video_frame`/`composite_layer_onto`/`composition_size`), `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs` (`Clip::position_x`/`position_y` semantics, `Timeline::normalise_legacy_positions`), `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/project.rs` (the schema-minor migration gate)
- **repro:** put two video clips on two video tracks so they overlap in time, give the lower one a non-zero `position_x` in the Inspector, then press Play and watch the overlay against the paused frame.
- **expected:** the composite is the same picture at any preview resolution — preview scale is a quality knob, not a layout input.
- **actual (pre-fix):** the overlay jumped to a different place, and (for some source/preview combinations) a different size, the moment playback started.
- **cause:** `composite_video_frame` made its canvas out of *the top layer's decoded pixel dimensions*, and every layer was decoded through `decode_pipe::scale_target(w, h, max_long_edge)` where `max_long_edge` is whatever the caller passed. `PreviewPane.tsx` passes `SCRUB_LONG_EDGE = 960` while paused and `PLAY_LONG_EDGE = 640` while playing. Two separate consequences: (1) `position_x`/`position_y` were absolute pixels *in that canvas*, so `position_x: 200` was 20.8% of the width at 960 and 31.25% at 640; (2) `scale_target` returns `None` when a source already fits under the cap, so a 640×360 overlay over a 4K background was 2/3 of the canvas at scrub and exactly full-frame at play — i.e. `scale: 1.0` didn't mean a fixed thing either. The same offsets also meant different things for a 4K source and a 720p one. Nothing had forced this into the open earlier because there is still **no timeline export path** — `chroma::export::export_video` is Colorist's single-clip path — so no code had ever had to answer "what resolution is the composition."
- **fix (D-136):** the timeline gets a real composition space — `ProjectSettings.width`/`height` (D-038), or the timeline's first video clip's probed resolution as fallback. `composite_video_frame`'s canvas is now that composition (downscaled uniformly to `max_long_edge` for the preview), each layer's footprint is its own SOURCE resolution mapped through the composition (not its decoded buffer's size), and `Clip::position_x`/`position_y` are normalised fractions of that composition — correct at every preview scale by construction, the same reasoning D-132's crop insets already used. A new `chroma_timeline_clip_geometry` Tauri command reports a clip's natural composition-space footprint for a frontend overlay to use. **Existing pixel-valued positions are migrated**, not silently reinterpreted in place: `chroma.project`'s schema gained a minor (`1.1`, `NORMALISED_GEOMETRY_MINOR`), a pre-`1.1` file's positions are reinterpreted as composition pixels and divided down on load (`Timeline::normalise_legacy_positions`, D-136 in `docs/08-decisions.md` has the full reasoning for why this can only be a stated reinterpretation, and why every project that never used a PIP offset — i.e. every project until someone drags one — migrates to bit-identical zeros), and the file is restamped so the same values are never divided twice.
- **verification:** `cargo test -p chroma-timeline` 115/115 (4 new migration tests); `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib -- chroma::edit::` 23/23, including `geometry_is_identical_at_every_render_scale` — the actual regression test, the same decoded layer buffer composited into a 100×100 and a 300×300 canvas, asserting the painted fraction agrees within 1% at both. Full verification and the Phase 1 on-canvas-handles work landed alongside this: D-136.

## B-042 — Crop (and straighten / flip / lens correction) is silently discarded on a Colorist video export: the preview shows one frame, the encoded file contains another
status: fixed (2026-09-04 — **D-127** made it fail loudly, **D-135** made it actually work; the export now applies crop / straighten / flip / 90° / lens warp for real) · severity: high (a WYSIWYG break that ships wrong pixels with no warning of any kind) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/export.rs`
- **repro:** load a video in Colorist, open the Crop panel, drag a real crop, export the video.
- **expected:** the exported file matches the preview — or, failing that, a refusal that says so. `docs/08-decisions.md` D-022 and `docs/09-engine-notes.md` both *state* that "crop/ROI on video errors out."
- **actual:** the export succeeded and produced a full-frame, un-cropped file. No error, no warning, no log line. Same for straighten (`rotation`), 90° orientation steps, horizontal/vertical flip, and the perspective/lens-correction warp.
- **cause:** all of that geometry is applied on the **CPU**, in `adjustment_utils::apply_all_transformations`, as a pre-pass before the GPU grade — `AllAdjustments` carries no geometry at all. The Colorist *preview* runs it (`process_preview_job` → `compute_full_transformed_res`), and so does the still export. `chroma::export::grade_frame` does not: it goes straight from the decoded frame to `render_core::render`. The guard that was supposed to catch this compared the graded frame's dimensions against the source's *inside the encode loop* — but with no geometry pass in that path nothing can ever change the frame size, so the check was unreachable by construction and had never fired.
- **first fix (D-127, stopgap):** a real pre-flight in `export_video` — `unsupported_geometry(js, w, h)` returned the names of every non-identity geometry control in the grade doc and the export refused before ffmpeg was spawned. Honest, but not the destination; that function is now deleted.
- **real fix (D-135):** `grade_frame` calls `adjustment_utils::apply_all_transformations` — the *same* pre-pass the live preview and the still export run, not a re-implementation — and builds its mask bitmaps at the **transformed** size with the **real** crop offset (`EXPORT_MASK_SCALE = 1.0`, the preview's `effective_scale` collapsed for a full-res render), including the two relight resolvers that carried the same hardcoded zero. The encoder is spawned **lazily, from the first graded frame's measured dimensions**, so a crop or a 90° step really changes the encoded size and the number ffmpeg is told cannot drift from the pass that produced it. The dead in-loop dimension check is replaced by one that can fire: every frame must transform to the same size as the first. D-019's tracked mattes needed no change — `generate_ai_bitmap_from_full_mask` already un-does crop/rotation/flip against a full-resolution matte, so passing the real offset *is* the fix (D-019's parenthetical corrected in place). h.264's even-dimension requirement is a stated rule: **round the encoder size down to a multiple of 2, for every codec**, trimming ≤1 row/column with `crop_imm` rather than padding or resampling — the ffmpeg `trunc(iw/2)*2` / HandBrake-modulus-2 convention. That also closes a latent D-049 hole where an odd custom export resolution went straight to libx264. Bonus, dropped by the same original omission and undocumented until now: the **lens blur** and the parametric **`color`/`luminance` masks** now work on video export too.
- **still refused, and only this:** a crop that rounds to zero in either axis (a sub-2-pixel drag). `resolve_encoder_dims` errors before the encoder is spawned, naming the size and the rule. Nothing can encode that frame; it is an impossibility, not a deferral.
- **verification:** real ffmpeg encodes probed back — a `{20,10,100×60}` crop on a 160×120 clip produces a **100×60** file whose frame 0 matches the source's own frame 0 cropped by `image` (mean |Δ| < 10, against > 20 for the un-offset region, so the assertion can't pass by luck), byte-identical across two runs; `orientationSteps: 1` encodes **120×160**; a `101×61` h.264 crop lands at **100×60** instead of libx264 refusing. Plus 13 GPU-free unit tests on real pixels. `chroma::` suite 231/231. Full detail and the honest gaps: D-135.

## B-045 — Every derived artefact Chroma caches died on process exit, so reopening a project always paid the full cold cost
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-129) · severity: high (a felt, repeatable multi-second wait on the single most common action in the app — opening a project) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{edit.rs,audio.rs,video.rs,media_cache.rs,filmstrip.rs}`
- **repro:** open `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma`, wait for the Edit tab's filmstrips and waveforms to appear. Quit the app. Open the same project again.
- **expected:** the second open is near-instant — nothing about the source files changed, and every expensive thing was computed a minute ago.
- **actual:** identical cost both times. `ffprobe` re-runs per clip, every filmstrip is re-decoded by `ffmpeg`, every waveform is re-decoded by `symphonia`.
- **cause:** every cache in the codebase was a module-level `Lazy<Mutex<HashMap<..>>>` static — `edit::PROBE_CACHE`, `edit::THUMB_CACHE` (D-119/D-121/D-124), `state::THUMB_CACHE` (D-033), `project::MANIFEST_CACHE` (D-114). All process-local by construction, so all wiped on every launch. Invisible during a session, which is why three rounds of filmstrip work never surfaced it; owner found it by feel ("we are doing actions which can be cached again and again... most editors do it already"). RapidRAW's own still-image thumbnail cache in this same repo has been disk-backed all along (`app_cache_dir()/thumbnails`); the video side never got the equivalent.
- **fix:** `chroma::media_cache` — a persistent, source-keyed (`blake3(path ‖ mtime ‖ size)`) blob store under `app_cache_dir()/chroma`, with filmstrip chunks, `VideoInfo` probes, waveform envelopes and measured keyframe intervals all persisted through it. Budget-pruned, atomic writes, every failure is a miss rather than an error.
- **verification:** measured cold vs. warm-from-disk on the owner's own 2.3 GB 4K source through a new env-gated integration test that clears the in-memory caches between runs (exactly what a relaunch does): a 16-second filmstrip window of 32 tiles goes **5.41s → 0.007s**, returning byte-identical pictures. Full writeup: D-129, `docs/notes/media-cache.md`.

## B-046 — Opening a project decoded a full-resolution frame of every clip and threw every one of them away
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-129) · severity: high (~1.9s per 4K clip, on the critical path between clicking a project card and anything appearing, for zero benefit) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{project.rs,load.rs}`
- **repro:** open a project with more than one 4K clip. Time the gap between the click and the Edit tab.
- **expected:** one real decode — the clip actually being shown.
- **actual:** one full-resolution decode per clip, each `ffmpeg`-decoding a 3840×2160 frame and PNG-encoding it, then `image::load_from_memory`-ing it back.
- **cause:** `open_manifest`'s per-clip loop called `load::load_video_frame`, which probes *and* decodes *and* installs into `AppState.original_image`. But the loop only ever needed the session bookkeeping (path + probe info + playhead). Every iteration overwrote the previous iteration's pixels, and the `seek_and_install` immediately after the loop overwrote the last one too — so not one of those decodes was ever displayed. It also re-probed via `video::probe` directly rather than `edit::probe_cached`, so it missed the probe cache that already existed. The owner spotted the symptom without seeing the code: "if you are loading 4k that might be wrong."
- **also:** the same call in `chroma_project_resync_clips` clobbered `AppState.original_image` with a newly-added clip's pixels, directly contradicting that function's own documented promise to leave the active shot undisturbed.
- **fix:** `load::register_video_shot` — probe (disk-cached) and register, no decode. Used by both call sites. A probe failure still means "offline"; a file that probes but can't decode is still caught, by the real decode the active clip goes through immediately afterwards.
- **verification:** the discarded work is 1.9s per clip, measured directly with the exact `ffmpeg` invocation `decode_frame` builds, against the owner's own `A001_08302215_C019.MOV`.

## B-044 — The waveform had no cache at all, and re-decoded a clip's entire audio on every zoom step, on the main thread
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-129) · severity: high (the same defect D-124 fixed for the filmstrip, still live in the sibling path, and blocking the UI thread while it ran) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs`, `packages/editor/src/Waveform.tsx`
- **repro:** open a project with a long clip that has audio; zoom, or drag a panel divider to resize the timeline.
- **expected:** zoom re-draws the waveform from data already held; at most one decode per clip range, ever.
- **actual:** a full `symphonia` decode of the clip's whole source range on every zoom step and every resize.
- **cause:** two things compounding. `chroma_audio_waveform` had **no Rust-side cache** — `edit.rs`'s own comment asserted it had "its own module-level cache", which was simply not true (found by reading, this pass). And `Waveform.tsx` keyed its frontend cache on `buckets`, which it derives from the clip's *on-screen pixel width* — so every width change was a new key and therefore a new decode. That is the identical mechanism D-124 diagnosed and fixed on the filmstrip (`count` derived from pixel width), never checked against the sibling path it was explicitly modelled on. Compounding it: the command was declared `pub fn`, not `pub async fn`, and a non-async Tauri command runs on the **main thread** — so the decode blocked the thread that paints the window.
- **fix:** a fixed-resolution (128 buckets/sec) envelope, cached in memory and on disk, keyed on the source range only and re-bucketed in memory for whatever width asks; the command is now `async` + `spawn_blocking`. `edit.rs`'s incorrect comment corrected. A real `log::info!` per actual decode, none on a cache hit — the D-124 discipline this path never got.
- **scope, honestly:** the fix is entirely backend. `Waveform.tsx` still keys its own module-level `Map` on `buckets`, so a width change still costs an IPC round trip — but that round trip is now a cache hit plus an in-memory re-bucket instead of a full audio decode, and it no longer runs on the main thread. Removing the redundant round trip too would mean re-bucketing in the browser, which is a bigger change to a file this pass otherwise didn't need to touch.

## B-039 — A clip's filmstrip never appeared, with no error and nothing in any log, because generating it took 105 seconds and every zoom step started another one
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-124) · severity: high (a shipped feature that looked simply broken for two rounds of fixes, because the failure was invisible by construction) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/video.rs`, `packages/editor/src/{Filmstrip.tsx,TimelinePane.tsx,ruler.ts}`
- **repro:** open the owner's real project (a 6.9s screen recording on track 0, a 517s 4K HEVC clip on tracks 1-2), then zoom. The screen recording's filmstrip never appears; `/tmp/chroma-tauri-dev.log` contains no thumbnail/ffmpeg/hwaccel line at all.
- **expected:** every video clip shows its filmstrip promptly, and zooming re-tiles it without refetching.
- **actual:** nothing renders on that clip, indefinitely, with no error anywhere.
- **cause, measured not guessed:** `extract_thumb_strip_range` decoded *every* frame of the clip's source range and discarded ~99.5% of them in a `select` filter — **105.5s** for one strip of the 517s 4K clip (timed directly, `-hwaccel videotoolbox` already on). Separately, D-119 derived the requested frame `count` from the clip's **on-screen pixel width**, so a zoom sweep produced `count` 64 → 41 → 20 → 10: four cache keys, four more 105-second decodes, each blanking the strip until it returned. Three of those saturate D-121's `Semaphore(3)`, and a short clip's one-second request queued behind them waits minutes. Nothing logged because `chroma_clip_thumbnails` only logged on hwaccel *failure* — a slow decode and an absent one were indistinguishable from outside. Two disproved leads are recorded in D-124 (the clip's project data is valid, and its codec decodes fine in 1.1s) so neither gets re-chased.
- **fix:** keyframe-only decode above a measured 4.0s sampling threshold (`-skip_frame nokey`), `fps=` time-based sampling instead of a frame-index stride (also fixes VFR sources and an off-by-one), thumbnails scaled to 2x the 52px row instead of 150px, `count` derived from clip duration instead of pixel width, render-time downsampling for zoom, and one `log::info!` per real decode. Also fixed: the fetch effect early-returned on `width <= 0` with `width` absent from its dependency array, and a failed fetch was cached permanently.
- **verification:** 105.5s → **9.27s** for a whole-file 64-frame strip plus a 4s sub-range, through a new env-gated integration test against the owner's actual 4K file (2.96s for the screen recording); a live 59-step zoom sweep on the real component went from one backend call per zoom bucket to **2 total**, with 1 blank render instead of one per bucket. Full writeup: D-124.

## B-038 — Two `chroma::project` tests have been failing on `main` since D-123 landed, one of them panicking outright
status: fixed (2026-09-04, D-124) · severity: medium (a red test suite on `main` — no user-facing symptom, but it hides the next real regression) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/project.rs`
- **repro:** `cargo test -p RapidRAW --no-default-features chroma::` on `main` at `cf94658`.
- **expected:** green, as D-119 last reported it (165 passed, 0 failed).
- **actual:** 2 failed — `remove_clip_by_id_lifts_it_and_clears_active_clip` panics `index out of bounds: the len is 0 but the index is 0`, and `track_commands_add_remove_and_move_clip_on_the_active_timeline` fails `removed from track 0`.
- **cause:** D-123 made a track that an edit just emptied get pruned automatically. Both tests still asserted the old "empty tracks persist" contract — one indexing `tracks[0]` after the only clip on the only track was removed (so `tracks` is now empty), the other expecting the source track to survive a cross-track move. D-123 updated `timeline.ts`'s mirrored test for exactly this and missed the two Rust-side ones; its verification covered `chroma-timeline` and `packages/editor` but never ran the app crate's own suite.
- **found:** during D-124, which stashed its own changes and re-ran on the clean tree to confirm the failures were pre-existing rather than its own.
- **fix:** both assertions brought up to the shipped contract (no behaviour change) — `cargo test -p RapidRAW chroma::` is 167 passed / 0 failed.

## B-010 — `ExportPanel` (`Panel.Export`) is reachable and appears usable while a video clip is loaded, but silently attempts a still-image export against the video's own path
status: open · severity: low (a working, prominent alternative — `ExportDialog`, D-049 — now exists; this is dead-end UX, not data loss) · area: `app/src/components/panel/right/ExportPanel.tsx`, `app/src-tauri/src/export_processing.rs`
- **repro:** open a video clip in the Colorist tab, click the "Export" icon
  in the panel switcher (`Panel.Export`), pick any format, click Export.
- **expected:** either the panel is unavailable for video content, or it
  exports something sensible from the clip (a frame, or the graded video).
- **actual:** `selectedImage.path` for a loaded video shot is the clip's own
  file path (`useSessionStore`'s `applyLoaded` — no still/video branch), so
  `ExportPanel`'s `numImages`/`canExport` read as if a real image were
  selected. Clicking Export calls `Invokes.ExportImages` →
  `export_processing.rs`'s `export_images_impl`, which `image::open()`s the
  path as a still — this will error (or, worst case, silently mis-handle a
  path `image` happens to partially parse) on a video container.
- **cause:** `ExportPanel` is RapidRAW's unmodified still-image exporter; it
  was never taught about `useChromaStore.videoInfo`/video-ness, because
  nothing in the Colorist pivot (D-039/D-043) ever routed a real video
  export through it — that gap is exactly what D-049's `ExportDialog`
  fills, via the correct backend (`chroma_export_video`).
- **fix (not done — out of scope for D-049):** gate `Panel.Export`'s
  availability/rendering on `!videoInfo?.isVideo` (simplest), or teach
  `export_images_impl` to route a video path to the real video-export path
  instead of `image::open()`. D-049 deliberately left `ExportPanel` fully
  routed rather than partially unrouting it — see that decision for why.

## B-012 — Drag a Sources-panel clip onto the Edit-tab timeline: lands with no real timeline position, silently breaking playback/edits from that point on
status: fixed (2026-09-03, D-058 + D-064) · severity: high · area: `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts`, `TimelinePane.tsx`, `app/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`
- **D-058 fixed the model, but D-064 found the drag still didn't fire at all in the real app — see D-064.** D-058's own live verification ran against a plain Chrome tab with Tauri mocked out (the documented workaround for this sandbox having no screen access to the real Tauri window); the owner's very next live retest in the actual app showed drag-and-drop still completely inert. Root cause, found by D-064: Tauri v2's window-level native drag-drop capture (`dragDropEnabled`, on by default, never set in `tauri.conf.json`) intercepts drag events before they reach the page's own HTML5 handlers — a bug the Chrome-tab method structurally could not have caught, since no Tauri runtime was present to intercept anything in that test. `dragDropEnabled: false` fixed it; confirmed by the owner's own live retest ("yeah drag and drop works").
- **found:** owner's own hands-on testing of the live app, 2026-09-03 — D-046 (2026-09-02) and D-054 (earlier the same day as this fix) had each been "verified" in isolation (D-046: code review only, flagged honestly as such; D-054: Rust-only, explicitly out of frontend scope) but never exercised together by a real drag.
- **repro:** open a project with at least one shot on the Edit tab's timeline, drag any item from the Sources panel onto the timeline strip.
- **expected:** the clip appears at the end of the track and plays back correctly at that position.
- **actual:** the clip's array entry appears (rendering happened to still look plausible, since `TimelinePane`'s own render — before this fix — derived on-screen position from summed preceding durations, not any stored position field), but the persisted `project.json` clip has no real timeline position; scrubbing to it in the preview (`chroma_timeline_frame`, which *does* read the real backend position) shows the wrong frame or nothing, and any further edit compounds the corruption.
- **cause:** D-054 (same day, landed after D-046) gave `chroma-timeline::Clip` a mandatory, timeline-absolute `start_frame: i64` field. `chroma_timeline_set` stores whatever the frontend sends **verbatim** — there is no server-side clamping — so `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts`'s pure-TS mirror of the edit ops, not the Rust ops in `lib.rs`, is what actually runs for every edit made through this UI. That TS file's `Clip` interface, and every op that builds a clip (`clipFromDraggedMedia` chief among them), was never updated for the new field — a dropped clip's JSON simply had no `start_frame` key. Rust's `#[serde(default = "legacy_missing_start")]` silently accepted that as the pre-D-054-migration sentinel (`i64::MIN`) rather than erroring, and `load_manifest`'s `backfill_legacy_positions` — a **migration** path, never intended to run on a live edit — reconstructed *some* position from Vec order on the next `chroma_timeline_get`, which happens to look right for a bare "append to an untouched track" but silently corrupts (duplicate/overlapping positions, no clamp) as soon as a split, reorder, or second drop is layered on top. Neither D-046 (code-review-only, explicitly flagged as its one unverified piece) nor D-054 (Rust-only by design, explicitly deferred "no frontend" — see that decision) individually missed anything they claimed to cover; the gap was structural, between the two.
