  five caret-pinned `@remotion/*` packages to the exact `4.0.519` used
  everywhere else. **A newly-added/changed `overrides` key did not reliably
  take effect via an incremental `npm install`, even after `rm -rf
  node_modules`** — the existing `package-lock.json`'s already-resolved
  entries for those packages kept getting replayed. Only a genuinely fresh
  resolution — `rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json && npm install` —
  produced a lockfile with exactly one `remotion` install (root, `4.0.519`,
  confirmed via `find . -path "*/node_modules/remotion/package.json"`).
- **verification:** a real `npm run tauri:dev` boot, twice, with the dev
  server stdout and the app's own log file both staying clean for the
  whole session — no "Multiple versions of Remotion" error, no
  frontend-ready timeout — versus every prior boot attempt this session
  hitting the error within seconds of the frontend starting to evaluate.

## B-008 — `@react-three/fiber`'s global JSX augmentation breaks any `React.ElementType`-typed component sharing its `tsc` program
status: fixed (2026-09-02) · severity: medium · area: `packages/ui/src/Text.tsx`, `app/src/components/panel/BottomBar.tsx`
- **repro:** in a `tsc` program that includes any file importing
  `@react-three/fiber` (e.g. `@chroma/motion-engine`'s `Scene3D.tsx`/
  `ParticleFlow.tsx`, first pulled into `app`'s program by wiring the
  Motion tab, D-046) alongside a component that renders a
  `React.ElementType`-typed prop via JSX (`<Component {...props}>` where
  `Component`'s static type is `React.ElementType`, not a concrete tag).
- **expected:** unrelated files type-check independently of what else is in
  the program.
- **actual:** the unrelated component fails to type-check —
  `error TS2745: This JSX tag's 'children' prop expects type 'never'…` and
  similar `never`-typed prop errors — even though nothing about that
  component changed.
- **cause:** `@react-three/fiber` augments the **global** `JSX.IntrinsicElements`
  interface (that's how `<mesh>`, `<group>`, etc. type-check anywhere) — a
  program-wide effect, not scoped to files that import r3f. Once present,
  `React.ElementType` (`keyof JSX.IntrinsicElements | ComponentType<any>`)
  is a much larger union that now includes r3f's custom intrinsics, whose
  prop shapes don't share a compatible `children` type with DOM elements.
  TS computing the intersection of props across that whole union for a
  dynamic `<Component>` JSX call collapses `children` (and other props) to
  `never`. Two pre-existing components used this "dynamic tag via a
  `React.ElementType` prop, rendered with JSX" pattern and both broke the
  moment r3f entered the same program for the first time: `@chroma/ui`'s
  `Text.tsx` (`as` prop) and `app`'s `BottomBar.tsx`'s `PanelToggleButton`
  (`Icon` prop).
- **fix:** `React.createElement(Component, props, children)` /
  `createElement(Icon, { size: 18 })` in place of JSX for that one call in
  each file — behaviorally identical (JSX desugars to the same call at
  runtime; this changes nothing about what renders), but `createElement`'s
  generic signature doesn't distribute over `JSX.IntrinsicElements` the same
  way JSX's own type-checking does, so it type-checks correctly whether or
  not r3f's augmentation is present.
- **verification:** a strict `tsc --noEmit` diff of `app/` before vs. after
  the full Motion tab change (file list, not just count) came back
  byte-identical (64 errors either side) — used specifically to catch every
  instance of this pattern application-wide, not just the ones exercised by
  manually clicking through the UI. Any *other* `React.ElementType`-via-JSX
  component elsewhere in the codebase would show up the same way if r3f's
  augmentation reaches its program later.

## B-007 — Edit tab stuck on stale "no project open" after opening a project in Colorist
status: fixed (2026-09-02) · severity: medium · area: app/src/main.tsx, @chroma/shell tab persistence
- **repro:** open the app, open/create a project in the Colorist tab, switch to the Edit tab
  in the same window (no alt-tab away and back).
- **expected:** Edit shows the real timeline for the project that was just opened.
- **actual:** Edit kept showing "No project open" until either the user hit its "Retry"
  button, or the OS window happened to lose and regain focus (alt-tab) — a real fix
  eventually arrived, just not from opening the project itself. Owner correctly diagnosed
  this live as a "state mounted at the Colorist level, not global" question before the fix
  was found.
- **cause:** `@chroma/editor`'s `EditorTab` only calls its timeline store's `load()` on its
  own mount and on the browser `window`'s `focus` event (see its `useEffect`s) — neither
  fires when a project opens from the Colorist tab, since all 3 tabs stay mounted under
  `@chroma/shell` (D-039) and switching tabs is not a window focus event.
- **fix:** `app/src/main.tsx` (the composition root — the one place that legitimately knows
  about both `useSessionStore`'s real "project is open" signal and `@chroma/editor`'s
  timeline store, without either package importing the other) now calls
  `useEditorTimelineStore.getState().load()` in a `useEffect` keyed on the same
  `projectOpen` boolean that already drives the shell's launcher-vs-tabs routing.
- **also fixed alongside (same root cause family):** `@chroma/shell`'s `activeTab` was
  persisted to `localStorage`, which meant the owner's explicit "Edit opens by default, not
  Colorist" request (2026-09-02, `D-039`/earlier `B-004` commit) only held on a machine that
  had never clicked another tab — every real session immediately overrode it back to
  whatever tab was last open, reading as if the default fix never landed. `activeTab` is now
  session-only (`packages/shell/src/store.ts`); every launch starts on `DEFAULT_TAB` ('edit').

## B-006 — Colorist main preview stays black even once the wgpu transform is positioned correctly
status: fixed (2026-09-02) · severity: high · area: `@chroma/shell` root background (`packages/shell/src/Shell.tsx`) — not gpu_processing.rs
- **repro:** open a project with a video shot in the Colorist tab; the shot-strip thumbnail
  renders fine, the main preview viewport never shows anything (solid dark/black).
- **expected:** the main preview shows the decoded, graded frame — the native wgpu surface,
  drawn directly onto the window behind a transparent hole in the webview at the preview
  panel's on-screen position (see `update_wgpu_transform`, `WgpuDisplay::render`).
- **actual (pre-fix):** `apply_adjustments` logs confirmed a real WGPU render fired at the
  *correct* output resolution and the *correct* on-screen position — the panel was still
  visually black regardless.
- **cause:** the render pipeline itself was never the problem. D-039's `@chroma/shell`
  wraps the whole window in a new root `<div>` (`Shell.tsx`) that carried a hardcoded,
  unconditional `bg-bg-primary` (opaque). The Colorist app's own root (`App.tsx`) already
  punches a transparent "hole" through itself (`isWgpuActive ? 'bg-transparent' :
  'bg-bg-primary'`) so the OS-transparent window (and the wgpu surface drawn directly onto
  it) shows through — but that hole only reveals whatever sits *behind* it in the DOM, which
  after D-039 is the shell's own new, permanently-opaque background, not the real
  transparent window. Before D-039 the Colorist app's root *was* the window's content root,
  so there was no opaque ancestor in the way and the trick worked. The shell's own opaque
  background silently defeated it once introduced — a plain CSS stacking regression, not an
  NSView/CALayer ordering issue and not a scissor/DPI bug.
- **how this was actually isolated (not by reasoning alone):** with no screen-recording
  permission available in the session that found this (`screencapture` failed with
  "could not create image from display" from every invoking path tried), two proxies stood
  in for a real screenshot: (1) a temporary debug hook in `WgpuDisplay::render` re-ran the
  *exact same* clear/scissor/bind-group/draw call into an off-screen `COPY_SRC` texture
  (the swapchain texture itself doesn't support `COPY_SRC`) and dumped it to PNG — this
  showed the real graded frame, correctly positioned, proving the render pass, scissor math,
  and bound texture were all already correct, and pointing the remaining search at
  compositing/visibility rather than rendering; (2) after the fix, a live
  `getComputedStyle` read of the shell root in the running app confirmed its
  `background-color` actually flips to `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` exactly when the Colorist wgpu
  surface is active. Both were run against the real app with the real
  `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma` project open. A final from-cold-boot re-check hit an
  unrelated environment wedge (WebKit's `markLayersVolatile`/process-suspension throttling
  the webview after this session's own repeated hard `kill -9` cycles on the dev app — see
  `sample`/`log show` trace from that session; confirmed unrelated to this fix since
  `gpu_processing.rs` and `lib.rs` carry zero diff from before the investigation started)
  and was not retried further; the two proxies above are the verification this fix rests on,
  not a literal on-screen screenshot.
- **fix:** `packages/shell/src/store.ts` — add a session-only (not persisted)
  `wgpuSurfaceActive` flag to `useShellStore`. `app/src/App.tsx` mirrors its own
  `isWgpuActive` into that flag via a `useEffect` (app → shell is the correct dependency
  direction here; shell still never imports the Colorist app). `Shell.tsx`'s root class now
  reads it: `wgpuSurfaceActive ? 'bg-transparent' : 'bg-bg-primary'`, restoring the same
  "no opaque ancestor between the window and the app's own hole" invariant D-039 broke. The
  tab bar and the project-launcher overlay both paint their own explicit backgrounds so they
  stay opaque regardless.
- **not this bug, ruled out during investigation:** `WgpuDisplay::render`'s scissor/clip
  math and `self.config` sizing — a live debug log during investigation showed
  `config` tracking the real window size correctly (the existing `on_window_event`
  `Resized` handler does keep it in sync) and the scissor bounds landing well inside it with
  `will_draw=true` every time; the off-screen dump then confirmed the draw itself was
  correct. Not a DPI/physical-vs-logical-pixel mismatch either — both sides already agree on
  physical pixels.

## B-005 — Colorist wgpu-position sync loop permanently stuck hidden after one transient 0×0 layout read
status: fixed (2026-09-02) · severity: high · area: app/src/components/panel/Editor.tsx (syncWgpu)
- **repro:** open a project / switch tabs so the preview container's `getBoundingClientRect()`
  briefly reads `0×0` during a layout transition (e.g. a tab switch) at the same moment
  `hasRenderedFirstFrame` flips true.
- **expected:** once the container actually lays out to a real size, the native wgpu frame
  is positioned there and becomes visible.
- **actual:** the frame stayed hidden (positioned at `x:-999999,y:-999999`) forever, even
  after the container had a real, valid size — required an unrelated dependency-array change
  (e.g. toggling a setting) to ever recover, or never recovered at all.
- **cause:** `syncWgpu`'s `requestAnimationFrame` self-scheduling loop only reschedules itself
  when the outgoing "hidden transform" string changes (a dedup guard against redundant
  `invoke()` calls). A transient `0×0` read produces one specific hidden-transform string;
  once sent, the loop stops rescheduling — nothing re-measures the container after that,
  since real DOM layout changes aren't part of the effect's dependency array (only React
  state is). Confirmed via a temporary debug log: `hasRenderedFirstFrame: true` but
  `rectW: 0, rectH: 0` at the exact moment the loop went silent.
- **fix:** split the single hidden-condition check into `notLaidOutYet` (a DOM-measurement
  state, not covered by any dependency array — must keep polling every frame until it
  resolves) vs. `notReadyToRender` (real React-state conditions, correctly covered by the
  existing dependency-array effect). `notLaidOutYet` now unconditionally calls
  `scheduleSync()` even when the outgoing transform was deduped, so measurement keeps
  polling until the container genuinely has a size — without spamming `invoke()`, which
  stays deduped as before.

## B-004 — entry module double-executes on every cold boot → corrupted Tauri IPC → "No project open" in Edit, blank Colorist preview
status: fixed (2026-09-02) · severity: blocker · area: app/index.html, frontend↔backend IPC
- **repro:** fresh `npm run tauri:dev` (verified across independent cold boots, port + processes killed first). Within ~2s of "Logger initialized successfully": `ReactDOMClient.createRoot() on a container that has already been passed to createRoot()`, then a burst of `[TAURI] Couldn't find callback id N` warnings and `Unhandled promise rejection TypeError: undefined is not an object (evaluating 'listeners[eventId].handlerId')` in `@tauri-apps/api/event.js`'s `_unlisten`, repeated several times.
- **expected:** Edit tab shows a real timeline once a project with shots is open in Colorist; Colorist's main preview viewport shows the current frame (not just the shot-strip thumbnail).
- **actual:** Edit tab stuck on "No project open" even with a project open and a shot visible in Colorist's shot strip; Colorist's own main preview viewport blank, though its shot-strip thumbnail rendered fine and the Rust backend logged successful `[apply_adjustments] … native WGPU display updated` renders. After the corruption, no further `invoke`/`listen` round-trip reached React for the rest of the session.
- **cause:** `app/index.html`'s entry `<script>` still pointed at `/src/main.jsx`, a file that hasn't existed since the D-039 TS rename (`main.tsx` is the real entry) — a dead reference `git log` shows predates `main.tsx`. Confirmed via a temporary `import.meta.url` probe at the top of `main.tsx`: on cold boot the module body runs **twice**, once for each of two distinct URLs — `http://localhost:1420/src/main.jsx` (the browser's literal fetch of the stale `<script src>`, which Vite's dev-server extension-probing fallback resolves to `main.tsx`'s real source and serves under the wrong module id) and `http://localhost:1420/src/main.tsx` (a second, genuinely separate fetch/execution of the same file under its correct canonical URL). Two independent top-level module executions call `createRoot()` on the same `#root` DOM node and independently wire up `useTauriListeners`' `listen()` calls (including `wgpu-frame-ready`, which the Colorist preview depends on) against the same shared `@tauri-apps/api/event.js` singleton, racing on `listen`/`unlisten` bookkeeping and corrupting the callback-id map — after which further `invoke`/`listen` resolutions silently stop reaching React. `chroma_timeline_get`'s real error text (`"no project open — open one in the Colorist tab"`) matches exactly what Edit tab's empty-state renders, confirming the open-project `invoke` was one of the calls that never resolved correctly.
- **fix:** `app/index.html` — `<script type="module" src="/src/main.jsx">` → `/src/main.tsx`. Re-verified with the same `import.meta.url` probe on a fresh cold boot: the module executes exactly once, no `createRoot` warning, no `unlisten` TypeError, for the whole session. Re-verified the two user symptoms directly (via `osascript`/System Events driving the real app window, since WKWebView exposes real accessibility): opened the existing `~/Movies/Chroma/New.chroma` project (a genuine saved `.chroma` project, not an Untitled/loose-clip session) — Edit tab's "No project open" state was replaced by a real Timeline pane, and Colorist's main preview `<img>` (726×516, positioned as the main viewport, distinct from the 49-thumbnail shot strip) rendered, with matching `[apply_adjustments] … WGPU display updated` log lines tied to the same action.
- **note:** unrelated to this bug — that one test project's `project.json` already had a *persisted-empty* `timeline` field (0 clips despite 1 shot) predating this session, most likely written during an earlier session while this same corruption was active. `chroma_timeline_get` returns a project's persisted timeline as-is once one exists (by design, D-041) rather than rebuilding, so that project's Edit timeline still shows 0 duration until its `timeline` field is cleared or a shot is re-added — a stale-data artifact of the bug's *effects*, not a remaining defect in the fix.

## B-002 — AI sidecar climbs to ~12 GB after repeated "Re-track"
status: fixed (2026-09-01) · severity: high · area: ai/server.py
- **repro:** load a video, track a subject, hit "Re-track subject across clip" several times (or scrub a lot during a track).
- **expected:** sidecar RSS bounded — one predictor + models ≈ 1–1.5 GB.
- **actual:** ~12 GB (mostly compressed under memory pressure; `ps` RSS showed ~330 MB resident, Activity Monitor "Memory" 12 GB). Machine hit 20 GB swap.
- **cause:** each `/track` built a *fresh* `SAM2DynamicInteractivePredictor` (~1 GB into the MPS pool, which never returns to the OS); track + on-seek `/refine_track` + ViTMatte warm-up ran concurrently, stacking their MPS working sets. The propagation loop itself is flat (verified — `memory_bank` stays at 1).
- **fix:** `_GPU` lock serialises every model call (per-frame in the track loop so refine interleaves); `_get_video_predictor` reuses one instance with per-track state reset; `_free_gpu()` (`empty_cache` + `gc`) after every `/segment`, `/refine_track`, and track pass; `/track` cancels any still-running track. Measured after: plateaus ~1.3 GB across repeated tracks, refines pull it back toward ~800 MB (577 MB after an hour of heavy testing).

## B-003 — tracking seek: render storm, frozen timeline, offset overlay
status: fixed (2026-09-01) · severity: high · area: engine + frontend
- **repro:** track a subject, click/scrub the timeline.
- **actual:** timeline playhead frozen; canvas re-rendering continuously (`apply_adjustments` ~30×/s); red overlay a person-shaped blob offset from the actual subject.
- **cause:** (1) `useAiMasking` (with the per-seek matte-swap effect) is mounted in 3 components → 3× work per seek, and it fired on every intermediate drag frame — each a `setAdjustments` + full re-render. (2) frame vs matte desync: `frameNonce` bumps faster than `setAdjustments` settles → frame-N matte on frame-M image. (3) `ChromaTimeline` root `onPointerDownCapture={e=>e.stopPropagation()}` ate the strip's own `onPointerDown` — clicks did nothing.
- **fix:** **D-019** — matte read from disk at render time (`generate_ai_subject_bitmap` → `tracked_full_mask`), no per-seek `setAdjustments`; a seek just bumps `frameNonce`. Root capture-phase `stopPropagation` moved onto the strip handler.

## B-011 — `cargo test chroma::` cross-test pollution: an export test's leftover "loaded video" fails a later, unrelated relight test
status: open · severity: low · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{export,relight,state}.rs` test suite (test isolation only — no runtime/product impact)
- **found:** 2026-09-03, while verifying D-050 (editor audio playback) with `CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO` set to run this repo's env-var-gated integration tests. Reproduces with no D-050 code involved at all — isolated to `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib -- --test-threads=1 chroma::export:: chroma::relight::` alone, confirmed unrelated to the audio work.
- **repro:** `CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO=<a real clip> cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib -- --test-threads=1 chroma::export:: chroma::relight::` (both modules must run in the same test binary process — the default `cargo test` invocation without `--test-threads=1` does not reliably reproduce it, since thread scheduling can luck into a different interleaving).
- **expected:** `chroma::relight::tests::keyframed_light_without_a_loaded_video_falls_back_to_raw_fields` asserts a keyframed light's raw (un-interpolated) `x` field is returned unchanged (`10.0`) when — per its own doc comment — "these pure-parsing tests run with no video loaded, so `interpolated_parameters` returns `None`."
- **actual:** fails with `left: 77.666664, right: 10.0` — the light's `x` came back *interpolated* against a real timestamp, meaning the test's "no video loaded" precondition was false.
- **cause:** `chroma::export`'s real-file-gated tests (`export_neutral_30_frames` etc., which only actually run when `CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO` is set — normally skipped) call `state::set_current_video(Some(CurrentVideo {..}))` and never reset it back to `None` afterward. `state::SESSION` is one process-global `Lazy<Mutex<Session>>` shared by the whole test binary (all `#[test]`s in `cargo test`'s single process), so whichever test runs next inherits that "a video is loaded" state — here, relight's `interpolated_parameters` hook (D-034) sees a loaded video + its current frame and genuinely interpolates against it, instead of hitting the `None` early-return its test assumes.
- **not a regression from D-050:** confirmed by reproducing with only `chroma::export::`/`chroma::relight::` selected, zero `chroma::audio::` tests in the run. D-050's own new tests (`chroma::audio::tests::*`) touch only `state::PROJECT` (via `state::set_project`, always paired with a `set_project(None)` cleanup) — never `state::SESSION`/`set_current_video` — so they neither cause nor are affected by this.
- **impact:** test-suite reliability only, order/CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO-dependent — not a product defect (`state::SESSION` behaving as "whatever was last loaded, loaded" is correct *runtime* behaviour for a single-session desktop app; the bug is the test's implicit unstated precondition plus the exporter test's missing teardown). Default `cargo test chroma::` (no `CHROMA_TEST_VIDEO`) does not hit this, since the export tests it would race against just skip.
- **fix (not done this pass — out of scope for D-050):** either have the export tests restore `state::set_current_video(None)` (and/or the prior value) in a guard/teardown, or have the relight test explicitly call `state::set_current_video(None)` itself before asserting instead of relying on ambient test-run state. Whoever picks up general test-isolation hardening for `app/src-tauri`'s growing set of shared-global-state tests should fix this alongside it — the same `SESSION`/`PROJECT`/`PIPE`/`THUMB_CACHE` pattern recurs across `state.rs`, `decode_pipe.rs`, `edit.rs`, `session.rs`, `project.rs`.

## B-063 — Clicking a layer to select it on the Motion canvas also toggled playback
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-172) · severity: medium (every selection click on the canvas also started/stopped playback — a real, constant friction on the exact gesture the whole visual-builder initiative was built around) · area: `packages/motion/src/MotionPreview.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-06, owner live, clicking an element on the Motion canvas to select/edit it.
- **repro:** open the Motion tab with a manifest loaded, click any layer on the canvas.
- **expected:** the layer selects (canvas outline, Inspector populates) and playback state is untouched.
- **actual:** the layer selects AND the player toggles play/pause, on every single click.
- **cause:** `MotionPreview.tsx` passes `controls` to `@remotion/player`'s `<Player>` but never set `clickToPlay`. `@remotion/player`'s own source (`Player.js`) defaults `clickToPlay` to `Boolean(controls)` when not explicitly given — so enabling the transport bar silently ALSO enabled "click anywhere on the canvas toggles play," which fights `MotionCanvasOverlay`'s own click-to-select (D-156): that overlay deliberately never calls `stopPropagation()` on a selection click, specifically so a click on EMPTY canvas still reaches Remotion's own controls normally — but that same non-interference also lets a click that DOES hit a layer bubble up to Remotion's click-to-play handler.
- **not a shared-component bug:** `@chroma/player` (the real, documented "one shared preview component all 3 tabs embed") is not used by Motion at all — Motion embeds `@remotion/player` directly. The Edit tab's own preview (`@chroma/player`-based `PreviewPane.tsx`) was never susceptible: its `onPlayPause` is wired only to a dedicated transport-bar button, never a generic canvas click, so it has no equivalent of Remotion's `clickToPlay`-defaults-to-`controls` behavior to trip over.
- **fix:** `MotionPreview.tsx`'s `<Player>` now passes `clickToPlay={false}` explicitly. The transport bar's own Play/Pause button and the spacebar shortcut are unaffected.
- **verification:** live, in `app/motion-harness.html` (D-165) — clicking a layer selects it (confirmed working, unchanged) and the transport bar's play button stays on its paused icon at `0:00`, where the same click previously started playback.

## B-064 — Selecting a layer on the Motion canvas reset the playhead to the current scene's start
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-173) · severity: medium (broke "scrub to a moment, then edit what's there" — the core workflow keyframing depends on) · area: `packages/motion/src/MotionTab.tsx` (`onSelect`)
- **found:** predicted and disclosed in D-158's and D-162's own decision entries as an inherited rough edge, months (hours) before it was confirmed annoying live by the owner: "MotionTab.tsx's own onSelect (D-081) always seeks the player to the clicked layer's scene START frame... for a canvas click the scene is ALREADY the one on screen, so this will visibly snap the playhead to frame 0... a one-line fast-follow... if this proves annoying in daily use."
- **repro:** scrub to a non-zero point in a scene, click a layer on the canvas to select it.
- **expected:** the layer selects; the playhead stays exactly where it was.
- **actual:** the playhead jumped back to the scene's own start frame, every time.
- **cause:** `onSelect` unconditionally called `playerRef.current?.seekTo(sceneStartFrame(...))` regardless of whether the target was already in the scene currently on screen.
- **fix:** new `manifestEdit.ts` export `sceneIndexAtFrame(manifest, frame)` (the inverse of `sceneStartFrame`/`sceneDurationFrames`, 5 new tests) lets `onSelect` compare the player's CURRENT frame's scene against the target's own `sceneIndex` and only seek when they actually differ.
- **verification:** live, in `app/motion-harness.html` — scrubbed to `0:01`, clicked a layer on the canvas: player stayed at `0:01` (previously reset to `0:00`). Clicked a `LayerList` row in a genuinely different scene (`space`, starts at `0:10`): player correctly jumped there — confirming the legitimate cross-scene jump still works.

## B-065 — A 2D layer added to a `scene3d` scene rendered nowhere, at any frame
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-174) · severity: high (silently invisible content — no error, no console warning, valid schema-passing manifest that renders nothing) · area: `packages/motion-engine/src/engine/Video.tsx` (`OneScene`), `packages/motion/src/manifestEdit.ts` (`addLayer`)
- **found:** 2026-09-06, owner live — added a `matrix` layer via the Catalog to the `space` scene (the sample manifest's only `scene3d` scene) and could not see it at all.
- **repro:** open a manifest with a `scene3d` scene, add any 2D-only primitive (`matrix`, `text`, `emphasis`, `layers`, `graph`) to that scene via the Catalog.
- **expected:** the new layer renders (composited over the 3D content, or at minimum visible somehow).
- **actual:** nothing renders, at any playhead frame — the layer exists in the manifest but has no render path to it at all.
- **cause:** `addLayer` routes a primitive by whether IT is 3D-capable (`catalogEntry(use).in3d`), never by whether the TARGET scene already has `scene3d` — a 2D primitive always lands in `scene.layers`. `Video.tsx`'s `OneScene` render branch was a hard `scene.scene3d ? <ThreeD/> : <TwoD/>` ternary — `scene.layers` was NEVER rendered for a scene that also has `scene3d`, regardless of contents or frame.
- **fix:** `OneScene` now renders `<TwoD>` as a 2D overlay ON TOP of `<ThreeD>` whenever `scene.layers` has entries — the general fix (2D-over-3D compositing is a real, useful thing to want), not a block on the insert. Confirmed safe for on-canvas interaction: `data-motion-world` is only ever emitted by `TwoD`'s own path, never by `ThreeD`/`Scene3D` (confirmed by grep), so stacking both never creates two competing world-space anchors.
- **verification:** byte-for-byte identical renders of the existing sample manifest before/after (its `space` scene has no `layers` at all, so the new branch never mounts for it — `git stash`/render/pop/render, identical SHA-256). A real smoke render adding a `matrix` layer to that same scene now shows the grid composited over the 3D particle stream, where before it rendered nothing.

## B-066 — Selection outline froze during a move/resize drag, ignoring the live-changing content
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-176) · severity: medium (cosmetic but actively misleading — the drawn outline lies about the current size/position while dragging) · area: `packages/motion/src/MotionCanvasOverlay.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-06, owner live — "i made the thingy small but the slector does not follow realtime".
- **repro:** select a layer on the Motion canvas, drag its resize handle (or drag-move it).
- **expected:** the drawn selection outline tracks the content in real time as it shrinks/grows/moves.
- **actual:** the outline stayed frozen at its pre-drag size/position for the whole gesture, even though the real content underneath visibly changed.
- **cause:** `recomputeBoxes` (measures each selection's real `[data-motion-box]` DOM and sets the drawn outline state) only re-ran on a `selections` change or the player's own `frameupdate`/`scalechange` events — never on `onTransientChange`, which is what a drag actually calls on every `pointermove`.
- **fix:** `onPointerMove`'s move/resize branches now call `requestAnimationFrame(recomputeBoxes)` right after `onTransientChange(...)` — one frame's grace for the re-render to land, then re-measure. `recomputeBoxes` added to the housing effect's dependency array.
- **verification:** live, in `app/motion-harness.html` — dragged the `emphasis` layer's resize handle from 520×130 down to ~108×15; the drawn outline shrank in sync, matching the Inspector's own W/H fields at every point checked.

## B-067 — The Inspector's `layers`/`layerstack` "Active index" field silently destroyed a real `active` step-schedule
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-178) · severity: high (data loss with no warning — a single click into the wrong field and a save wipes out real keyframe data) · area: `packages/motion/src/propCatalog.ts`
- **found:** 2026-09-06, owner live, on a real project manifest — "any way to expose these layer as well as you can see some things are off is there anyway i can fix it?"
- **repro:** select a `layers`/`layerstack` layer whose `active` field is a real `[{at,i}]` step-schedule (not a plain number); scroll the Inspector.
- **expected:** exactly one editor for the `active` key.
- **actual:** TWO — `timingFields`'s correct generic `active: {kind:'json'}` (labelled "Active (index or schedule)", under TIMING) AND a per-primitive `active: {kind:'number'}` override (labelled "Active index", under SOURCE) — both bound to the same manifest key. Typing into the number field overwrites the WHOLE array with a plain number, silently destroying the schedule.
- **cause:** `PRIMITIVE_FIELDS.layers`/`PRIMITIVE_FIELDS.layerstack` each declared their own `active` entry, unaware `timingFields` (concatenated onto every primitive's own list by `fieldsForPrimitive`) already covers the same key correctly for both shapes `Active` (`schema.ts`) allows.
- **fix:** deleted the destructive per-primitive override from both `PRIMITIVE_FIELDS.layers` and `PRIMITIVE_FIELDS.layerstack` — the generic `timingFields` json field is now the only editor.
- **verification:** live, in `app/motion-harness.html` — selecting the `layers` layer now shows exactly one "Active (index or schedule)" field, correctly populated with `[{"at":2.5,"i":2},{"at":4,"i":0}]`; no SOURCE section, no second number field.

## B-069 — An uncaught React crash in one Edit-tab panel wedges the ENTIRE `editor_*` control-server bridge, for every op, not just that panel
status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: blocker (during this session — made the Edit tab's MCP surface, D-183, completely unusable until the app was restarted) · area: `packages/editor/src/TimelinePane.tsx`, `app/src/hooks/useEditorControl.ts`, control server (`chroma::control`, port 19788)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, first real end-to-end use of the D-183 Edit-tab MCP tools (building the performance-comparison reel) — the first live test this surface had ever had.
- **repro:** open the Edit tab with two freshly-imported clips whose media probe had just failed (see B-070), let `<TimelinePane>` render them; the app's own console showed `[Unhandled error] Error: Rendered fewer hooks than expected. This may be caused by an accidental early return statement.` inside `<TimelinePane>`, with React's own warning that no error boundary caught it. Immediately after, every `editor_*` MCP call — including a plain read like `editor_get_state`/`get_timeline` that had worked moments earlier in the same session — started failing.
- **expected:** either the crash is caught by an error boundary and the rest of the app (including the control server) keeps working, or at minimum unrelated read-only ops like `editor_get_state` keep responding.
- **actual:** `editor_get_state`/`get_timeline`/`editor_add_track` all returned a generic "Error executing tool" via the MCP layer; confirmed it's not just the Python bridge — a direct `curl -XPOST http://127.0.0.1:19788/op -d '{"op":"editor_get_state","args":{}}'` hung and timed out after 10s with zero bytes back, i.e. the Rust control server itself stopped responding, for every op, not just the ones touching the crashed panel.
- **cause, confirmed:** a genuine Rules-of-Hooks violation, pinpointed via a second crash's exact stack trace (`Rendered more hooks than during the previous render`, pointing at the `linkCheck` `useMemo` in `TimelinePane.tsx`). The component had TWO conditional early `return`s (`if (!timeline) return null;` and `if (tracks.length === 0) { return <empty-state/> }`) sitting BETWEEN hook calls — `linkCheck`'s `useMemo` and three DnD `useCallback`s were declared AFTER both. Whenever `timeline` flipped null↔non-null, or `tracks.length` crossed 0 (exactly what happens adding the first/second track, or a probe-failed clip putting the store into a transient state), the SET of hooks actually called differed between renders of the same component instance — React only detects this once the counts/order actually diverge, which is why it surfaced as two different-looking errors ("fewer hooks" one time, "more hooks" the next) rather than immediately.
- **fix:** moved both early-return conditions to AFTER every hook call, immediately before the final JSX `return` — all hooks now run unconditionally on every render; only which JSX gets returned is still conditional (the only thing that's ever allowed to branch). No behavior change for either the null-timeline or empty-tracks case — same JSX, just relocated.
- **still open, not this fix's scope:** no error boundary exists around `<TimelinePane>` (or any Edit-tab panel) — a *different* future crash there would still take down the whole app/control-server bridge with no isolation. Also, whatever in the control-server bridge blocks indefinitely on a crashed render tree should time out or fail fast instead of hanging (confirmed live: a direct `curl` to the control server hung 10s with zero bytes back during the crash) — neither addressed here, both worth a real look if this class of bug recurs.
- **verification:** live, same session — reproduced the exact crash (`editor_add_track` on a fresh 1-track timeline), applied the fix, Vite HMR reloaded `TimelinePane.tsx`, retried the identical `editor_add_track` call: succeeded, `get_timeline` read back correctly, no crash in the dev console. `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 303/303 (1 unrelated pre-existing `EPERM` vitest-cache flake under concurrent disk load, not a regression).

## B-070 — Not a bug: macOS names screen recordings with a narrow no-break space (U+202F) before AM/PM, invisible when a human (or an LLM) retypes the path
status: wontfix (root-caused, not a defect anywhere in our code) · severity: n/a · area: none — environmental/user-input, not Chroma
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, importing two screen recordings from `~/Desktop` into a fresh Edit-tab project. First looked exactly like a real bug: `editor_import_media`/the app's own `ffprobe` call, AND a plain shell `ls` on the identical absolute path, both reported "No such file or directory" for a file that `ls -la ~/Desktop/ | grep <name>` / a glob (`~/Desktop/*2026-09-07*.mov`) listed just fine, moments apart, repeatably. Initially misattributed (see history below) to concurrent system load / iCloud Desktop-sync eviction.
- **actual cause, confirmed:** `python3 -c "import os; print([hex(ord(c)) for c in name])"` on the real directory-listing entries showed the character between the minutes and "PM" is ` ` (NARROW NO-BREAK SPACE) — macOS's own Screenshot/screen-recording naming convention — not a regular ASCII space (`0x20`). Every literal path typed by a human or an LLM into a shell command or an MCP tool call (`"...1.08.16 PM.mov"`, ordinary space) therefore never byte-matches the real file — glob expansion (`~/Desktop/*.mov`) works because the shell resolves the real bytes itself; a hand-typed literal never can. This is true for EVERY process attempting direct-path access, including the Chroma app's own `ffprobe` call — nothing app-side was ever wrong.
- **why the load/iCloud theory looked plausible at first:** the failures happened to coincide with genuinely high concurrent system load (two background agents) and retrying "worked" a couple of times purely by chance (glob-based retries, not identical literal retries) — a coincidence, not a fix.
- **resolution used this session:** stopped retyping the literal path anywhere. Copied both source files (via a shell glob, which resolves the real bytes) to plain ASCII-safe filenames under `chroma/scratch/reel-src/` and used those for every subsequent import — the durable fix for this specific reel, not a per-call workaround.
- **takeaway for next time:** a macOS screen-recording filename with "AM"/"PM" in it is NOT safely retypeable as a literal string by a human or an LLM — always resolve it via shell glob or a directory listing, never by hand-transcribing the visible filename, in ANY tool (Chroma's own included).

## B-071 — First `editor_*` MCP call after the Tauri app restarts fails once, succeeds on immediate retry
status: open · severity: low (self-heals on retry, only affects the single request right after an app restart) · area: `mcp/server.py`'s HTTP client to `chroma::control` (port 19788)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, right after restarting the Tauri dev app to recover from B-069 (same session).
- **repro:** with `mcp/server.py` already running and having made at least one successful `editor_*`/control-server call, kill and restart the Tauri app (new process, same port). Call any `editor_*` MCP tool immediately after the new app is up and its port is confirmed listening.
- **expected:** the call succeeds — a direct `curl` to the same port at the same moment worked fine.
- **actual:** the MCP tool call returned a generic "Error executing tool"; the identical call retried immediately after succeeded normally.
- **cause:** not confirmed — likely `mcp/server.py`'s HTTP client holds a pooled/keep-alive connection to the OLD process's now-closed socket and the first request against it fails before the client reconnects, rather than transparently retrying on a dead connection.
- **fix:** not attempted — if `mcp/server.py`'s HTTP client library doesn't already retry once on a connection error, adding that would make an app restart transparent to callers instead of surfacing one throwaway failure.

## B-072 — mlx-audiocraft SFX generation shows a large transient memory spike during model load
status: open (deferred, owner) · severity: low (self-resolved within the same session — RSS was back to ~1.2GB seconds after the spike; not confirmed to recur or grow unbounded) · area: `~/my_projects/videoAgent/src/audio/audiocraft_generate.py` (cross-repo, not Chroma code — logged here per this project's own "note every issue" rule since it directly affected work happening in this repo's `scratch/`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, owner watching Activity Monitor while an `audiocraft_generate.py --model audiogen-medium` subprocess generated `zoom-whoosh.wav` into `chroma/scratch/reel-sfx/` for the performance-comparison reel — Activity Monitor showed that Python process at 17.58GB.
- **repro:** run `audiocraft_generate.py --model audiogen-medium` and watch Activity Monitor's Memory column during model load.
- **expected:** unclear what "expected" peak memory is for `audiogen-medium` — not previously measured/documented anywhere in either repo.
- **actual:** a 17.58GB spike in Activity Monitor; re-checked via `ps` ~90s later on the same still-running PID and RSS was back down to ~1.2GB, stable, not climbing further while it finished generating.
- **cause:** not investigated — owner's own read, deferred rather than chased down now: "it's definitely audiocraft, we'll solve it later." Plausibly a one-time model-load allocation that Activity Monitor's Memory column (which can reflect Apple Silicon unified-memory/GPU-shared allocations `ps` RSS doesn't) shows differently than steady-state resident memory.
- **fix:** not attempted — deferred by owner. If this recurs or the machine feels pressured during future SFX generation, worth checking mlx-audiocraft's own memory-release behavior around model load specifically (not generation), and whether repeated calls in one process compound it.

## B-073 — A media-pool item whose initial probe failed (offline/transient) is stuck that way forever — never re-probed, and invisible to `editor_add_clip` after reopening the project
status: open · severity: medium (blocks placing a clip whose first import happened to race a transient file-access failure, with no MCP-visible way to recover short of editing `project.json` by hand or recreating the project) · area: `packages/bridge/src/media.ts` (`importPaths`), `packages/editor/src/useEditorControl.ts` (`editor_add_clip`), Rust `chroma_media_import`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, building the performance-comparison reel — `editor_add_clip` kept returning `"no pool item matching mediaId/sourcePath — call editor_import_media first"` for both source `.mov`s, even immediately after calling `editor_import_media` on their exact paths (which itself reported `"added": []`, i.e. success with nothing new).
- **repro:** `new_project(media_paths=[...])` at a moment a path's ffprobe transiently fails (see B-070 — happened here for real, not contrived); the project's on-disk `project.json` ends up with a `media` entry that has `id`/`sourcePath`/`name` but no `video` block (no width/height/fps/frameCount — confirmed by reading `project.json` directly). Restart the app, `open_project` the same project, call `editor_import_media` again on the identical path.
- **expected:** either the already-pooled-but-never-successfully-probed item gets re-probed and gains real `video` metadata, or `editor_add_clip` can find and use it in some other way.
- **actual:** `editor_import_media`/`chroma_media_import` treats "already in the pool" as fully handled and returns it in neither `added` (frontend only appends what comes back in `added`) nor does anything call `useMediaPoolStore.refresh()` on `open_project`, so the frontend's in-memory `items` array never contains this project's already-pooled media at all after a fresh app reopen. `editor_add_clip`'s lookup is purely against that in-memory array, so it reports "no pool item matching" — a misleading error, since the item DOES exist, just not in a place this tool checks, and even if it were found it has no `frameCount` to place a clip with anyway.
- **cause:** two compounding gaps: (1) nothing refreshes `useMediaPoolStore` from the backend when a project is opened via `open_project`/`chroma_project_open`; (2) `chroma_media_import`'s "skip already-pooled paths, don't duplicate" behavior doesn't distinguish a successfully-probed pool entry from one that never got real video metadata, so a one-time transient probe failure permanently sticks.
- **fix:** not attempted this session (worked around by recreating the project instead — see `docs/CHANGELOG.md`). Two independent real fixes worth doing: `open_project` should refresh the media pool store (fixes visibility); `chroma_media_import` should re-probe (or at least offer a way to re-probe) a pool entry with no `video` metadata instead of silently treating it as done forever. This is also a good argument for the MCP media-pool gap noted in `docs/notes/mcp-tool-coverage.md` (`chroma_media_list`/`_remove` have no tool) — there was no way to inspect or clear the stuck entry via MCP at all; only reading `project.json` directly revealed what was wrong.
- **same-session addendum:** the SAME staleness pattern hit the TIMELINE too, not just the media pool — deleting a project's `.chroma` directory on disk and calling `new_project` with the identical name in the same running app session still returned the OLD in-memory `useEditorTimelineStore` timeline (same `id`, same leftover tracks from before the delete) rather than a fresh empty one; `editor_add_track` then produced a 3rd track on top of 2 stale ones. Worked around by hiding the extra track (`editor_set_track_hidden`) rather than fighting it further. Same root shape as the media-pool gap above: nothing forces the frontend's in-memory Edit-tab state to actually resync from whatever `new_project`/`open_project` just did on the backend.

## B-074 — `editor_export`'s `scale` forces every overlay to the OUTPUT CANVAS's own aspect ratio — a full-width/half-height stacked layout is mathematically impossible for any canvas size
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-184) · severity: high (silently distorts or truncates any composited clip whose real aspect ratio differs from the output canvas's own — the exact "stacking/PIP primitive" `editor_set_clip_transform`'s own docstring claims to support) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildClipFilterChain`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, building the performance-comparison reel — tried to stack two 2940×1670 screen recordings full-width, one per half of a 1080×1920 canvas.
- **repro:** composite any clip whose native aspect ratio differs from the output canvas's own aspect ratio, at any `scale`.
- **expected:** a clip sized to fill the canvas's full width (or some target region) at its own real aspect ratio, undistorted.
- **actual:** `buildClipFilterChain` emitted `scale=${width*scale}:${height*scale}` — BOTH dimensions forced to fractions of the CANVAS's own width/height. Since a single `scale` value multiplies both canvas dimensions by the same factor, every overlay this function could ever produce had exactly the canvas's own aspect ratio, just resized — never a differently-shaped box. Proved this holds for ANY choice of canvas dimensions or `scale` value, not just the specific numbers tried live.
- **cause:** the function had no way to know a clip's real source resolution (documented in its own prior comment as a known gap: "this pure, no-I/O module has no way to probe" it) and used the OUTPUT canvas's dimensions as a stand-in for the source's, conflating "fraction of the canvas" (correct, and still true for width) with "fraction of the canvas, in BOTH axes independently" (never correct unless canvas aspect happens to equal source aspect).
- **fix:** `scale=${width}*${scale}:-2` — ffmpeg's own `-2` auto-computes height from the actual (post-crop) input frame, preserving its real aspect ratio, no source-resolution threading needed. The old forced-stretch behavior is kept available as an explicit per-clip opt-in (`fitOverrides: {clipId: 'stretch'}`), not deleted — see D-184 for the full design and why default-`'fit'`-with-opt-in-`'stretch'` was chosen over always-one-or-the-other.
- **verification:** `timelineExport.test.ts` 21/21 (2 new tests pin the exact behavior: default `'fit'` never emits the old `height*scale` formula regardless of canvas shape; `fitOverrides: 'stretch'` reproduces the old formula exactly for a caller who explicitly wants it). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean.

## B-075 — `editor_export` silently misreads a clip's `duration`/`source_start`/keyframe `frame` at the WRONG frame rate, and the generated ffmpeg filtergraph was syntactically invalid for any keyframed clip
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-187) · severity: blocker (every real export of a keyframed clip failed outright with an ffmpeg parse error; a mixed-native-fps timeline that DID export would have silently rendered the wrong durations) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildExportFfmpegArgs`, `buildClipFilterChain`), `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts` (`Clip`, `DraggedMedia`), `packages/editor/src/useEditorControl.ts` (`editor_add_clip`), `app/src/components/chroma/SourcesPanel.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, the first real `editor_export` call of the whole performance-comparison reel session (stacking two screen recordings at two different native frame rates, 47.6fps and 44.1fps, onto one canvas).
- **repro (part 1, the parse error):** export any clip with more than a trivial one-keyframe animation on `position_x`/`position_y`. ffmpeg rejected the filtergraph outright: `[AVFilterGraph] No option name near 'if(lt(t'` / `Error parsing a filter description`.
- **cause (part 1):** `overlay=x=${xExpr}*W:y=${yExpr}*H:enable=...` — `enable=` was correctly wrapped in single quotes, but `x=`/`y=` were not. ffmpeg's filtergraph syntax splits on bare `,`/`:` OUTSIDE quotes; a keyframed `if(between(t,a,b),...)` expression is full of exactly those characters. Only ever exercised by a real ffmpeg run — every existing unit test only string-compared the generated argv, never invoked ffmpeg, so this was invisible until now.
- **repro (part 2, the fps mix-up):** `crates/chroma-timeline`'s own doc comment on `Clip` is explicit: "`source_start`/`duration` are in **source frames**; `start_frame` is in **timeline** frames." But `buildExportFfmpegArgs` divided `source_start`/`duration` (and a keyframe's `frame`, via `positionExpr`) by `opts.fps` — the single EXPORT-wide rate — uniformly, for every clip. Confirmed via a real export: with `opts.fps=30`, a clip whose `duration` was its native probed frame count at 47.6fps computed `-t = duration/30`, a completely different (and wrong) number of seconds than `duration/47.6` (the real source duration).
- **cause (part 2):** `editor_add_clip` (and the GUI's own `linkedClipsFromDraggedMedia`) never threaded the probed source's own fps onto the `Clip` they build — `duration` was set to the native frame count with nothing recording which rate it was AT, so the exporter had no way to know it wasn't already in `opts.fps` terms.
- **fix:** (1) `x=`/`y=` wrapped in single quotes too. (2) new `Clip.source_fps` (and `DraggedMedia.fps`), populated at both real clip-creation sites (`editor_add_clip`, `SourcesPanel.tsx`'s drag payload) from the media pool's own probed `video.fps`; `timelineExport.ts` uses `clip.source_fps ?? opts.fps` everywhere a source frame count needs converting to seconds, keeping `start_frame`'s own `opts.fps` conversion unchanged (a genuinely different unit, per the Rust doc above).
- **verification:** `timelineExport.test.ts` 27/27 including 4 new tests pinning the exact mixed-fps math and the quoting fix. A NEW real-ffmpeg integration suite, `timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts` (B-075's own fix prompted this — string-only tests are exactly how this shipped invisibly) — generates real 24fps/25fps synthetic clips, actually runs ffmpeg, and asserts real output durations via `ffprobe`: 3/3 passing, all real renders succeeding where they previously errored out immediately.

## B-076 — Every `editor_export` render ran forever (bounded only by manually killing the process) — the `color=[base]` backdrop has no duration of its own and nothing capped the output
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-187) · severity: blocker (no export could ever complete on its own) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildExportFfmpegArgs`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, immediately after fixing B-075's parse error let a real export actually start rendering for the first time — a real export of a single ~4s test clip was still running, actively consuming ~470% CPU, 10+ minutes later (output file already 8.6MB and climbing, for content that should have produced tens of KB) before being killed by hand.
- **repro:** call `editor_export` on any timeline and watch the ffmpeg process — it never exits on its own.
- **cause:** `color=black:size=WxH:rate=FPS[base]` (the black backdrop every clip's overlay composites onto) is an ffmpeg `lavfi` synthetic source with no `duration=`/`d=` of its own — unlike a real decoded file input, it never reaches EOF. Every real clip's own `-t` bounds ITS input, but nothing bounded the OUTPUT overall, so the exported file's length was governed by the one layer with no natural end: never. This was true of literally every export this module could ever produce, from the moment it was written — B-075's parse error had simply made every real attempt fail before rendering ever started, masking it completely until that bug was fixed.
- **fix:** `-t <furthest clip's real end, in output seconds>` added to the OUTPUT (after `-map`), computed as `max(chain.endSec)` across every composited clip — `0` for a timeline with no clips at all, never an unbounded run.
- **verification:** `timelineExport.test.ts` — 2 new tests (multi-track max-end-wins, empty-timeline-gets-zero). The real-ffmpeg suite (`timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts`) — all 3 tests complete in under a second each, where the equivalent real command previously ran unbounded.

## B-077 — `endFrame`/the GUI timeline display add a clip's SOURCE-frame `duration` directly to its TIMELINE-frame `start_frame`, with no fps conversion — wrong gap detection, wrong displayed duration, for any clip whose native fps differs from the project's
status: open · severity: high (a real, silent correctness bug across gap detection/trim/ripple/display for exactly the content this session's reel uses — mixed native-fps clips — though it does NOT affect `editor_export`'s actual rendered output, which already converts correctly per B-075) · area: `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts` (`endFrame`, and by extension `trackDuration`/`timelineDuration`/gap detection/ripple/trim), `packages/editor/src/TimelinePane.tsx` (every `clip.duration / fps` / `clip.source_start / fps` pixel-width and gap calculation)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, owner looking at the real Edit-tab timeline UI for the two placed clips (AFTER: 39.38s real, 1875 native frames @ 47.6fps; BEFORE: 47.86s real, 2113 native frames @ 44.1fps) — the transport bar showed a total duration of **00:01:28:00** (88s) and both clip bars visibly extended far past their real content (a thumbnailed region for the real footage, then a long plain blue "empty" tail).
- **repro:** place a clip whose native fps differs from the project's own (`timelineFps`, `DEFAULT_FPS=24` when unset, as on any fresh project) and look at its displayed length, or the total timeline duration.
- **expected:** BEFORE (real 47.86s) should show as such; the total timeline should read ~0:48, not 1:28.
- **actual:** displayed duration used `clip.duration / timelineFps` = `2113 / 24` = **88.04s (1:28)** — BEFORE's raw native FRAME COUNT divided by the project's default 24fps has nothing to do with its real 44.1fps duration. The exact match between 2113/24≈88 and the transport bar's literal "1:28:00" confirms this precisely, not approximately.
- **cause:** `crates/chroma-timeline`'s own `Clip` doc is explicit: "`source_start`/`duration` are in **source frames**; `start_frame` is in **timeline** frames" — genuinely two different frame-rate spaces, by design (so a trim boundary check can correctly compare against the source's own `source_len`). But `endFrame(c) { return c.start_frame + c.duration; }` (`timeline.ts`) adds them directly with no conversion, and `TimelinePane.tsx` divides `clip.duration`/`clip.source_start` by the single project-wide `fps` (`timelineFps(timeline)`) throughout — both are only correct when a clip's native fps happens to equal the project's own, which is true for almost all normal same-fps footage and therefore was never noticed until today's genuinely mixed-fps content (two screen recordings at two different real frame rates) exercised it for the first time. This is the SAME root shape as B-075 (which already fixed the identical conflation, but only inside `timelineExport.ts` specifically) — `endFrame`/gap-detection/ripple/trim/display are a second, separate, much more pervasive set of call sites with the same bug, now that `Clip.source_fps` (B-075) exists to actually fix them with.
- **NOT a thumbnail bug** (first guessed live, then corrected by the owner) — the thumbnail/filmstrip rendering was working correctly the whole time, generating real thumbnails only for the clip's REAL footage duration; it was the clip BAR's drawn length that was wrong (too long), making the correctly-short thumbnailed region look like a bug in the thumbnails instead of in the bar's own length.
- **does NOT affect export correctness:** `editor_export`'s own `buildExportFfmpegArgs` computes `startSec`/`endSec` independently (never calls `endFrame`) and already uses `clip.source_fps ?? opts.fps` correctly per B-075 — the rendered video's actual timing is right; only the GUI's live display/gap-math for these clips is wrong.
- **fix:** not attempted here — genuinely large and cross-cutting (every `duration`/`source_start` consumer across gap detection, trim/ripple, snap points, and display needs the SAME `clip.source_fps ?? timelineFps`-aware conversion `timelineExport.ts` already got in isolation for the export path only), scoped out as real follow-up work rather than rushed inline. `Clip.source_fps` (B-075) already exists and is already populated by both real clip-creation sites (`editor_add_clip`, the GUI drag-drop path) — this fix has what it needs, it just hasn't been done yet.

## B-078 — `Clip.source_fps` (B-075) does not survive `save_project`/`open_project` — silently dropped, regressing B-075's own fix the moment a project is saved and reopened
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-194, fixed incidentally by the B-077 audit) · severity: high (silently reintroduces B-075's wrong-duration bug on ANY project that gets saved and reloaded — which is every real usage session, not an edge case) · area: `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts` (`Clip.source_fps`), `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs` (`Clip`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, immediately after B-075 — restarted the app (to pick up the B-075/B-076/D-188 code fixes cleanly), called `save_project` then, post-restart, `open_project` on the exact same project, then re-ran `editor_export` with the identical parameters that should have produced a correct ~48s render. Got `duration=70.433333` back — exactly `2113/30` (BEFORE's raw native frame count divided by the DEFAULT export fps), i.e. B-075's bug reproduced exactly, despite B-075's own fix being confirmed live in the served JS bundle (`curl`'d the actual running Vite dev server's module output and found `clipFps`/`source_fps` present and correct).
- **repro:** place a clip via `editor_add_clip` (which correctly sets `source_fps` in memory, per B-075), `save_project`, restart the app (or just reopen the project fresh), `open_project`, `editor_export` — the reopened clip's `source_fps` is gone.
- **expected:** a field on the live `Clip` object survives a save/reload round trip like every other clip field (`scale`, `position_y`, `chroma_keyframes`, all confirmed intact after the same reload).
- **actual:** confirmed directly by reading the saved `project.json` off disk after `save_project`: every other field was present and correct (`duration`, `chroma_keyframes`, `positionY`, etc.) but `source_fps` was entirely absent from both clips' JSON.
- **cause:** not fully root-caused (didn't have Rust-side save/load source open at the time this was found, mid-session) — the leading theory, given every OTHER field round-tripped fine: whatever Rust-side command backs `chroma_project_save`/`_open` deserializes the timeline into a strongly-typed `chroma_timeline::Clip` Rust struct that has no `source_fps` field (since B-075 only added it to the TS-side type, not its Rust mirror), silently dropping any key Rust's struct doesn't recognize on the save round trip (or the open round trip re-hydrating a struct with no such field to populate).
- **workaround used this session:** avoided a save/reload between adding clips and exporting for the reel's own final render — `source_fps` only needs to survive from `editor_add_clip` to `editor_export` within ONE live session, which doesn't require going through `project.json` at all.
- **fix:** confirmed live — Tauri deserializes `chroma_timeline_set`'s JSON straight into the strongly-typed `chroma_timeline::Clip` Rust struct, and serde silently drops unknown keys; the struct had no `source_fps` field. The B-077 audit (D-194) added `Clip.source_fps: Option<f64>` to the Rust struct (additive, `impl Default` updated), closing this incidentally while fixing the broader mixed-fps display bug. Pinned with a new `source_fps_round_trips_through_serde` Rust test.

## B-081 — Tauri's fast custom-protocol IPC transport intermittently fails at startup, silently falling back to the slower `postMessage` bridge
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-201) — but **not the bug it looked like**: the warning is a harmless side effect of a full page reload, and the reload itself was the real (and much more expensive) defect · severity: as filed, medium; as root-caused, the warning itself is **cosmetic** (the flag is per-JS-context and dies with the outgoing document) while its *cause* — every HMR update reaching `app/src/main.tsx` full-reloading the whole app — was a high-cost dev-iteration defect · area: `app/src/main.tsx` / `app/src/Root.tsx` (React Fast Refresh boundary), NOT Tauri's IPC transport
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, this exact session — observed in the dev server's own console output across three separate `npm run tauri:dev` launches this session.
- **repro:** not yet reliable — happened on one of three launches this session (`tauri-dev-2.log`: 88 occurrences of the warning, all at the same timestamp, `4:19:00 pm`, right at startup; `tauri-dev.log` and `tauri-dev-3.log`, the other two launches: zero occurrences). Intermittent, not yet tied to a specific trigger.
- **actual:** `[console.warn] IPC custom protocol failed, Tauri will now use the postMessage interface instead` — logged once per pending/queued IPC call at that moment (88 times = 88 calls that were in-flight or about to be made when the fallback kicked in, not 88 separate failures of the same call), each with a `json@[native code]` stack frame (opaque, gives no direct cause).
- **expected:** the fast custom-protocol transport (Tauri v2's default, lower overhead than `postMessage`) should be used consistently across launches, or if it's expected to occasionally fail, that should be a known, documented condition (e.g. a specific macOS/WKWebView state), not an unexplained one-in-three occurrence.
- **cause, confirmed (2026-09-07, D-201):** **a Vite full page reload while IPC calls were in flight** — nothing to do with the transport, entitlements, port reuse or a webview-readiness race. The correlation across the same three saved logs the entry was filed from is exact: `tauri-dev.log` 0 Tauri-webview reloads / 0 warnings; `tauri-dev-2.log` 3 reloads / 88 warnings, in three bursts of 51 + 17 + 20 each immediately following its own `page reload src/main.tsx` line; `tauri-dev-3.log` 0 Tauri-webview reloads / 0 warnings (its 12 `page reload`s are all of `harness.html`, a plain Chrome page with no Tauri IPC at all). `tauri-2.11.5/scripts/ipc-protocol.js` logs this warning from the **rejection** handler of the `fetch()` to `ipc://localhost/<cmd>`, and WKWebView cancels every pending custom-scheme task when the page navigates — so it fires once per call that happened to be in flight at the instant of the reload (hence the varying counts, not a constant boot sequence), and `tauri-dev-2.log`'s very next second logs Tauri's own `[TAURI] Couldn't find callback id … This might happen when the app is reloaded while Rust is running an asynchronous operation` ×4, which is the same event described from the Rust side. `customProtocolIpcFailed` is a per-JS-context flag, so the document that flips to `postMessage` is the one already being torn down; the freshly-loaded document starts clean on the fast transport. **The reload, not the warning, was the real cost** — every module re-fetched, all app state lost, the open project re-opened.
- **why the reload happened:** `app/src/main.tsx` declared the `Root` component (so `@vitejs/plugin-react` made it a Fast Refresh boundary candidate and injected `import.meta.hot.accept`) while exporting **nothing** — it is the entry module. The refresh runtime's `validateRefreshBoundaryAndEnqueueUpdate` requires at least one export and requires every export to be a component; zero exports fails with `hasExports === false`, which is what prints the confusing `Could not Fast Refresh ("true" export is incompatible)` message seen in the log. Vite answers an invalidated boundary with a full page reload. And updates reached `main.tsx` constantly, because no `@chroma/*` barrel is a refresh boundary either (they re-export stores/functions/types alongside components), so an edit to `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts`, `timelineStore.ts` or `index.ts` — three of the most-edited files in the Edit tab — propagated straight through the barrel to `main.tsx` and reloaded the whole app.
- **fix (D-201):** `Root` moved to its own module, `app/src/Root.tsx`, which exports only that component and is therefore a valid boundary that stops the propagation; `app/src/main.tsx` keeps only bootstrap (`installFrontendLogBridge()`, `createRoot`, `render`) and now contains no component at all, so the plugin never makes it a boundary candidate. Both files carry a header comment saying why they must stay that way. This is React's own "only export components from a component file" rule / Vite's `#consistent-components-exports` guidance, not a workaround.
- **verification:** live, before and after, against a real Vite 8 dev server and a real Chromium page (a second dev server on port 1440 inside the agent's worktree, so the owner's running `npm run tauri:dev` was never touched). **Before:** appending a comment to `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts` produced `hmr update … /src/main.tsx …` → `hmr invalidate /src/main.tsx Could not Fast Refresh ("true" export is incompatible)` → `page reload src/main.tsx`, plus the identical `You are calling ReactDOMClient.createRoot() on a container that has already been passed to createRoot() before` console error the original session log shows. **After:** that same edit, and the same edit to `timelineStore.ts` and to `packages/editor/src/index.ts`, each produced `hmr update … /src/Root.tsx …` with **no `hmr invalidate` and no `page reload`**.
- **what is NOT live-verified:** the final link — "no reload ⇒ no `IPC custom protocol failed` burst" — rests on the three-log correlation plus reading Tauri's own `ipc-protocol.js`, not on a fresh `npm run tauri:dev`. The owner's dev server and single-instance Tauri app were running on the main checkout throughout; `tauri.conf.json` pins `devUrl` to `http://localhost:1420` and `tauri-plugin-single-instance` refuses a second app process, so a second real Tauri launch was impossible without killing the owner's. To close it next session: run `npm run tauri:dev`, edit `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts`, confirm the console shows an `hmr update` (not a `page reload`) and logs zero IPC-fallback warnings.
- **left alone deliberately:** `app/src/harness-main.tsx` and `app/src/motion-harness-main.tsx` still full-reload. They own module-level fixture state a hot update would leave half-stale, so a reload is the correct behavior for a test page, and they import the `@chroma/*` barrels directly for non-component values, so making them boundaries would mean restructuring them for no real gain.

## B-082 — `chroma_project_new`'s auto-placed clips never got `source_fps` — the B-075/B-077/D-194/B-079 fix line's fourth blind spot, this time at the CREATION site rather than a consumption site

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: high (any project created with seed media — `new_project`'s own documented, primary use — has every auto-placed clip silently mis-timed on the timeline the moment its native fps differs from the project default, with no error of any kind) · area: `crates/chroma-project/src/manifest.rs` (`append_media_clip`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, rebuilding the before/after comparison reel via MCP after this session's B-075/B-077/D-194/B-079 fps-unit work had already landed. `new_project` placed a real 2940×1670@47.6fps screen recording; the GUI's own timeline block rendered roughly TWICE its real 39.4s length (`00:01:18:02` in the transport readout — 1875 source frames / 24fps ≈ 78.1s, vs. the real 1875/47.6 ≈ 39.4s), with the filmstrip thumbnail only covering the correct, real proportion and a blank tail filling the rest — screenshotted live by the owner, who caught it immediately ("is this solved? i dont think so the frame issue").
- **repro:** `new_project("x", ["/path/to/a-video-not-at-24fps.mov"])`, then `get_timeline` — the placed clip's `sourceFps` is `null`, so every `source_fps ?? fps` consumer (B-077's GUI display, B-079's live playback, this session's own D-197 audio-export mixing) silently substitutes the project's default 24fps for the clip's real rate.
- **expected:** a clip built from a successfully-probed source carries that source's real fps from the moment it's created, the same guarantee `editor_add_clip` (TS, `useEditorControl.ts`) already gives — this is the ONE OTHER real `Clip`-construction site (used by both `new_project`'s seed-media placement and "add to grading"), and it never got the same treatment.
- **cause:** `append_media_clip` already probes the source (`probe_cached`, feeding `frames`/`duration`/`has_audio` correctly) but built the `Clip` literal with a bare `..Default::default()` for every field it didn't explicitly list — `source_fps` was never one of them, so it silently defaulted to `None` despite the real probed `VideoInfo` sitting one line above, fully available. The B-075/B-077/D-194/B-079 passes all fixed CONSUMPTION of `source_fps` at various layers; none of them audited the (small number of) CREATION sites for whether the field was actually being populated in the first place.
- **fix:** `let source_fps = probed.as_ref().map(|i| i.fps()).filter(|f| *f > 0.0);` (mirrors `VideoInfo::fps()`'s own `fps_num/fps_den` rational, the same computation the probe's own DTO conversion uses elsewhere) threaded into the `Clip` literal explicitly, ahead of its `..Default::default()`.
- **verification:** new regression test `new_project_seeds_source_fps_from_the_real_probe_b082` (`crates/chroma-project/src/manifest.rs`) — a real ffmpeg-synthesized 25fps clip (deliberately NOT the 24fps default, so a false agreement can't mask the bug), asserting `source_fps == Some(25.0)` both immediately after `new_project_in` and after a `load_manifest` round trip (closing the door on a B-078-style persistence regression too). `cargo test -p chroma-project`: 53/53 (was 52). `cargo clippy -p chroma-project --all-targets`: 15 warnings, same pre-existing count/location as before this fix (confirmed via `git stash` diff). `cargo fmt -p chroma-project -- --check`: clean on every line this fix touched.

## B-083 — `open_project` switches the Colorist tab's own project state but never refreshes the Edit tab's `useEditorTimelineStore` — the GUI (and every `editor_*`/`get_timeline` MCP call) keeps showing whichever project's timeline was last loaded into that store, not the one `open_project` just opened

status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-203) · severity: medium-high (silently reading/editing the WRONG project's timeline after switching projects via MCP — no error, no stale-state warning of any kind) · area: `app/src/Root.tsx` (the composition root's bridges), `packages/editor/src/timelineStore.ts`, and — same root cause, found by audit in the same pass — `packages/motion/src/motionProjectStore.ts` + `packages/bridge/src/media.ts`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, rebuilding the comparison reel — after starting a fresh scratch project (`perf-comparison-reel-v2`) to route around B-082/B-073, called `open_project("perf-comparison-reel")` to switch back to the earlier, already-correctly-stacked project. The tool call itself succeeded (`{opened: {name: "perf-comparison-reel", ...}, shots: [...]}` — real data for the RIGHT project), but an immediate `get_timeline` right after still returned `perf-comparison-reel-v2`'s own timeline, unchanged. Confirmed not a timing flake: repeated after several other calls, and after re-opening `perf-comparison-reel-v2` itself (a no-op project switch) — `get_timeline` never changed either time.
- **repro:** with any project A already loaded and its timeline visible in the Edit tab, call `open_project` (or `new_project`) for a DIFFERENT project B. `open_project`'s own return value correctly reflects B (its Colorist `shots`). `get_timeline` (and the Edit tab GUI, unrefreshed) keeps showing A's timeline.
- **expected:** opening a different project should make the Edit tab load and display THAT project's own active timeline, the same "one shared state" guarantee D-020 makes for everything else the GUI and MCP layer touch.
- **cause:** `new_project`/`open_project` (`useChromaControl.ts`) call `useSessionStore.getState().newProject`/`openProject`, which hydrate Colorist's own session state (`_hydrateOpenDto`) — entirely separate from `useEditorTimelineStore` (`@chroma/editor`), which only loads a timeline via its own `load()` (`chroma_timeline_get`) on `setProjectOpen(true)`, triggered by the APP's initial boot listener, not by either control-hook handler. Nothing in the `new_project`/`open_project` control-hook path calls that reload — the store simply never hears that the active project changed underneath it, and keeps serving whatever timeline it already had in memory (including one created by an EARLIER `new_project` call in the same session).
- **the real root cause, one level up from the above (D-203):** the composition root (`app/src/Root.tsx`) told the Edit tab only *whether* a project was open — `setProjectOpen(!!projectPath || !!projectName)`, a **boolean**, and `setProjectOpen` is idempotent. Both sides of a project *switch* are `true`, so the switch was literally invisible to that bridge: the effect re-ran with an unchanged value and returned immediately. This is why nothing in the `open_project`/`new_project` path could have fixed it by itself — the signal it would have had to change didn't carry the information in the first place, and the same hole was open for the GUI path and for any future caller.
- **fix (D-203):** the one input every per-project store takes is now the open project's **identity**, not a boolean. `useSessionStore` gains `selectProjectKey` (the `.chroma` path, or an `untitled:<name>` marker for an in-memory session, `null` for none); `Root.tsx` hands it to `useEditorTimelineStore.setOpenProject(key)`. A changed key is a real switch: the store drops everything that belonged to the outgoing project (timeline, timeline list, selection, gap selection, playhead, play flag) and runs the same load + bounded retry ladder + `loadList()` a first open runs — the store's existing B-034/D-112 machinery, reused, not a second reload path. The same-key call stays a no-op, so re-opening the project already open is still free. Two more things fall out of it: a debounced save still pending for the outgoing project is cancelled rather than left to fire (by the time the store hears about a switch, `chroma_timeline_set` would land on the INCOMING project — see the B-080 note below), and `useEditorControl`'s `noTimeline` error is now status-aware, so an `editor_*` call landing in the brief reload window says "still loading, retry" instead of the flatly wrong "no timeline — open a project first". `editor_get_state` also reports `openProject` (which project the Edit tab believes it is on), so this class of report is answerable in one call.
- **the same gap in two more stores, audited and fixed in the same pass** (same root cause, same one-line shape, so folded in here rather than filed as separate bugs): `@chroma/motion`'s `motionProjectStore` took the identical boolean, and its manifest is a per-project sidecar — a switch left the Motion tab editing project A's manifest, where a save would have written it into project B's own `motion/manifest.json`. It now takes the project *path* (still the narrower signal B-058/D-150 chose, deliberately null for an Untitled session) and re-reads on a change. `@chroma/bridge`'s media pool had no composition-root bridge at all — `SourcesPanel` fired the only `refresh()` from its own effect, keyed on the same boolean — so `items`, the array `editor_add_clip` resolves `mediaId`/`sourcePath` against (B-084), stayed on the outgoing project's media. It now takes the same project key from `Root.tsx`, clears on a switch, and its `refresh()` is token-guarded so a slow read from the previous project can't land on the new one; `SourcesPanel` keeps only the panel-local reset (active bin + multi-selection, both equally per-project).
- **verification:** `npm test --workspaces` all green — `@chroma/editor` 499/499 (+4 new: a switch loads the incoming project's timeline and timeline list; the outgoing project's timeline/playhead/selection are gone *synchronously*, never served under the new project's name; a same-key re-open issues no calls; a pending debounced save never writes into the new project), `@chroma/motion` 441/441 (+2), `@chroma/bridge` 5/5 (new — this package had no test setup; it now has the same `vitest run` script the other packages use), `@chroma/history` 11/11. `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; the app's own `tsc` has exactly the same 64 pre-existing errors before and after (none in any file this touched); `npm run build --workspace app` builds the whole frontend clean. **NOT live-verified against the running Tauri app** — the isolated worktree has no built `target/`, and a from-scratch cargo build plus the fixed control-server port would have collided with the checkout's own running dev server. The tests drive the real store actions against a mocked `invoke`, which covers the exact repro at the store boundary but not the end-to-end `open_project` → `get_timeline` round trip; that round trip is worth one real check the next time the app is running (open A, load its timeline, `open_project(B)`, confirm `get_timeline`/`editor_get_state.openProject` report B).
- **known residual (by design, called out so it isn't rediscovered as a bug):** the reload is a React effect, so it lands a beat *after* `open_project`'s own MCP response.
