An `editor_*` call issued immediately after the switch can catch the store mid-reload — it now says so ("still loading, retry") rather than answering with stale data. Both MCP docstrings say this.
- **workaround (no longer needed, kept for the record):** before this fix, the only safe course was to avoid `open_project`/`new_project` mid-session once a timeline was loaded and in use.

## B-084 — `editor_import_media` reported success for a path already known to the Rust-side manifest, but never populated the frontend's own `useMediaPoolStore.items` for it — leaving `editor_add_clip` unable to find that item at all

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: high (any media seeded via `new_project`'s own `media_paths`, or otherwise already present in `project.json`'s `media` array by the time `editor_import_media` is called, is permanently unreachable to `editor_add_clip` for the rest of the session — no error pointing at the real cause, just "no pool item matching") · area: `packages/editor/src/useEditorControl.ts` (`editor_import_media`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, rebuilding the comparison reel — moved a clip off the track `new_project` had auto-placed it on, then tried to re-place it via `editor_add_clip` (by `media_id` AND by `source_path`, both failed identically: `"no pool item matching mediaId/sourcePath — call editor_import_media first"`) despite `editor_import_media` on the exact same path having just reported `{ok: true, added: []}` moments earlier, and `project.json` on disk clearly listing the item in its `media` array.
- **repro:** `new_project("x", [pathA])` (which seeds `pathA` into the manifest's own `media` array directly, entirely server-side, bypassing this store), then `editor_import_media({paths: [pathA]})` — the Rust command correctly reports `added: []` (nothing NEW to add, it already exists), but `useMediaPoolStore.importPaths`'s own `if (added.length > 0) set(...)` never fires, so `items` (this store's OWN client-side cache, populated ONLY by that append or by an explicit `refresh()`) never actually gains an entry for `pathA` — even though the Rust manifest has had it all along. `editor_add_clip`'s lookup reads exactly this `items` array.
- **expected:** a tool that just reported success for a path should leave that path reachable by the next tool (`editor_add_clip`) that needs it — "already known" and "not in my local cache" should not be indistinguishable failure states.
- **cause:** `useMediaPoolStore.importPaths` only ever appends its own `added` array to `items`; nothing calls `refresh()` (the store's own full re-fetch from `chroma_media_list`) when `added` comes back empty for a path that ISN'T actually already in `items` — the exact same symptom `app/src/main.tsx`'s own `onMotionRendered` callback had already hit and already worked around (see its own comment: "already in the pool from an earlier render... refresh so its thumbnail/video info reflect the new render"), but that fallback was never applied to `editor_import_media`'s own handler.
- **fix:** `editor_import_media` now calls `useMediaPoolStore.getState().refresh()` when `importPaths` returns an empty `added`, then re-derives its own `added` response from the refreshed `items` filtered to the requested paths — mirroring `onMotionRendered`'s established pattern for the identical symptom, rather than inventing a new one.
- **verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 463/463 (no regression; no new unit test added — this handler's real behavior depends on a live Tauri `invoke`/probe round trip, the same reason its sibling `editor_*` media/probe handlers have no unit coverage either, only live verification). Live-verified against the real running app: after the fix, `editor_import_media` on an already-manifest-known path followed immediately by `editor_add_clip` for that same path succeeds.

## B-085 — On-canvas clip selection for resize/reposition doesn't work: a clip's `TransformOverlay` handles only activate once the clip is ALREADY selected via the timeline — clicking the clip directly on the canvas does nothing

status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-204) — but D-204 alone did NOT fix it in the real app; the repro below kept failing in the shipped WKWebView window until **B-092** (same day). Both entries are needed to understand this bug: D-204 built the missing behaviour, B-092 is why that behaviour never ran outside a stubbed backend. · severity: medium (a real, owner-reported usability gap — "not able to click on a clip in canvas to resize it, need to click from timeline") · area: `packages/editor/src/useCanvasClipPick.ts` (new), `canvasPick.ts` (new), `transformGeometry.ts`, `useClipGeometry.ts`, `PreviewPane.tsx`, `TransformOverlay.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, screenshotted by the owner directly during the comparison-reel rebuild while trying to grab a clip's resize handles straight from the preview canvas.
- **repro:** with no clip selected (or a DIFFERENT clip selected), click directly on a clip's own content in the live preview canvas — nothing selects, no `TransformOverlay` handles appear for it. Selecting the same clip via the timeline first, THEN returning to the canvas, does show its handles/let you drag them (D-199's own live-verification already covered THIS half; the "click straight on the canvas" half was never covered because that verification pass always pre-selected via the harness's own `select()` helper, not a canvas click).
- **expected:** clicking a clip's own rendered content on the canvas should select it — standard behavior in a reference NLE's own program monitor.
- **cause (confirmed, and it was exactly the "nothing is listening" shape the entry guessed, not something narrower):** nothing in the preview's component tree had a pointer handler for the picture at all. `PreviewPane`'s surface was an `<img>` plus two overlays: `CanvasBoundary` (`pointer-events-none` outright) and `TransformOverlay`, whose root is also `pointer-events-none` with `pointer-events-auto` only on the selected clip's own box body and corner handles. So a press anywhere else on the canvas reached no handler whatsoever. `TransformOverlay`'s own module doc had said so all along — "clicking on the picture to select is deliberately deferred... this component only ever REFLECTS the timeline-side selection, never sets it" (D-136 Phase 1) — and `docs/notes/on-canvas-transform.md`'s Open Question 3 assumed closing it needed the backend to report per-layer rects, which is why it stayed open. **That assumption was wrong**: the frontend can already derive every layer's on-canvas rect itself — `chroma_timeline_clip_geometry` gives a clip's natural footprint as a composition fraction, and `position_*`/`scale`/`box_*` are already normalised fractions on `Clip` — so no new Rust command was needed.
- **fix (D-204):** `canvasPick.ts` (pure, node-tested) mirrors the compositor rather than guessing: `visibleVideoLayersAt` is the TS twin of Rust's `Timeline::resolve_visible_video_layers_at` (video tracks only, `hidden` excluded, gaps excluded, **track-index-ascending == topmost-first**, because `composite_video_frame` decodes in that order and paints in reverse), and the box each layer is hit-tested against comes from `transformGeometry.ts`'s `clipBoxFraction(resolvedBoxSize(...))` — literally the same call `TransformOverlay` makes to DRAW its at-rest box (`resolvedBoxSize`/`fractionBoxContains` were extracted for exactly that, so a click can never select a clip whose handles then appear somewhere else). `useClipGeometries` fetches the same per-clip probe for every visible layer, keyed on the request list's content so playback doesn't refetch per frame. `useCanvasClipPick` wires it to `PreviewPane`'s surface and drives the SAME `useEditorTimelineStore.setSelection` the timeline does (no parallel selection concept), with empty canvas clearing selection + gap, mirroring `TimelinePane`'s own D-100/D-105 rule.
- **the shape that did NOT work, found live and worth recording:** the obvious implementation — a full-bleed `pointer-events-auto` hit div under `TransformOverlay` in z-order — passed unit and jsdom tests and is **wrong**, which only real-browser hit-testing showed. A full-frame clip's own transform box is itself full-bleed, so once such a clip was selected its box swallowed every press and nothing else on the canvas could ever be picked again. No z-order fixes that (the two genuinely overlap; which should win depends on what is under the pointer). The shipped version is therefore a **capture-phase** `pointerdown` listener on the shared surface that decides per press: a corner-handle grab (`[data-transform-handle]`, new marker) is never a selection; a press on the already-selected clip's own picture is D-136's move drag and is left alone; anything else selects the topmost hit layer (or clears) and `stopPropagation()`s so it cannot also start a drag of the selection it just replaced.
- **verified:** (1) `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 534/534, up from 495 — 18 new pure hit-test tests, 8 new shared-box-math tests, and a new **12-scenario jsdom DOM test** (`PreviewPane.pick.dom.test.tsx`) that mounts the real `PreviewPane` and drives real `PointerEvent`s under StrictMode with zero console errors; confirmed non-vacuous by sabotaging the handler and watching 8 of them fail. (2) `tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean. (3) **Real Chromium, real layout, real `document.elementFromPoint` hit-testing** via `app/harness.html?mode=preview` (D-142/D-199's permanent harness, extended here so `chroma_timeline_clip_geometry`'s stub resolves its actual arguments — without which two layers cannot have different boxes): with a 25%-scale PIP on track 0 over a full-frame clip on track 1 and **nothing selected**, a press on the PIP's own picture selected it and its handles appeared; a press where only the lower clip is selected that one; a press on the PIP *through the selected full-frame clip's own transform box* selected the PIP; a press on the letterbox cleared the selection; and then **dragging the selected PIP's corner handle really resized it** — the overlay box grew live (306→377→448→519→590 px) and committed `scale` 0.25→0.482 with `position_x/y` untouched. Zero console errors/warnings on that run.
- **follow-up, same day — the repro above was still failing in the real app, and the verification above is exactly why that was missed.** The owner tested a fresh build of `main` with this merged and reported live: "still not able to select / unselect clips / video by clicking on the canvas", while confirming "from timeline it works". Every check listed under **verified** answers `chroma_timeline_frame` from a synchronous stub, so the first frame and the composition size land in one microtask flush; the real backend takes hundreds of milliseconds to decode a frame and milliseconds to report a composition size, and that ordering difference is the whole bug. Nothing about D-204's own logic was wrong — the listener it installs was simply never installed, because the content box it gates on was measured against a container that had not been rendered yet. Root cause, fix and its own regression coverage are **B-092**; that entry is the one to read.

## B-092 — `useContentBox` measures a container that may not be mounted yet and can never re-measure once it is, so the Edit tab's canvas click-to-select was dead in the real app while passing every stub-backed test (B-085's second half)

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: high (the user-visible effect is B-085 verbatim — clicking the preview canvas selects and deselects nothing, ever — and the same latent defect silently disabled `CanvasBoundary` in any build without React's dev-only StrictMode double-invoke) · area: `packages/player/src/useContentBox.ts`, `packages/editor/src/{PreviewPane.tsx,useCanvasClipPick.ts,CanvasBoundary.tsx,TransformOverlay.tsx}`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, owner-reported live against a real WKWebView build of `main` immediately after D-204 merged — "still not able to select / unselect clips / video by clicking on the canvas... from timeline it works."
- **repro:** open a project with video clips in the Edit tab and click anywhere on the preview picture. Nothing selects; with a clip already selected (picked from the timeline) clicking empty canvas does not clear it either. Selecting from the timeline still works, and the transform handles that appear from a timeline selection still drag correctly. **Not reproducible against any stubbed backend** — see cause.
- **expected:** the canvas click-to-select D-204 built actually runs.
- **cause (confirmed by reproducing it in a test, not inferred):** `useContentBox` took a `React.RefObject<HTMLElement | null>` and read `.current` inside a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `[containerRef, width, height]`. A ref object notifies nobody when it is populated, so that effect can only ever re-run when the *size* changes. `PreviewPane` renders the surface `<div>` every overlay measures against **only once the first frame exists** (`frameSrc !== null`), and the two backend calls that feed this race each other: `chroma_timeline_composition_size` is a cheap settings/probe read that answers in milliseconds, `chroma_timeline_frame` is a real ffmpeg decode over IPC that answers hundreds of milliseconds later. So in the real app the size always landed in a commit where the container did not exist yet — the hook stored a 0×0 box — and the commit that later mounted the container changed no dependency, so it never measured again. `useCanvasClipPick` gates listener registration on `ready = !!size && contentBox.width > 0 && contentBox.height > 0`, which was therefore permanently false: **the capture-phase `pointerdown` listener was never added to the DOM at all**, which is why neither selecting nor clearing worked, with no error anywhere.
- **why every existing test and the browser harness passed:** both the jsdom suites and `app/harness.html`'s `installInvokeStub` answer `chroma_timeline_frame` synchronously, so the frame and the composition size resolve in the same flush and React commits them together — the container mounts in the *same* commit that changes `size`, the layout effect re-runs, and the box is correct. The passing tests were not wrong about the logic; they never exercised the ordering the real backend always has. (`act()` compounds this: it drains everything scheduled inside one block into a single render pass, so even a delayed stub collapses back into one commit unless the two are awaited in separate `act` blocks — which is what the new test does.)
- **why the sibling overlays looked fine, which is what made this read as "only the click is broken":** `TransformOverlay`'s size comes from a per-selection `chroma_timeline_clip_geometry` probe that resolves long *after* the container exists, so selecting from the timeline changed its `size` and forced a correct re-measure — exactly the owner's "from timeline it works". `CanvasBoundary` was saved only by React StrictMode's **dev-only** double-invoke of a newly-mounted effect (its second run happens after the parent's ref is attached); in a release build it was as dead as the click. StrictMode cannot rescue `useCanvasClipPick`, because that hook belongs to `PreviewPane`, which mounted long before.
- **fix:** `useContentBox` now takes the **element** (`HTMLElement | null`), not a ref object, and `PreviewPane` holds the surface node in state via a callback ref (`const [surfaceEl, setSurfaceEl] = useState(...)`, `ref={setSurfaceEl}`) — React's own prescribed shape for measuring a node that may mount later. The node appearing is now a real render with a real dependency change, so every consumer re-measures when it does, in any ordering and with or without StrictMode. All three call sites (`useCanvasClipPick`, `CanvasBoundary`, `TransformOverlay`) take the element too, which also removed two `containerRef.current` null-guards inside pointer handlers. Deliberately fixed in the shared hook rather than by making `PreviewPane` render its surface unconditionally: the second would have worked for these three consumers by re-establishing an ordering invariant nothing states, and left the same trap set for the next caller.
- **verified:** (1) two new cases in `PreviewPane.pick.dom.test.tsx` reproduce the REAL ordering — `chroma_timeline_frame` resolving on a real timer, in its own `act()` block, after the composition size has already committed — one under StrictMode (asserting the click selects) and one **without** StrictMode, i.e. the shape a release build runs (asserting the click selects *and* the canvas boundary renders). Both fail on the pre-fix code (`expected [] to deeply equal [ { track: 0, id: 'solo' } ]` and `expected null not to be null`) and pass after; confirmed by reverting the five source files and re-running. (2) `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 542/542. (3) `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` and `-p packages/player` clean, `-p app` the same 64 pre-existing errors as `main`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` exits 0 with only the pre-existing `RapidRAW` lib warnings `main` already has (no Rust changed by this fix). (4) **Live, in a real WKWebView window, with real clicks — the tier that was missing.** Built and ran a second, fully isolated Tauri dev instance from this worktree (its own vite port 1430, `CHROMA_CONTROL_PORT=19799`, and its own bundle identifier so `tauri-plugin-single-instance`'s `/tmp/<identifier>_si.sock` doesn't make it exit into the checkout's already-running app), opened the real `perf-comparison-reel-v3.chroma`, and drove real `cliclick` presses at real screen coordinates over the preview picture, reading `editor_get_state`'s new `selection` field over HTTP after each. Same instance, same coordinates, three passes with only the five source files changing (Vite HMR in between, no restart): **fix applied** — click at CSS (640,218) → `selection` `[]` → `[{track: 0, id: "0529d7f1-…"}]`, click at (950,218) on the letterbox → back to `[]`, click at (700,218) → selected again; **fix reverted** — the identical clicks at (640,218), (640,180), (600,218), (700,218), (950,218) all left `selection` at `[]`, which is the owner's report reproduced exactly; **fix re-applied** — select/clear/select again. That A/B is the evidence this entry rests on, not the jsdom tier.

## B-088 — The Edit tab's live-preview compositor doesn't visibly reflect a clip's current position/crop/scale — the underlying data (and a real export) are correct, but the on-screen frame shows the clip un-transformed, and dragging `TransformOverlay`'s handles moves only the overlay box, never the pixels underneath

status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-202) · severity: high (the live preview is actively misleading — a human editing by eye has no way to tell what they're actually building, and dragging on canvas silently writes real, wrong transform values with no visual confirmation) · area: `packages/editor/src/PreviewPane.tsx` (the scrub effect's refetch trigger), `packages/editor/src/timelineStore.ts` (the new `savedVersion`). **Not** the Rust compositor — see "ruled out" below
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, twice independently during the comparison-reel rebuild: (1) after setting both clips' `position_y`/`crop_left`/`crop_right` via `editor_set_clip_transform` to stack them (top half / bottom half of a 1080×1920 canvas, safe margins), the preview showed the AFTER clip full-bleed, uncropped, un-repositioned — screenshotted live by the owner ("the video is not stacked yet... i think in MCP it is, some UI issue"); (2) the owner then dragged the AFTER clip's `TransformOverlay` handles directly on canvas — the selection box visibly moved/resized, but the clip's own rendered pixels never moved, screenshotted live ("even though i move and all nothing moves... just the box not the clip").
- **repro:** set a clip's `position_y`/`crop_left`/`crop_right`/`scale` (via `editor_set_clip_transform`, keyframes, or a canvas drag) to anything other than the identity transform; the live preview keeps rendering that clip as if none of it were set. `TransformOverlay`'s own selection box DOES move correctly (it's computed independently from the same live store state, not decoded from any rendered frame) — only the actual composited image lags/never catches up.
- **expected:** the live preview is the one surface a human (or an agent sanity-checking before export) can trust to show the ACTUAL current state — it should match a real export of the same frame.
- **confirmed NOT the compositor's math, and NOT a data-persistence bug:** exported the exact same timeline (`editor_export`, 1080×1920) to a real file and extracted a real frame via `ffmpeg`/viewed it directly — correctly stacked, correctly cropped, correct safe margins, matching every value `get_timeline` reported. `get_timeline` itself also correctly reflects every write, including the owner's own canvas drag (which really did write `position_x: -0.00734, position_y: -0.31837, scale: 0.36068` to the clip's base transform — confirmed, then restored to the intended `0/0.130208/1` since the drag had no visual feedback to catch it going wrong). So the STORED state and the EXPORT PATH (`timelineExport.ts`'s ffmpeg filter graph) are both provably correct; the defect is isolated to the SEPARATE live-preview compositor path (`chroma_timeline_frame`) and/or its own React-side fetch/redraw triggering, which this entry has not yet distinguished between.
- **the other candidate, ruled out for real — the Rust compositor is correct and needed no change:** read `chroma_timeline_frame` → `resolve_visible_video_layers_at` → `composite_video_frame` → `composite_layer_onto` end to end against `timelineExport.ts`'s (confirmed-correct) filter-graph compiler, field by field. It reads every transform field the export path does, resolving static-or-keyframed values through the same D-034 engine (`resolve_clip_transform`), and its single-layer plain-decode fast path is already gated on `ClipTransform::is_identity()` precisely so a lone transformed clip cannot skip compositing (D-132/B-053). That is not an assumption: `chroma::edit`'s own `composite_tests` already assert the painted pixels for each of these independently — `composite_layer_onto_respects_position_offset`, `_respects_scale`, `position_is_normalised_per_axis`, `composite_layer_onto_crops_in_place_without_recentring`, `_crops_each_edge_independently`, `crop_composes_with_scale`, `only_a_genuinely_untransformed_clip_takes_the_fast_path` — all of them re-run green here (`cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib chroma::edit`, 33 passed / 0 failed), on top of D-199's own live verification, which drove the unmodified production `timeline_frame` with two real synthetic clips at the exact reported stacking and looked at the decoded result. Zero Rust changed by this fix, so no `docs/09-engine-notes.md` divergence entry.
- **cause — an ordering race between two facts that were each individually fine.** (1) `chroma_timeline_frame` is handed **no timeline**: it resolves the open project's **persisted** manifest off disk (`chroma_project::timeline::resolve_timeline_and_settings`) and composites from that. (2) `timelineStore`'s `applyOp` updates the in-memory `timeline` **optimistically** and persists it via `chroma_timeline_set` on a deliberate 400 ms debounce (`SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS`). `PreviewPane`'s scrub effect keyed its refetch on the **`timeline` object's identity** — i.e. on fact (2)'s optimistic half. So every edit fired exactly one `chroma_timeline_frame`, roughly 400 ms *before* the state it was meant to show existed anywhere the backend could see it, and got back the pre-edit picture. Nothing changed identity again when the save finally landed, so nothing ever refetched: the preview sat **one edit behind, permanently**, with no error of any kind. Every write path shares this — the Inspector, `TransformOverlay`'s pointer-up commit, and the `editor_set_clip_transform` MCP tool all funnel through the same `applyOp`, which is why both of the owner's independent repros above are the same defect. `TransformOverlay`'s box appearing to work is the same fact from the other side: it is drawn from the live store and never from a rendered frame, so it alone was reading the half of the state that was up to date.
- **why "one edit behind" read as "dead" rather than "laggy":** the *next* edit's own (equally premature) fetch is what finally picks up the *previous* one, so a stream of edits shows a picture that is always exactly one step stale — and a canvas drag commits once, on pointer-up, so a drag-then-look sequence never produces the second edit that would have revealed the lag. `EditorTab`'s window-`focus` refetch (`load()`, which does replace `timeline` from the backend) could incidentally rescue it, which is why this was intermittent-looking rather than uniformly reproducible.
- **fix:** `timelineStore` gained `savedVersion`, a monotonic counter bumped **only** when the backend's copy of the timeline is known to have changed — on `chroma_timeline_set` actually resolving (in `_flushSave`) and on `chroma_timeline_get` actually landing (in `load()`, which also covers the focus refetch, the timeline switcher, and `restoreSnapshot`'s trailing reconcile, so undo/redo needs no bump of its own). `PreviewPane`'s scrub effect now depends on `[playhead, playing, hasTimeline, savedVersion]` instead of on `timeline`, which is exactly the set of things that can make `chroma_timeline_frame` return a different picture. Bumping on the promise's `.then` rather than optimistically beside the `set` is the whole point — an optimistic bump would reinstate the identical race. Deliberately **not** fixed by removing or shortening the debounce: writing `project.json` on every pointer-move is the worse trade, and the real defect was the ordering, not the delay. Residual, intended behaviour: the picture now catches up ~400 ms after the last edit in a burst rather than instantly — which is what `TransformOverlay`'s own module doc always claimed happened ("the composited picture catches up once the drag commits and the next frame is fetched") and, until now, never did.
- **verification:** new real-DOM regression test `packages/editor/src/PreviewPane.staleness.dom.test.tsx` (jsdom tier, `testUtils/pointerHarness`) — mounts the **real** `PreviewPane` against the **real** `timelineStore`, with an `invoke` stub that reproduces the one backend property this bug turns on (`chroma_timeline_frame` returns what `chroma_timeline_set` last persisted, never the store's in-memory timeline), then asserts the user-visible outcome: the preview `<img>`'s actual `src` after a `set_clip_transform` op and a real wait past the debounce. **Confirmed failing before the fix and passing after** (pre-fix it returned `POSITION_Y=0` for a clip set to `0.25` — the bug itself, reproduced in a test — while the "a frame was fetched" assertion passed, which is precisely why "did we refetch?" was never the right question). A second case covers two consecutive edits, the shape that made this look like a one-edit lag. `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 497/497 (was 495, +2). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `-p app` the same 64 pre-existing errors as `main`, zero new. `reactCompiler.test.ts`'s `PreviewPane` bailout check (D-201) still passes, so the edited component is still auto-memoized. Also added: `waitMs` to `testUtils/pointerHarness.ts` — an `act()`-wrapped real-timer wait, the missing sibling of the existing `waitFrames` (frames are useless for a 400 ms debounce, and the state updates its callback makes would otherwise land outside `act` and warn). Not live-verified in the running Tauri app: not practical to build the full WKWebView app from this isolated worktree — disclosed, not glossed over, and the reason the regression test was built to drive the real component and assert real rendered output rather than to check that a command was called.

## B-086 — `CanvasBoundary`'s displayed composition size goes stale after a `set_project_settings` write made from anywhere OTHER than `CanvasSettingsPopover`'s own "Apply" button — e.g. the `set_project_settings` MCP tool

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: low (display-only — a genuinely stale NUMBER on the preview's boundary overlay; the real project settings, and everything downstream of them, are correct) · area: `packages/editor/src/useCompositionSize.ts`, `packages/editor/src/CanvasSettingsPopover.tsx`, `packages/editor/src/PreviewPane.tsx`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/project.rs`, `app/src/harness-main.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, rebuilding the comparison reel — called the `set_project_settings` MCP tool (`width: 1080, height: 1920`); the tool call itself succeeded and `chroma_project_get_settings` (which `CanvasSettingsPopover` reads on open) correctly showed 1080×1920 when the owner opened that popover — but `CanvasBoundary`'s own overlay kept showing the OLD clip-derived `2940×1670` the whole time, screenshotted live by the owner ("i see this... UI and MCP mismatch").
- **repro:** with a timeline already loaded and its `CanvasBoundary` visible, call `set_project_settings` (MCP, or any path other than clicking "Apply" in `CanvasSettingsPopover` itself) — the boundary's displayed size does not update until the next unrelated timeline edit (any `applyOp`) happens to fire.
- **cause:** genuinely anticipated, not a surprise — `useCompositionSize.ts`'s own module doc said outright: "a `set_project_settings` write doesn't itself touch the timeline, so a caller applying one MUST also bump a `refreshToken`... done here by accepting an optional extra dependency value." `CanvasSettingsPopover.tsx`'s own "Apply" handler did exactly that for ITS OWN write. The `set_project_settings` MCP tool calls `useSessionStore.getState().setProjectSettings` directly — a completely different code path that had no way to reach into `CanvasBoundary`'s own component-local `refreshToken` state.
- **expected:** any successful project-settings write, regardless of which caller made it, should make the boundary overlay reflect the new size promptly — not depend on incidentally happening to also touch the timeline afterward.
- **fix:** the proposed broadcast shape, verified rather than copied blindly (`chroma_project_set_settings`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/project.rs`, had no `AppHandle` and emitted nothing before this — confirmed by reading it, not assumed). It now takes `app_handle: tauri::AppHandle` and emits `chroma://project-settings-changed` (payload: the merged `ProjectSettings`) on every successful write, unconditionally — not gated on which caller made it. `useCompositionSize` (`packages/editor/src/useCompositionSize.ts`) listens for that event directly (`@tauri-apps/api/event`'s `listen`, the same `safeUnlisten`-guarded pattern `useEditorControl.ts`/`useMotionControl.ts` already use for B-032/B-034/D-112) and bumps an internal refresh token — replacing the old per-caller `extraDep` token entirely, since the broadcast now covers every caller including `CanvasSettingsPopover`'s own. That let `PreviewPane.tsx` drop its `compSizeVersion` state and `CanvasSettingsPopover`'s `onSaved` callback prop outright (dead code once the broadcast exists) rather than leaving both mechanisms running side by side. This is a real fix for the general gap, not a narrow patch for the one MCP symptom — any future caller of `chroma_project_set_settings`, in any language, is covered with zero code on its own side.
- **verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 495/495 including `reactCompiler.test.ts` (the rewritten hook still compiles with no React Compiler bailout). Live-verified in a real Chromium tab against `app/harness.html?mode=preview` (chrome-devtools MCP) — the harness's `installInvokeStub` gained a minimal `plugin:event|listen`/`unlisten`/`emit` + `transformCallback` stand-in (harness-only; no general `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__` reimplementation, see that file's own new comment) so `listen()` works in the browser-only harness, and `chroma_project_set_settings`'s stub now also calls `emitHarnessEvent('chroma://project-settings-changed', ...)`, mirroring the real command. Called `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke('chroma_project_set_settings', {path: null, partial: {width: 1080, height: 1920}})` directly from the browser console — deliberately bypassing `CanvasSettingsPopover`'s own UI entirely, standing in for the MCP tool's path — and confirmed the boundary overlay's on-screen label changed from `1920×1080` to `1080×1920` immediately, with no other interaction: `{"before":"1920×1080","res":{"width":1080,"height":1920},"after":"1080×1920"}`.
## B-087 — Two components read Zustand state with `getState()` during render, so their UI never updates when that state changes — the Colorist "Paste" button is the visible one
status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: low-medium (self-heals: both components re-render often for unrelated reasons, so the stale window is usually short — but it is a real, reproducible wrong-state window with no error of any kind) · area: `app/src/components/views/EditorView.tsx` (line ~111), `app/src/App.tsx` (`ImageDragOverlayNode`, line ~98)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, during D-201's React Compiler bailout pass. Both files bail out on *"Hooks may not be referenced as normal values, they must be called"* — flagged in `docs/notes/react-compiler-coverage.md` as "a real Rules-of-Hooks issue... worth checking whether it's a latent real bug." It is; this entry is that check's result.
- **repro (EditorView):** in the Colorist tab with an image selected, copy an image's adjustments (which sets `useEditorStore.copiedAdjustments`). The "Paste" button stays disabled until something *else* causes `EditorView` to re-render.
- **expected:** the Paste button enables the moment adjustments are on the clipboard.
- **actual:** `EditorView` renders `isPasteDisabled={useEditorStore.getState().copiedAdjustments === null}`. `getState()` is a one-shot read with **no subscription** — the component's only real subscription to that store is `useEditorStore(useShallow((state) => ({ selectedImage: state.selectedImage })))`, which does not include `copiedAdjustments`. So a change to `copiedAdjustments` alone re-renders nothing, and the button keeps whatever disabled state it last happened to render with.
- **the same shape in `App.tsx`:** `ImageDragOverlayNode` reads `useProcessStore.getState().thumbnails[activeItem.path]` during render. Lower impact (the node is mounted at drag start, by which time the thumbnail usually exists) but the same defect: if that thumbnail arrives *during* the drag, the overlay never picks it up.
- **cause:** `store.getState()` inside a render body is a deliberate escape hatch for reading state you specifically do NOT want to subscribe to (used correctly all over this codebase inside event handlers and effects — e.g. `TimelinePane`'s `locateClip`). Using it in the render body makes render output depend on untracked state, which is exactly the Rules-of-React violation the React Compiler refuses to compile around.
- **fix:** subscribe, exactly as proposed. `EditorView` — `copiedAdjustments: state.copiedAdjustments` added to the existing `useShallow` selector, and `isPasteDisabled` reads the destructured `copiedAdjustments` instead of a fresh `useEditorStore.getState()` call. `App.tsx` — `const url = useProcessStore((s) => s.thumbnails[activeItem.path]);`. Both one-line-per-file and mechanical, as expected. `docs/09-engine-notes.md` divergence entry added (both are vendored-fork files).
- **verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` on `app/` shows the same 64 pre-existing errors as before this change (none in either touched file — confirmed by diffing the error list before/after, not just eyeballing it). `npx eslint` on both files shows only pre-existing `any`/unused-var issues, none on the touched lines. Built and ran the REAL Tauri app from this fix's own isolated worktree (`npm run tauri:dev`, on alternate port 5556 to avoid the shared checkout's own running dev server on 1420 — reverted after) — a full fresh `cargo build` (839 crates) succeeded with no new warnings/errors, and the app launched and stayed up (confirmed via `osascript`'s System Events process list, `RapidRAW` present and running). Could not go further and click through the actual Copy → Paste flow in that live window: this environment's terminal has no macOS Screen Recording permission (`screencapture` fails with "could not create image from display") and no generic GUI-automation/computer-use tool is available for a native window (chrome-devtools MCP is CDP-only, which doesn't reach a WKWebView-backed native Tauri window regardless) — so there was no way to see or drive the window's UI, only confirm the process itself is alive. Reported explicitly rather than claimed as a full interactive pass, per this task's own instruction to disclose when a live GUI check isn't possible. The fix itself is a mechanical, one-line-per-file store-subscription correction verified correct by source inspection (the added field name/type match `useEditorStore`'s real `copiedAdjustments: Adjustments | null` exactly) and is the same pattern already proven live for dozens of other selectors in this codebase.

## B-089 — TWO independent gates both hard-required a video stream and reported ANY pure-audio source `offline` — SFX/music (mp3/flac/wav, no video track at all) could never be imported through `editor_import_media`/`editor_add_clip`, and the second gate kept failing even after the first was fixed

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: high (blocks the Edit tab's own real, shipped audio-track feature — D-057/D-149's mixing/ducking — from ever being exercised with a REAL standalone audio file; only worked before via a video file's embedded audio track) · area: `crates/chroma-media/src/video.rs` (`probe`, `is_video_file`'s callers), `crates/chroma-project/src/manifest.rs` (`media_item_is_online`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, adding real downloaded SFX (click/whoosh/success-chime) and a background-music bed to the comparison reel — `editor_import_media` reported all four real, valid mp3/flac files as `{offline: true, video: null}`, which then made `editor_add_clip` refuse them (`has no known frame count`).
- **repro:** `editor_import_media` on any file with a real audio stream and NO video stream at all (confirmed directly against `ffprobe`: the file probes fine as audio, `ffprobe -select_streams v:0` on the SAME file returns an empty `streams` array, which is a normal, correct answer for a file that genuinely has no video track — not a probe failure).
- **expected:** a real, readable audio-only file is a normal, ONLINE media-pool item — the exact same category video files with embedded audio already are, just without a picture.
- **cause (gate 1 — the probe itself):** `video::probe`'s `ffprobe` call passes `-select_streams v:0` and then does `v.get("streams").and_then(|s| s.get(0)).ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("no video stream in {}"))` — treating "this file has no video stream" (a legitimate, common case for audio-only media) as an unconditional probe ERROR.
- **cause (gate 2 — found AFTER fixing gate 1, same symptom persisted):** `probe_media_item` only calls `video::probe` at all when `media_item_is_online(path)` is true, and `MediaItemDto::from`'s own `offline` field is computed the SAME way, independently — both call `video::is_video_file(path)`, an EXTENSION allowlist (`VIDEO_EXTENSIONS`: mov/mp4/m4v/mkv/webm/avi/mts/m2ts/mxf/braw/r3d — no audio extensions at all). A `.mp3`/`.flac` file was reported offline by this check before gate 1's fix ever got a chance to run.
- **a real, previously-poisoned pool entry made this look unfixed even after both code fixes landed:** `add_media`'s dedup treats "already known by this exact source path" as permanent — reusing a path already recorded `offline` from an EARLIER (pre-fix) import attempt kept replaying the stale stored answer, never re-probing. Confirmed by copying the same files to a path Chroma had never seen, which DID pick up both fixes correctly. See roadmap item 23 for the real, architectural version of this gap (the pool has no correction mechanism at all, not just a probe bug) — not something this entry's own fix attempts to close.
- **fix:** gate 1 — when `-select_streams v:0` returns zero streams, fall back to `probe_audio_only` (new) instead of erroring: `width`/`height` genuinely `0`, `fps_num`/`fps_den` a NOMINAL `24/1` (mirrors `chroma-timeline`'s own `DEFAULT_FPS = 24.0` convention), `frame_count = duration_secs * 24` rounded, `codec`/`pix_fmt`/`color_*` empty, `has_audio`/`audio_sample_rate`/`audio_channels` from the existing real `probe_audio_stream`. Gate 2 — new `is_media_file` (video OR a new `AUDIO_EXTENSIONS` list: mp3/wav/flac/m4a/aac/ogg), used by `media_item_is_online` instead of `is_video_file` alone; `is_video_file` itself is UNCHANGED and still video-only, since its other callers (`image_loader.rs`/`session.rs`/`formats.rs`) genuinely need that narrower distinction for decode-path branching.
- **verification:** new regression tests — `probe_succeeds_on_a_real_audio_only_source_b089` (a real ffmpeg-synthesized pure-audio file via `-f lavfi -i sine=...`, asserting `probe()` succeeds with `width`/`height == 0`, `fps() == 24.0`, real `has_audio`) and `is_media_file_accepts_audio_is_video_file_rejects_b089` (confirms the extension-gate fix without weakening `is_video_file`'s own contract). `cargo test -p chroma-media` 100/100 (was 98), `-p chroma-project` 53/53. Clippy clean, no new warnings on touched lines. Live-verified against the real running app with a genuinely fresh path (never previously imported, sidestepping the poisoned-entry issue above): `editor_import_media` reports `offline: false` with real `video` facts, and `editor_add_clip` places the clip successfully.

## B-090 — `editor_export`'s ffmpeg compiler silently ignored `scale` keyframes: only `position_x`/`position_y` were ever animated per-frame, so a "zoom" authored via `editor_set_clip_keyframes` compiled to a FIXED-SIZE overlay panning around — any pan away from the box's rest position revealed a real black gap where the overlay never grew to cover it

status: fixed (2026-09-07) · severity: high (this is the Edit tab's own advertised keyframe capability — `scale` is explicitly one of the five keyframeable params in `editor_set_clip_keyframes`'s own docstring — silently compiling to a no-op in the one tool that produces a real output file) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildClipFilterChain`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, building the comparison reel's punch-in zoom effect — after several rounds of tuning `position_x`/`position_y` pan values to "center the zoom on the actual cursor," the owner spotted a real black band on one edge of the frame, live, in an exported file ("i can see a black thingy in the side right side so i dont think so its a fluke"). Pixel-sampled a dense sequence of real exported frames (`ffmpeg`, not a screenshot) and found the gap's width tracked `position_x`'s own magnitude almost exactly, with zero dependence on the `scale` keyframe value supposedly in effect at that frame — the tell that `scale` wasn't doing anything.
- **repro:** any clip with `editor_set_clip_keyframes` animating `scale` together with `position_x`/`position_y` (the standard "punch in toward a point" shape: scale up, pan to keep the target roughly centered) — export it, and inspect real decoded frames: the overlay's actual on-screen size never changes: only its position does, panning a same-size box and exposing background wherever it doesn't reach.
- **expected:** an overlay's compiled pixel size follows its own `scale` keyframes over time, exactly as `position_x`/`position_y` already do — `editor_set_clip_keyframes`'s own docstring lists `scale` as an equal, first-class keyframeable param, with no stated exception for export.
- **cause:** `buildClipFilterChain` computed `const scale = clip.scale ?? 1` — the clip's STATIC base field — and used it ONCE to build a fixed `scale=<w>:<h>` ffmpeg step, entirely separate from (and upstream of) the `overlay` step that DOES correctly build a per-frame `x`/`y` expression from keyframes (`positionExpr`/`keyframeExprAt`). `scale` keyframes were faithfully stored on `Clip.chroma_keyframes` (the Rust live-preview compositor and the data model both support them) but this ONE compiler simply never read them.
- **fix:** a `scaleExpr` mirroring `positionExpr`'s own shape exactly (`keyframeExprAt(rebaseKeyframesToClipInput(clip), 'scale', scale, clipFps)` when scale keyframes exist, else the static value) feeds both `widthExpr` and the `'stretch'`-mode `heightExpr`; the `scale` ffmpeg filter gets `eval=frame` (default `eval=init`, sampled once — the same "solved by name, not by effect" trap the earlier fix would have fallen into) whenever `scale` is actually animated. Also fixed in the same pass: `widthExpr`/`heightExpr` are now single-quoted (the identical B-075 class of bug — ffmpeg's filtergraph syntax splits bare commas/colons outside quotes, and a keyframed expression is full of them) and passed as named `w=`/`h=` args instead of positional, since a bare unquoted `-2` sentinel must stay unquoted while a real expression must not.
- **verification:** new real-ffmpeg pixel test (`timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts`) — a solid-green source, `scale` keyframed 0.5→1.0 with NO pan at all (isolates this fix from position-interpolation entirely), sampling a real decoded pixel that is real background-black at the 0.5 frame and real green at the 1.0 frame; this is the first test in the suite that checks actual overlay SIZE rather than a compiled string or "ffmpeg didn't error" (the existing scale-keyframe test only checked the latter, which is exactly how B-090 shipped invisibly underneath it). `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 495/495 (was 494 pre-fix, +1 new pixel test; the 8 existing string-match tests asserting the OLD unquoted/positional `scale=` shape were updated to the new quoted/named shape, not weakened). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean.
- **a genuine dev-mode HMR gap, found while fixing this:** editing `timelineExport.ts` (a plain utility module, not a React component) and letting Vite hot-reload did NOT actually update the code the running app executed — a real `editor_export` call right after the edit still compiled the OLD, unquoted/unanimated `scale=1080*1:-2` shape, verified by inspecting the returned argv directly. A full app restart picked up the fix correctly. Not root-caused further here (this session's own D-201 already fixed ONE HMR full-reload defect in `main.tsx`; this looks like a DIFFERENT gap — a non-component utility module's update not propagating into an already-running Tauri-command closure) — flagged for whoever next touches dev-mode HMR reliability, since "the code you just saved is what's actually running" is exactly the property D-201 was trying to restore.

## B-091 — `editor_export`'s own MCP tool response intermittently fails outright ("Error executing tool editor_export") for a large-but-not-huge payload, instead of the graceful "saved to a file, read it in chunks" handling the same size class gets most of the time — while the underlying ffmpeg render always completes successfully regardless

status: open · severity: medium (never lost real work — the exported FILE is correct every time this was checked — but the tool call itself is unreliable enough to need a blind retry, and a caller with no way to inspect the real file might reasonably conclude the export itself failed) · area: MCP response-size handling between `mcp/server.py`'s `editor_export` and whatever enforces the token/size limit on its return value (not `editor_export`'s own Rust/TS code, which was confirmed unaffected — this is a transport/tooling-layer question, not this repo's own bug, but worth a firm repro trail since it directly blocked verifying a real fix this session)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, re-exporting the comparison reel after the B-090 fix (a real timeline with 2 video clips × ~50 dense eased keyframes each + 11 audio inputs — a large but not unusually large `filter_complex` string by this project's own standards). One `editor_export` call failed with the bare, detail-free error `"Error executing tool editor_export"`; the IMMEDIATATE retry (identical arguments, nothing else changed) instead got the SAME general size class of response gracefully handled — "result exceeds maximum allowed tokens… saved to /path/to/file.txt" — and the export itself was, on inspection of the real output file, correct and complete both times.
- **repro (not fully reliable — this is what's confirmed, not a deterministic trigger):** call `editor_export` on a timeline whose compiled `filter_complex` is large (dozens of keyframes across multiple clips plus several audio inputs — this session's own real reel is a working example). Sometimes the tool call errors outright with no detail; sometimes the identical call is caught by size-limit handling and saved to a file instead; the actual `ffmpeg` render itself has never been observed to fail or produce a wrong file in either case — confirmed each time via direct `ffprobe`/pixel inspection of the real output path from `outPath`, independent of what the tool call itself returned.
- **expected:** either the response is ALWAYS handled the same way for a given size (gracefully truncated/saved, per the size-limit behavior already seen working), or the hard failure mode at minimum surfaces WHAT was too large / what actually happened, rather than a bare "Error executing tool" with zero diagnostic detail.
- **cause:** not root-caused — the failure sits somewhere between `mcp/server.py`'s `editor_export` (which unconditionally returns `json.dumps(_op("editor_export", **args))`, no truncation of its own — see this function's own code, unchanged by this investigation) and whatever layer enforces a maximum tool-result size for this session. Given the SAME general payload size was observed to hit BOTH the graceful path and the hard-failure path on different calls, this looks like a real boundary-condition instability (e.g. a race, or a threshold that isn't purely payload-byte-count) rather than a simple "too big, every time" limit — but this wasn't independently instrumented enough to say precisely where the line is.
- **fix:** not attempted — outside this repo's own code as far as this investigation could tell (mcp/server.py's own `editor_export` implementation was inspected and is not itself doing anything unusual with response size). Documented as a real, reproducible-in-kind (if not reliably reproducible exactly) operational gotcha for whoever next works on the MCP transport layer or on `editor_export`'s own response shape — a real mitigation worth considering on THIS side of the boundary regardless of where the limit itself lives: `editor_export` could return a SUMMARY (outPath, duration, whether it succeeded) by default instead of echoing the full compiled `ffmpeg` argv verbatim on every call, with the full argv available on request — the full argv is genuinely useful for debugging a failed compile, but is arguably the wrong default once a timeline has more than a handful of keyframes, and is the entire reason this response is large enough to hit the limit in the first place.
- **workaround used this session:** verify the real output file directly (`ffprobe`/frame extraction) rather than trusting the tool call's own success/failure signal when the timeline is this complex; retry once on a bare "Error executing tool" failure — it has, so far, always turned out the underlying export had already succeeded.

## B-093 — On a KEYFRAMED clip the on-canvas transform box is drawn from the clip's STATIC transform, so it sits nowhere near the picture — and dragging it commits a write the compositor can never show

status: fixed (2026-09-08, D-209) · severity: high (on any animated clip the whole on-canvas transform affordance is wrong in both directions at once: the box is drawn in the wrong place, the hit rect that selects it is in the wrong place, and a drag silently writes a value that has no effect on any frame) · area: `packages/editor/src/{TransformOverlay.tsx,clipKeyframes.ts,canvasPick.ts}`. **Not** the compositor, not `useContentBox`, not the hit-testing logic B-085/B-092 fixed
- **found:** 2026-09-08, live, reported with screenshots by the owner on `perf-comparison-reel-v3.chroma` — a real project whose video clips carry ~50 pan/zoom keyframes each — immediately after B-092 made canvas click-to-select actually work in the shipped app, which is what put a human in front of these handles on a keyframed clip for the first time. "the box spans the full canvas width at a height that doesn't correspond", "the handles are entirely to the side of the visible picture", and after a resize drag: "i made it small but no clip follows that only the box". On a freshly restarted app, so not stale HMR state.
- **repro:** give a clip keyframes that move `position_x`/`position_y`/`scale` (e.g. `editor_set_clip_keyframes`, or the comparison-reel skill's zoom-punch recipe), select it, and look at the transform box: it is drawn at the clip's static `position_x`/`position_y`/`scale`, not at the animated value the picture is actually composited with. Then drag it: the box moves, the picture never follows, at that frame or any other.
- **expected:** the box is drawn around the picture, at every playhead position, and dragging it changes that picture.
- **cause (confirmed against the owner's own `project.json`, not inferred):** `TransformOverlay` read `clip.position_x`/`position_y`/`scale` directly — the clip's STATIC base fields. The compositor does not: `chroma::edit::resolve_clip_transform` resolves each of those fields through the clip's `chroma_keyframes` against its own source frame and only falls back to a static field for a property no keyframe names. On the owner's track-0 clip those two answers are not close — static `position_x -0.6679`, `scale 0.3764`; keyframed at frame 0 `position_x 0`, `position_y 0.13021`, `scale 1.0` — i.e. the box was drawn at a third size, two thirds of the canvas to the left of a picture that actually fills the frame. Track 1's clip is worse: static `position_x 1.2653` puts its box a full canvas-width off the right edge. Both screenshots are that arithmetic. **Pre-existing since D-136 shipped the overlay**, and invisible until now only because the overlay could not be reached from the canvas at all (B-085/B-092) and a keyframed clip was rarely selected from the timeline while parked on a frame where the two disagree.
- **the second half, and why fixing the drawn box alone would have been a trap:** `TransformOverlay`'s pointer-up committed `set_clip_transform`, which by that op's own contract sets the **base/unkeyframed** value. Per property, a keyframe overrides that base at every frame (D-208's `interpolate_param`) — so on a clip whose keys name `scale`, the drag wrote a real, persisted number that no frame of any render can reflect. That is exactly the owner's "only the box" report, and it is also where the odd static values above came from: they are the fossils of earlier drags that appeared to do nothing. Genuinely different from B-088 (a staleness race in *when* the preview refetched, fixed): here the write itself is inert, so no amount of refetching would show it.
- **also wrong for the same reason, fixed in the same pass:** `canvasPick.ts`'s hit rect, which by construction has to be the same box the overlay draws — so on a keyframed clip you could not click the picture to select it either, and could "select" empty canvas where the static box happened to be. Its own module doc had recorded the static-transform limitation as a deliberate, acceptable one, which it was not.
- **fix (D-209):** `clipKeyframes.ts` gained `resolveClipBoxTransform(clip, sourceFrame)` — the five fields a box's geometry is a function of, each resolved through the **existing** `paramValueAt`, i.e. the same per-property interpolation D-208 already built and the Inspector's number fields already trust, mirroring `interpolate_param` on the Rust side. `TransformOverlay` draws from it at `clipSourceFrame(clip, playhead, fps)`; `canvasPick.ts` hit-tests from it at each layer's own source frame (`CanvasLayer` now carries the `sourceFrame` `clipAt` already computed). And a drag commits **per property**: each dragged property that is already animated gets a keyframe merged in at the playhead (`mergeClipKeyframeParams`), each one that is not gets the ordinary static write — the same rule, through the same helpers, as D-208's `applyParam` for a typed Inspector value. Interpolation is piecewise-linear, so a key inserted at the interpolated value sits exactly on the existing segment: every property the drag did not touch, and every other frame of the animation, is unchanged.
- **one further defect found while proving the fix, fixed here:** `handlePointerUp` committed from inside a `setDraft(current => …)` updater, so `applyOp` (a store write, i.e. an update to `PreviewPane`) ran during React's own update computation — React's "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component", and under StrictMode an updater may run twice, which would commit the gesture and push its undo entry twice. That matters more after this fix than before it: a doubled commit on an animated clip is two keyframe writes, not one idempotent static write. Now committed from a `draftRef` mirror of the same state, keeping the original "commit precisely what was last rendered" property. Surfaced by the new DOM drag coverage below (the pre-existing suites never dragged this component).
- **verified:** (1) `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` **632/632** (was 609): 11 new `clipKeyframes.test.ts` cases for `resolveClipBoxTransform` (one seeded with the owner's real keyframe/static values, asserting the two disagree exactly as the screenshots show) plus the per-param index's own cache-correctness cases; 5 new `canvasPick.test.ts` cases; and a new 7-case real-DOM suite `PreviewPane.transform.dom.test.tsx` that mounts the real `PreviewPane` and reads the box's actual CSS `left`/`top`/`width`/`height` back off the DOM — asserting it equals the INTERPOLATED geometry and explicitly *not* the static geometry — then drives real `PointerEvent` drags and asserts the resulting ops (one key at the playhead carrying only the dragged params, static fields untouched; a static write still for an unkeyframed clip; the per-property split when only one axis is animated; a `box_width` override cleared on a corner drag). (2) **Five of those seven DOM cases were confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix static read** by temporarily restoring it and re-running — the two that still pass are the two about un-keyframed clips, which is the correct signal. (3) `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `reactCompiler.test.ts` still reports no bailout on the edited components (D-201). (4) **Live, partially:** a second isolated Tauri instance was built and run from this worktree (vite 1432, `CHROMA_CONTROL_PORT=19801`, its own bundle identifier) and opened a real copy of the owner's 50-keyframe project — the changed modules load and run in a real WKWebView, the Edit tab reaches `status: ready`, the playhead scrubs, and no console error or panic is logged; the real vite build also reports 114 React-Compiler bailouts repo-wide and **none** in `packages/editor/src`. The POINTER tier could not be reached (no screen-recording permission for this shell, and the `decorations: false` window exposes no Accessibility window to locate), and more fundamentally there is **no `editor_set_selection` op**, so nothing can put a clip into the state where `TransformOverlay` mounts — see D-209's "Verification" note and the roadmap item filed for it.
- **follow-up, 2026-09-08 (D-216, roadmap item 26): that last blocker is gone.** `editor_set_selection` now exists, so an agent can put a clip into the selected state and the on-canvas transform surface is drivable and checkable from MCP. The DOM tier this bug's fix was verified at is unchanged; what D-216 adds is that the NEXT on-canvas change (and a re-check of this one) no longer depends on a human clicking. The screen-recording/Accessibility limitation on the pointer tier is unrelated and still stands — `debug_screenshot` (D-210) is the answer to that half, and D-216's own live pass used exactly that combination to watch a real composited picture land **inside** the box after a transform change, which is this bug's own symptom checked directly for the first time.

## B-094 — The live-preview compositor turns a per-property keyframe animation into a STEP function: any keyframe entry that doesn't name a param silently freezes that param, so a `scale` ramp with an unrelated `opacity` key sitting between its two keys never actually ramps on screen — while the very same data exports correctly

status: fixed (2026-09-08, D-208) · severity: high (a preview that disagrees with the export for a shape the data model, the export compiler and the MCP tool all already accept — and the exact defect class B-088 was filed for; it would have made D-208's whole per-property keyframe feature lie about the picture) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`resolve_clip_transform_unfaded`), `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/keyframes.rs` (`interpolate`)
- **found:** 2026-09-08, before building D-208's per-property keyframe UI — the brief explicitly said not to assume the Rust live-preview path mirrored `timelineExport.ts`'s already-per-property `keyframeExprAt`, given B-088's recent history in this exact area. Read the resolver, predicted the divergence from the code, then pinned it with a failing test (`each_param_interpolates_across_only_its_own_keyframes`) BEFORE writing any fix or any GUI. It failed with `scale at frame 25: expected 1.25, got 1` — a real step, not a ramp.
- **repro:** any clip whose `chroma_keyframes` entries name different param subsets, which is exactly what per-property keyframing produces and what `editor_set_clip_keyframes` has always accepted. Minimal: `[{frame:0,{scale:1}}, {frame:50,{opacity:0.5}}, {frame:100,{scale:2}}]`. Scrub 0→100 in the Edit tab.
- **expected:** `scale` ramps 1→2 linearly across the whole 0..100 span, unaffected by an unrelated `opacity` key in the middle of it — the contract `timelineExport.ts` has always had (it filters every key by `hasOwnProperty(param)` before interpolating, so each param sees only its own keys) and the one an exported file really produces.
- **actual:** `scale` holds flat at 1.0 up to frame 50, then jumps and holds flat at 2.0 — a step function. The preview shows no zoom at all; the export of the identical project shows the correct smooth zoom.
- **cause:** `resolve_clip_transform_unfaded` resolved every field out of ONE call to `chroma::keyframes::interpolate`, which brackets by frame across ALL keys and then applies this module's documented rule "a field present in only one of the two bracketing keys is held from that key." For frame 25 the bracket is 0→50; only the low key names `scale`, so `scale` is *held* at 1.0. For frame 75 the bracket is 50→100; only the high key names it, so it is held at 2.0. That rule is correct for mask/relight geometry (D-034), where every key is written by one gesture and carries the same field set — it is simply not a per-property resolver, and nothing had ever fed it per-property data before.
- **fix (D-208):** a second entry point, `chroma::keyframes::interpolate_param(keyframes, frame, name)` — filter to the keys that actually define `name`, THEN bracket and lerp, holding outside that param's own range; same `lerp_value`, so `rotation`'s shortest-arc rule still applies. `resolve_clip_transform_unfaded` resolves each of its eleven fields through it. `interpolate` is deliberately left untouched and remains the mask/relight resolver: its union-and-hold rule is that path's documented, tested contract, not a bug there. Byte-identical results for any clip whose keys all carry the same params — i.e. every keyframe the pre-D-208 whole-clip button ever wrote.
- **verification:** the failing test above now passes, plus `an_unkeyed_param_keeps_its_static_value_on_a_keyframed_clip`, five new `chroma::keyframes` per-param tests (one of which pins that `interpolate` really does still differ, so the two resolvers cannot be "simplified" back into one by accident, and one proving they agree for complete keys), and — the strongest — `the_preview_really_animates_one_keyed_property_and_holds_the_rest`, a real resolve-then-composite **pixel** test through the same pair `composite_video_frame` runs per frame, sampled at frame 25 *inside the diverging bracket* (endpoints alone would have passed either way — the first draft of that test made exactly that mistake and was rewritten). Both new `chroma::edit` tests were re-confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix resolver by temporarily restoring it. `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib chroma::` 150 passed / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`chroma::project::tests::track_resolution_opaque_top_wins_across_two_video_tracks`, confirmed failing on the clean baseline too).

## B-095 — `editor_export`'s ffmpeg compiler ignores `opacity` and `rotation` completely — not just their keyframes, their STATIC values too — and animates only `position_x`/`position_y`/`scale`, so a half-transparent or rotated clip exports fully opaque and upright

status: fixed (2026-09-08, D-215) · severity: high (silent wrong output in the one tool that produces a real file: `opacity` and `rotation` are first-class `Clip` fields, real Inspector controls, real `editor_set_clip_transform` params and — as of D-208 — properties the GUI now shows a keyframe stopwatch for, and none of that reached an exported file) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildClipFilterChain`)
- **found:** 2026-09-08, while auditing the export compiler's per-param keyframe coverage for D-208 (to confirm which properties the new per-property diamonds could honestly claim to animate). `grep` for `opacity`/`rotation` in `timelineExport.ts` returned **nothing at all** — they were not in the filter chain in any form, static or animated. Only `position_x`/`position_y` (`positionExpr`) and `scale` (`scaleExpr`, added by B-090) built keyframe expressions; the four crop insets were read but only as static values.
- **repro (pre-fix):** set a clip's `opacity` to 0.5 (or `rotation` to 45) in the Inspector — the live preview honours it, `chroma_timeline_get` reports it — then `editor_export`. The output file showed the clip fully opaque and unrotated. Keyframing either property (one diamond click, D-208) made no difference: the animation was absent from the export for the same reason the static value was.
- **expected:** every transform property the live preview composites and the Inspector exposes reaches the export, statically at minimum, and animates when keyframed — the standing "same doc ⇒ same picture" expectation, and the same bar B-090 was fixed to.
- **cause:** the two fields were simply never implemented in this compiler. D-196's own FCPXML interchange table had already noted it in passing for rotation ("MORE complete than `timelineExport.ts`'s own ffmpeg compiler, which never implemented rotation at all") without a bug entry of its own.
- **fix (D-215):** `buildClipFilterChain` now emits, only when a clip actually needs them (identity-preserving — a clip untouched by either field compiles byte-identically to before):
  - `format=rgba` (both effects below need a real alpha plane a plain decoded frame doesn't have),
  - `rotate=angle='(<deg-expr>)*PI/180':fillcolor=black@0.0` for `rotation` (static or keyframed; verified empirically against the Rust compositor's own `imageproc::rotate_about_center` that the two agree on both sign/direction and on keeping the original frame's own dimensions — no negation, no `ow`/`oh` needed),
  - `geq=r='r(X,Y)':g='g(X,Y)':b='b(X,Y)':a='alpha(X,Y)*(<alpha-expr>)'` for `opacity` — **not** `colorchannelmixer=aa=<expr>`, the shape this bug originally suggested: verified empirically that `colorchannelmixer`'s options flatly reject a `t`-based expression ("Undefined constant... in 't,...'"), unlike `scale`/`crop`/`rotate`. `geq` is the filter that actually supports a time-varying per-pixel expression, and its time variable is spelled `T` (uppercase) — also a real trap, since every other filter here uses lowercase `t`.
  - The alpha expression is `opacity × fade`, mirroring `resolve_clip_transform`'s own composition (D-147) and `buildTextDrawtextStep`'s existing `alphaExpr` exactly — this **also** fixes a previously-unfixed adjacent gap: a VIDEO clip's own fade handle (D-207, real in the GUI on every clip, video or audio) only ever reached the exported EMBEDDED AUDIO, never the picture, since there was no picture-opacity filter step of any kind to fold it into.
  - `eval=frame`/no-eval-needed established empirically per filter, not assumed: `rotate`'s `angle` and `geq`'s `alpha_expr` both re-evaluate every frame with no extra flag (unlike `scale`/`crop`, whose default `eval=init` is the B-090 trap).
- **verification:** three new real-ffmpeg pixel tests in `timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts` (mirroring B-090's own methodology — a decoded pixel, not a string match): a keyframed opacity ramp sampled near-full and near-zero; a static 90° rotation moving an asymmetric top-left marker to top-right (the exact direction confirmed empirically first, not assumed); a video clip's fade-in handle sampled dark-then-lit. `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor`: 694/694 (was 691 pre-fix, +3; every pre-existing test, including the pure string-match `timelineExport.test.ts` suite, is unchanged — confirming the identity-preserving claim above for every clip that doesn't touch either field). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean.
- **still open, deliberately not folded in here:** crop's four insets remain STATIC-only in export — `hasKeyframesFor(clip, 'crop_left')` etc. is never checked, so a keyframed crop animates in the live preview (`resolve_clip_transform` resolves it) but exports frozen at whichever value the clip's base fields hold. Filed separately as B-098 rather than absorbed, matching this bug's own original discipline (it was filed apart from D-208 for the identical reason) — same `eval=frame`-on-a-time-varying-`crop=` shape `scaleExpr` and this fix's own `rotate=` already established, next in line to reuse it.

## B-068 — The solo-scene transport bar (D-181) let a click "see through" it to select/drag the canvas underneath
status: fixed (2026-09-06, D-182) · severity: medium (silent wrong-target mutation — a click meant for the transport moved/selected canvas content instead, with no visible error) · area: `packages/motion/src/MotionCanvasOverlay.tsx`, `packages/motion/src/MotionPreview.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-06, live, while testing D-182's own per-card drag — clicking the D-181 transport's scrubber unexpectedly selected and moved the whole `layers` stack rendered behind it.
- **repro:** solo a scene (D-181), click anywhere on the custom transport bar at the bottom of the preview.
- **expected:** the transport's own control (scrubber/play button) responds; nothing on the canvas is touched.
- **actual:** the click "saw through" the transport bar to the canvas layer rendered at that exact pixel, selecting AND dragging it (a zero-delta drag, since the pointer never moved, but still committed via the existing "always commit on pointerup" convention).
- **cause:** `MotionCanvasOverlay.tsx`'s hit-tests (`findLayer`/`findLayerItem`) deliberately walk the WHOLE `elementsFromPoint` stack at a point (needed to see through `data-motion-layer`'s own `display:contents` wrapper) — a side effect nothing exercised before D-181 put an OPAQUE sibling on top of the canvas for the first time.
- **fix:** a `data-motion-transport` marker on the transport bar (`MotionPreview.tsx`) and one check at the top of `onPointerDown` — `e.target.closest('[data-motion-transport]')` bails before any hit-testing. `e.target` (real stacking-aware pointer target), not `elementsFromPoint`, is the right tool for "was this actually our own chrome."
- **verification:** live, in `app/motion-harness.html` — reproduced the bug pre-fix (clicking the scrubber selected the layer, manifest went dirty), confirmed fixed post-fix (clicking the scrubber only moves the scrubber, no selection, no dirty flag).

## B-077 — The Edit-tab GUI (transport bar, filmstrip/waveform width, gap detection, ripple/insert math) mis-converts a clip's `duration`/`source_start` at the WRONG frame rate — the same B-075 bug, everywhere else it appears
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-194) · severity: high (every displayed clip/timeline duration, gap boundary, insertion snap, and ripple-shift amount was silently wrong for any clip whose native rate differs from the project's `timelineFps` — B-075 only fixed the export compiler, not the live GUI these numbers actually come from) · area: `packages/editor/src/timeline.ts` (`endFrame` and everything that calls it), `packages/editor/src/TimelinePane.tsx`, `packages/editor/src/marquee.ts`
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, a real project with `timelineFps()` defaulting to 24 (unset `rate`) and one clip at 2113 native frames @ 44.128089105464674fps (real duration 47.86s) — the GUI's transport bar displayed **00:01:28:00** (88s), exactly `2113 / 24`, nothing to do with the clip's real length.
- **repro:** put a clip whose `source_fps` differs from the timeline's own `timelineFps()` on the timeline; read its displayed duration, or the timeline's own total duration, or drop/drag a clip near it.
- **expected:** every displayed/derived duration reflects the clip's REAL length (`duration` source frames ÷ its own `source_fps`, converted to the project's timeline-frame grid), matching what `timelineExport.ts` already got right after B-075.
- **actual:** `endFrame(c) { return c.start_frame + c.duration; }` added `start_frame` (timeline frames) and `duration` (source frames, per `chroma-timeline::Clip`'s own doc) directly, with no fps conversion — correct only when a clip's native rate happens to equal the project's, which is exactly why ordinary same-fps footage never caught this. Nearly every position-deriving function in `timeline.ts` routes through `endFrame` (gap detection, insertion snapping, ripple-shift amounts, sync-lock straddle checks, the trim clamps) and inherited the same wrongness; `TimelinePane.tsx` additionally divided `clip.duration`/`clip.source_start` by the single project-wide `fps` directly (not the clip's own `source_fps`) for filmstrip/waveform fetch ranges and drag-preview widths.
- **cause:** B-075 (D-187, same session) added `Clip.source_fps` and used it correctly, but scoped the fix to `timelineExport.ts` alone (the only place a real ffmpeg failure had surfaced it) — its own bug entry explicitly flagged "everywhere else it appears" as still open. Nothing else in the codebase had been re-audited against the field once it existed.
- **fix:** `endFrame(c, fps)` now converts `c.duration` through `c.source_fps ?? fps` before adding it to `start_frame`; `fps` (the project's own `timelineFps(tl)`) threads into every function that combines a `start_frame`-space position with a `duration`/`source_start`-space quantity — `trackDuration`, `timelineDuration`, `computeInsertion`/`resolveClipLanding` (now take the real clip, not a bare duration number), `nextAppendFrame`, `gapAt`, `clipAt`, `findStraddlingSyncLockedTrack`, `collectSyncLinkedClips`, `syncLinkedClipIds[AtPosition]`, the two trim clamps, and `applyOp`'s `add_clip`/`move`/`trim_start`/`trim_end`/`split`/`remove_gap` cases. `trim_start`/`split` needed a real split (not just an fps parameter): the UI's own drag delta/offset is timeline-native, applied to `start_frame` unconverted but to `source_start`/`duration` via its source-frame equivalent — the pre-fix code applied the SAME raw delta to all three. `TimelinePane.tsx`'s filmstrip/waveform/drag-overlay props switched from `/ fps` to `/ (clip.source_fps ?? fps)`. See D-194 for the full design writeup (why "convert at every consumption site," matching B-075, was chosen over "normalize duration to timeline frames at clip-creation time") and the real persistence gap this pass also closed (`Clip.source_fps` didn't exist on the Rust struct, so it silently vanished on every `chroma_timeline_set`/`_get` round trip — B-075's fix would have stopped working after the first save of any session).
- **verification:** `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` — 335/335 (was 324; 11 new tests in a `B-077 — mixed native-fps clips` block, pinning the exact live repro numbers and a clean 48fps-on-24fps ratio for `add_clip`/`move` ripple, `computeInsertion`/`gapAt`/`clipAt`, and `trim_start`/`trim_end`/`split`). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean. `cargo test -p chroma-timeline` 129/129 (128 pre-existing + 1 new, pinning `source_fps`'s serde round trip).

## B-079 — The live Rust playback/preview engine (`Track::clip_at`, `clip_spans_from`, `Track::duration`, and `chroma::audio.rs`'s inline position math) has the SAME `duration`/`start_frame` unit conflation as B-075/B-077 — found during that audit, not fixed
status: fixed (2026-09-07, D-200) · severity: medium-high (a mixed-native-fps clip's actual video scrub/preview frame, audio decode length, duck-envelope timing, and the timeline-switcher's displayed duration were all wrong, independently of B-077's GUI-edit-model fix — a real, reachable defect on the live playback path, not a hypothetical — plausibly the root cause, or a contributing cause, of the SAME session's separately-reported "live preview shows the AFTER clip full-bleed instead of stacked with BEFORE" — see D-199/`docs/notes/preview-canvas-boundary.md`) · area: `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs` (`Track::clip_at`, `Track::clip_spans_from`, `Track::duration`, `Clip::end_frame`), `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`chroma_timeline_frame`/`timeline_frame`, `resolve_track_duck`, `resolve_audio_track_positions`), `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs` (`chroma_audio_play`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, during the B-077 audit — checking "does the live Rust preview/scrubbing path need the same fix" (as instructed) surfaced that it does, and that the defect is broader than just the preview: `Track::clip_at(&self, timeline_frame: i64) -> Option<(&Clip, i64)>` computes `(c, c.source_start + (timeline_frame - c.start_frame))` — the identical B-075/B-077 conflation, with no `source_fps` conversion — and is used by BOTH `chroma_timeline_frame`'s video-preview decode path (`resolve_visible_video_layers_at`/`resolve_video_clip_at`) AND `chroma::audio.rs`'s own audio-clip-at-playhead lookup (`chroma_audio_play`, line ~288). `Track::clip_spans_from` (the duck-envelope trigger boundaries) and `Track::duration` (the `chroma_timeline_list`/`TimelineSummary` display the timeline switcher shows) both call `Clip::end_frame()` — same plain `start_frame + duration` addition. `chroma::audio.rs` additionally REIMPLEMENTS the same math inline twice more (`chroma_audio_play`'s embedded-audio and audio-track branches: `source_frame = clip.source_start + elapsed_frames`, `remaining_frames = clip.end_frame() - start_frame`) rather than going through `Track::clip_at`.
- **repro:** play/scrub a timeline with a clip whose `source_fps` differs from the project's; the decoded video frame, the audio actually heard, its fade/duck timing, and the switcher's displayed duration for that timeline are all computed from the unconverted, wrong `source_frame`/`remaining_frames`.
