- **expected:** the same fps-aware conversion B-077 gave the GUI/MCP edit model (`source_fps ?? timeline_fps`) applied to every one of these Rust-side position lookups.
- **cause:** this Rust crate's own frame-resolution methods predate `source_fps` entirely (it didn't exist until B-075) and were never revisited once it did — B-077's audit found them but they are OUT OF SCOPE for that pass: unlike the Rust crate's `trim_start`/`trim_end`/`split`/`move_clip` (confirmed dead code — every real edit, GUI or MCP, goes through `@chroma/editor`'s `applyOp` + `chroma_timeline_set`, never these Rust ops directly), `clip_at`/`clip_spans_from`/`Track::duration` ARE on the live path, so this is a real bug, not a documentation nit — it is being filed rather than fixed in the same pass because fixing it correctly means threading a new `timeline_fps: f64` parameter through methods with ~30 existing Rust unit-test call sites, touches the real-time audio mixer's actual sound output (a much higher-stakes surface to change under the same pass as an unrelated GUI fix), and deserves its own focused verification (a real audio/video output diff, not just unit assertions) rather than a rushed addition here.
- **fix (D-200):** added `Timeline::fps(&self) -> f64` (mirrors `timeline.ts`'s
  `timelineFps` — `rate.num/rate.den` when both positive, else a new
  `DEFAULT_FPS: f64 = 24.0`) and `source_frames_to_timeline`/
  `timeline_frames_to_source` (mirror `sourceFramesToTimeline`/
  `timelineFramesToSource` exactly — same formula, same rounding). Added
  `Clip::end_frame_at(&self, fps: f64) -> i64` alongside the existing plain
  `end_frame()` (kept, unchanged, re-documented as "for this crate's own
  confirmed-dead editing ops only"). `Track::duration`/`clip_at`/`clip_spans_from`
  all gained an `fps: f64` parameter and route through the new fps-aware
  arithmetic; `Timeline::duration`/`resolve_video_clip_at`/
  `resolve_visible_video_layers_at` compute `self.fps()` once and thread it
  through, with unchanged PUBLIC signatures. `Track::gap_at` (the dead
  `remove_gap` op's own lookup) is deliberately left fps-naive, paired with the
  unchanged `end_frame()`. `resolve_audio_track_positions` now returns the
  SOURCE FRAME `Track::clip_at` already resolves correctly instead of discarding
  it — its caller, `chroma_audio_play`, used to silently RE-DERIVE it with a
  second, fps-naive `clip.source_start + elapsed_frames` formula, which is
  deleted in favor of the resolver's own (now correct) value. Both
  `remaining_frames` computations in `chroma_audio_play` (embedded-video-audio
  and audio-track) now use `end_frame_at(fps)`, `fps` from a new
  `chroma::edit::timeline_fps()` helper. `resolve_track_duck`'s
  `clip_spans_from` call is fps-corrected too. One incidental call-site fix:
  `crates/chroma-project/src/manifest.rs`'s `append_media_clip` (Colorist's "add
  to grading" convenience) needed `Track::duration`'s new `fps` argument.
- **a TS mirror, fixed in the SAME change as instructed by this entry's own prior
  note:** `packages/editor/src/clipKeyframes.ts::clipSourceFrame` now takes a
  `fps: number` parameter and converts via `timelineFramesToSource` (B-077),
  exactly mirroring `Track::clip_at`'s now-fixed formula — fixed together with
  the Rust half specifically to avoid the authoring/playback desync this entry
  warned a TS-only fix would cause. `EditorInspectorPanel.tsx` (the only caller)
  passes `timelineFps(timeline)`.
- **verification:** `cargo test -p chroma-timeline` 137/137 (129 pre-existing + 8
  new, a `b079_mixed_native_fps` test module — `end_frame_at` conversion/
  identity/fallback, `Track::clip_at`/`duration`/`clip_spans_from` at a clean
  48fps-on-24fps ratio, `Timeline::fps`'s rate-field/malformed/default cases,
  `resolve_visible_video_layers_at` resolving a mixed-fps layer end to end).
  `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib` 146/146, `cargo test -p chroma-project` 52/52 —
  every existing fixture in both suites is same-native-fps, where the fix is a
  strict identity, so no existing expected number changed. `cargo clippy -p
  chroma-timeline -p RapidRAW --all-targets -D warnings` clean; `chroma-project`
  has 15 PRE-EXISTING clippy errors (confirmed via `git stash` present in
  identical count/location on `main`, none touching the one line this pass's
  `append_media_clip` fix changed) — left alone, not folded into this fix's
  diff. `cargo fmt --check` confirmed clean on every line this pass added or
  changed (pre-existing, unrelated drift elsewhere in the same files, confirmed
  present on `main` before this branch, deliberately
  left alone — same discipline D-193's own verification note used).
  `packages/editor`: `clipKeyframes.test.ts`'s new "B-079 — mixed native-fps
  clips" block (3 tests) + the whole suite at 360/360, `tsc` clean. See D-200
  for the complete design writeup.
- **roadmap:** `docs/04-roadmap.md` item 19, closed.

## B-080 — Switching or creating a timeline while an edit's debounced save is still pending silently drops that edit — never reaches disk, never reachable again in memory either

status: open · severity: high (silent data loss on the Edit tab's own multi-timeline feature — no error, no warning, the app looks completely normal both before and after) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineStore.ts` (`applyOp`'s debounced `_flushSave`/`saveTimer`, `createTimeline`, `setActiveTimeline`)
- **found:** 2026-09-07, live, during D-195 Task 3's own multiple-timelines verification (`docs/notes/timeline-editing-feature-gap-analysis.md`'s "needs live verification" item) — extended the D-142 browser harness (`app/harness.html`+`app/src/harness-main.tsx`) to mount `TimelineSwitcher` alongside `TimelinePane` with a real in-memory multi-timeline fake backend (mirroring `chroma::edit`'s `chroma_timeline_list`/`_create`/`_set_active`/`_get`/`_set` exactly, including `_set`'s real "always writes onto whichever timeline is ACTIVE, never by matching the posted timeline's own id" behavior) and drove it with real Chromium `PointerEvent`s via chrome-devtools MCP.
- **repro (exact, reproduced live in the harness):** on timeline A (active), call `applyOp` with any edit (e.g. `add_track`) — this schedules `_flushSave` `SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS` (400ms) later via the module-level `saveTimer`. Before that timer fires, switch the active timeline to B (`setActiveTimeline`, e.g. clicking another tab) or create a new one (`createTimeline`, e.g. the "+" tab). Wait past 400ms. Timeline A's edit is gone — not on disk (the fake backend, mirroring the real `chroma_timeline_set`, never received it), and not recoverable from memory either (the store's `timeline` field has already been overwritten by B's own content via `setActiveTimeline`'s `load()`). Confirmed both ways: A's track count reverts to its pre-edit count, and B is untouched (not corrupted with A's stale edit either) — the edit is simply discarded, nowhere.
- **expected:** an edit made just before switching timelines either (a) is flushed to disk before the switch proceeds, or (b) if that's not chosen, at minimum the app warns / the switch waits — never a silent, un-flagged loss. The **happy path** (switch timelines with >400ms since the last edit) is unaffected and verified correct: `TimelineSwitcher`'s "+" tab, tab-click switching, and both timelines' own independent content (persistence AND non-bleed) all work exactly as designed once the debounce window has already elapsed — this bug is specifically about the RACE, not the feature's basic correctness.
- **cause:** `applyOp` (`timelineStore.ts`) debounces its own `_flushSave()` (`invoke('chroma_timeline_set', {timeline: get().timeline})`) by `SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS` = 400ms via a single module-level `saveTimer`, deliberately (a documented perf fix, see the surrounding comment: "a full `chroma_timeline_get` refetch after every single debounced save... was pure redundant latency"). `restoreSnapshot` (undo/redo) is careful to `clearTimeout(saveTimer)` before restoring a snapshot, precisely because it knows a stale pending save could otherwise fire against the wrong state — but `createTimeline`/`setActiveTimeline` have NO such handling at all: they call `chroma_timeline_create`/`chroma_timeline_set_active` then `load()` (which overwrites `get().timeline` with the NEWLY active timeline's own fetched content) with the OLD timeline's debounce timer left running untouched. When that stale timer eventually fires, `_flushSave` reads `get().timeline` AT FIRE TIME (not at schedule time) — by then it's already B's content — and posts `chroma_timeline_set({timeline: B's own already-current content})`, which the real Rust command (and this bug's own fake-backend mirror) writes onto whichever timeline is CURRENTLY ACTIVE (B, by definition, since nothing switched again) — a harmless no-op FOR B, but A's real edit was never sent anywhere and no longer exists in memory to retry.
- **the same race exists on the PROJECT axis, and B-083/D-203 only made it deterministic, not fixed** (2026-09-07): switching *projects* has the identical pending-save hazard, with a worse worst case — by the time `setOpenProject` hears about the switch, the Rust side already points at the INCOMING project, so a surviving `saveTimer` would have written the outgoing project's timeline into the incoming one. `setOpenProject` therefore cancels the timer outright, which is the safe half of the trade: an edit made in the last `SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS` before a project switch is still silently dropped, exactly as described above. Closing it properly (flush before the switch proceeds) is still this entry's own scoped fix, and should cover both axes when it lands.
- **fix:** not attempted here — this is exactly the discipline B-079 (this same file) used: found during a live-verification audit, filed precisely with an exact repro, not folded into an unrelated task's pass. Proposed shape: `createTimeline`/`setActiveTimeline` should mirror `restoreSnapshot`'s own care — synchronously flush any pending save (call `get()._flushSave()` and `clearTimeout(saveTimer)`, or better, `await` a real flush that resolves after `chroma_timeline_set` actually returns) BEFORE invoking `chroma_timeline_create`/`chroma_timeline_set_active`. Needs its own scoped pass: there's a real UX judgment call in whether the tab switch should visibly wait on that flush (a spinner / brief disabled state) or fire-and-continue optimistically, and it deserves verification against the real Rust backend (not just the harness's fake one), not a rushed one-line addition.
- **harness note:** while extending `app/src/harness-main.tsx`/`app/harness.html` for this verification, also fixed a real usability defect in the harness itself (not a product bug): `#harness-status`'s fixed, `z-index: 9999` debug overlay was pinned `top: 0; left: 0`, directly on top of `TimelineSwitcher`'s own left-anchored tab strip (also mounted at the very top) — a real CDP pointer click at those screen coordinates silently landed on the (click-inert) status badge instead of the tab underneath it, even though the accessibility tree still reported the tab as a normal, clickable element. Moved the badge to `top: 0; right: 0` (nothing else docks there). Worth remembering for the next feature that adds UI to the very top of this harness's `#root`.

## B-096 — The Edit tab's own preview/timeline split was a fixed `h-[46%]` flex row, not a real resizable panel — a direct violation of this repo's own standing rule

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: low (display/UX only — the timeline area worked correctly, it just could not be resized) · area: `packages/editor/src/EditorTab.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-08, owner live, reporting the Inspector panel's content cut off against the window edge and, separately with a screenshot, "the timeline panel is also not resizable" — while auditing the first report, found the SECOND is real too: `EditorTab.tsx`'s preview/timeline split was `<div className="h-[46%] min-h-[180px] shrink-0">`, a fixed CSS percentage, not a `ResizablePanel` at all — while the SAME file's Inspector column (to its right) already correctly uses one. CLAUDE.md's own standing rule ("every resizable-by-nature panel/pane/sidebar must actually be resizable," 2026-09-03) names exactly this class of pane and says a fixed one found later is "a real, worthwhile fix ... not something to leave as-is out of inertia."
- **repro:** open the Edit tab with a project loaded — there is no drag handle between the preview and the timeline; the split is permanently 46%/54% of the remaining height regardless of window size or user preference.
- **expected:** a real, user-draggable vertical split, matching the Inspector column's own horizontal one, and matching `packages/motion/src/MotionTab.tsx`'s own preview/timeline split, which already uses exactly this pattern correctly.
- **fix:** wrapped the preview and timeline blocks in a nested `<ResizablePanelGroup orientation="vertical">` (same `@chroma/ui` component, same pixel-based `defaultSize`/`minSize` convention this file already uses for the Inspector column and `MotionTab.tsx` already uses for its own vertical split) — `PREVIEW_MIN_HEIGHT = 200`, `TIMELINE_DEFAULT_HEIGHT = 320`, `TIMELINE_MIN_HEIGHT = 180` (matching the old fixed min-height so nothing gets more cramped than before). No new component, no new pattern — the exact one already proven elsewhere in this same codebase.
- **verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean; `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor` 610/610 (no regressions — this is a pure layout change, no test targets the exact pixel split). Not live-verified against the real running app (no visual-inspection tool available yet at fix time — see `docs/notes/debug-tooling.md`, in progress); high confidence from the pattern being a verbatim, already-shipped-and-working match to `MotionTab.tsx`'s own equivalent split, but flagging the gap honestly rather than claiming a look that didn't happen.

## B-097 — `Track::clip_at`'s resolved source frame is off by one at a clip's own first frame after a top-track gap ends, and has been since the track existed at all

status: open, pre-existing, low priority (never independently reported by the owner; only ever surfaced as noisy `cargo test` output during unrelated verification passes) · severity: low (one-frame source-frame drift at a specific boundary condition — a top-gap-fallthrough position — not a crash, not data loss, not visible in the GUI at normal playback speed) · area: `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs` (`Track::clip_at`, called by `Timeline::resolve_video_clip_at` → `chroma::edit::resolve_video_position`)
- **found:** independently reconfirmed 3 times across this session's merge-verification `cargo test` runs (each time as a pre-existing, unrelated failure alongside real work being verified), traced but never filed with its own entry until now.
- **repro:** `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib chroma::project::tests::track_resolution_opaque_top_wins_across_two_video_tracks` — deterministically fails today:
  ```
  thread '...' panicked at app/src-tauri/src/chroma/project.rs:1577:9:
  assertion `left == right` failed: B's own source frame at this timeline position (it starts at 0)
    left: 26
   right: 25
  ```
  The test: track 0 has clip A (~25 frames) then nothing; track 1 has clip B (~75 frames) starting at timeline frame 0. Querying `resolve_video_position(dur_a)` — the first timeline frame where track 0's gap begins and track 1 (starting at frame 0) should show through — correctly picks clip B (the track-priority/fallthrough logic itself is right), but reports B's own **source frame** as `dur_a + 1` instead of `dur_a`.
- **expected:** since clip B starts at timeline frame 0 with no trim (`source_start = 0`) and (per the test's own guard assertion at line 1547) probes at the same fps as the project timeline, `Track::clip_at`'s source-frame formula (`c.source_start + timeline_frames_to_source(c.source_fps, timeline_frame - c.start_frame, fps)`, `crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs:2258`) should reduce to plain identity — the source frame at timeline position `dur_a` should just be `dur_a`.
- **cause:** not fully re-derived this session (the mechanism was traced once already and not written down anywhere durable — this entry exists to close that gap). `timeline_frames_to_source`'s own formula (`crates/chroma-timeline/src/lib.rs:141-144`, `round(timeline_frames * src_fps / fps)`) is an exact identity when `src_fps == fps` and `timeline_frames` is an integer — no floating-point rounding can produce a whole-number-off-by-one here. That points at one of the two inputs to the subtraction (`timeline_frame - c.start_frame`) being off by one instead: either clip B's `start_frame` lands at `-1` rather than `0` after `chroma_timeline_move_clip(0, 1, 1, 0, false)` (the move that puts it on track 1), or `Timeline::resolve_video_clip_at`/the test itself passes `dur_a` when the fallthrough boundary is actually meant to be evaluated one frame later (an inclusive/exclusive fencepost in `end_frame_at`). Previously traced to predate every change from this session, in the original `Multi-track NLE Phase B1` commit that introduced `resolve_video_clip_at` (`ffb23d2`, 2026-09-03, D-056) — i.e. this is not a regression from anything shipped tonight, it has been latent since two-video-track resolution first existed, just never exercised by a GUI user landing exactly on that boundary frame at a moment they'd notice a one-frame source drift.
- **fix:** not attempted here — filing this properly (rather than continuing to let it show up as unexplained noise in unrelated `cargo test` runs) is the scoped task; the actual fix needs the mechanism above confirmed with a debug print or `dbg!()` at `Track::clip_at`'s two inputs before touching the arithmetic, since two different fencepost directions are exact matches for a `+1` off-by-one and only one is real.
- **verification:** the repro above is the verification — deterministic, no ffmpeg/timing flakiness (skips cleanly without ffmpeg on `PATH`, per the test's own guard).

## B-098 — `editor_export`'s ffmpeg compiler still crops with STATIC insets only — a keyframed crop animates in the live preview but exports frozen

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: medium (a real, narrower instance of B-095's own shape — the live preview and the export disagreed, just for one field instead of two) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildClipFilterChain`)
- **found:** 2026-09-08, while fixing B-095 (opacity/rotation). `buildClipFilterChain`'s crop step read `clip.crop_left`/`crop_top`/`crop_right`/`crop_bottom` as four plain static numbers (`const cl = clip.crop_left ?? 0`, etc.) and never called `hasKeyframesFor(clip, 'crop_left')` or built a `keyframeExprAt` expression for any of them — unlike `position_x`/`position_y`/`scale`/`opacity`/`rotation`, which all check for and honour keyframes. The Rust preview side (`resolve_clip_transform_unfaded`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs`) already resolves all four crop insets through the same per-property keyframe engine (D-132/D-208) as everything else — so this was purely an export-compiler gap, not a missing model capability.
- **repro (pre-fix):** keyframe any of the four crop insets on a clip (`editor_set_clip_keyframes`) to animate a reveal/hide over time — the live preview animated it correctly; `editor_export` produced a file cropped at whichever value the clip's own static `crop_*` fields held, for the clip's entire duration.
- **expected:** same bar as B-095 — a keyframed crop inset exports animated, matching the live preview frame for frame.
- **fix:** each of the four insets now gets the same `hasKeyframesFor` + `rebaseKeyframesToClipInput` + `keyframeExprAt` treatment `scale`/`opacity`/`rotation` already have, wired into `crop`'s `w`/`h`/`x`/`y` as quoted expressions (same B-075 single-quoting requirement). **This entry's own original fix guidance was wrong, corrected here rather than silently**: it assumed `crop` needed an `eval=frame` flag the way `scale` does (B-090's own trap) — checked empirically instead of assumed, and `crop` has **no `eval` option at all** (`ffmpeg -h filter=crop` lists none): a real two-colour test (an `hstack` source, an animated `x` expression) confirmed `crop`'s `w`/`h`/`x`/`y` are simply always evaluated per frame, with nothing to add.
- **verification:** a new real-ffmpeg pixel test in `timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts` — a `crop_left` keyframe animating 0 → 0.3 across a clip with a marker in its leftmost quarter, sampled visible-then-gone. The pre-existing static-crop string-match test in `timelineExport.test.ts` was updated to the new quoted/named-param shape (same "not weakened, just corrected to the new-but-equivalent syntax" discipline B-090's own verification note used) rather than left asserting the old, now-superseded unquoted form. `npm test --workspace @chroma/editor`: 718/718 (was 717 pre-fix, +1 new pixel test — the string-match update doesn't add a test, it corrects an existing one). `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/editor` clean.

## B-100 — the debug screenshot tool shipped ungated: `chroma_debug_screenshot` / `chroma_debug_sample_pixel` were compiled into a RELEASE build

status: fixed (2026-09-08, D-219) · severity: medium (no user-facing symptom and no exploitable surface — the commands only write a PNG of the app's own window under `$TMPDIR` — but a direct violation of the project's own stated invariant, and the pattern was about to be copied by four more tools) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/{mod.rs,control.rs}`, `app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `app/src/hooks/useDebugScreenshot.ts`
- **found:** 2026-09-08, while reading D-210's implementation as the reference pattern for D-219's own tools. The brief was to "match whatever gate the already-shipped commands use." They used none.
- **repro:** `grep -rn "debug_assertions" app/src-tauri/src/` on the pre-fix tree returns two hits, neither of them debug tooling (`main.rs`'s `windows_subsystem`, and a *comment* in `lib.rs` about the `e2e-testing` Cargo feature). `chroma::debug_capture` was an unconditional `pub mod`; both commands were unconditional entries in `tauri::generate_handler!`; both `native_op` arms in `control.rs` were unconditional; and the human-facing Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+D hook invoked the command unconditionally too.
- **expected:** per CLAUDE.md's standing rule (owner, 2026-09-08) — "every one of them must be compiled out of a production build via a real compile-time gate (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`, a Cargo feature, or equivalent) — never a runtime flag, never 'just don't call it in prod.' For us, never shipped." — a `tauri build` release binary contains no webview-screenshot code path.
- **actual:** it contained all of it. A shipped Chroma could be asked over IPC to photograph its own window and write the PNG to disk.
- **cause:** D-210's own decision entry never states a gate, and neither does `docs/notes/debug-screenshot-tool.md`. The rule and the tool landed on the same day; the rule was written in CLAUDE.md, the tool was written without it. Nothing in the build would ever have complained — an ungated debug command compiles and works perfectly, which is exactly why this class of miss survives review.
- **fix (D-219):** `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` on `chroma::debug_capture` (the whole module), on both commands *inside* `tauri::generate_handler!` (the macro re-emits an outer attribute onto that command's match arm — verified in `tauri-macros` 2.6.3's `command/handler.rs`, so the IPC name genuinely does not exist in a release build), and on `control.rs`'s `native_op`, which gains a `None`-returning `#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]` twin so its single call site reads identically either way. The frontend half (the Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+D hook) is gated on `import.meta.env.DEV`, which Vite folds to a literal `false` at build time — otherwise the shortcut would survive into a release build and only ever toast "command not found". D-219's own five new tools use the same two gates from the start, and the frontend one is verified by grepping a real production `vite build` output rather than assumed.

## B-099 — D-217 broke the real-Chromium browser harness outright: `atob` on a string that still carried its `data:` prefix, so `harness.html` mounted nothing at all

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: medium (no shipped-app impact whatsoever — this is debug tooling; but it silently removed a whole VERIFICATION TIER, which is exactly the class of failure this repo keeps paying for) · area: `app/src/harness-main.tsx`
- **found:** 2026-09-08, on the first attempt to use the harness since D-217 — opening `harness.html?mode=preview` to verify D-218's preview zoom in a real browser. The page rendered its status strip and nothing else; `window.__chromaHarness` was `undefined`.
- **repro:** serve `app/` (`npm run dev`) and open `/harness.html?mode=preview`. Console: `Uncaught InvalidCharacterError: Failed to execute 'atob' on 'Window': The string to be decoded is not correctly encoded.` Nothing mounts, so every check the harness exists to perform is silently unavailable.
- **cause:** D-217 changed `chroma_timeline_frame`'s payload from a `data:image/jpeg;base64,…` string to raw bytes, and correctly updated the harness's stub to hand back bytes — `Uint8Array.from(atob(STUB_FRAME_BASE64), …)`. But `STUB_FRAME_BASE64` was (and had always been) the whole **data URL**, prefix included; it was previously handed to an `<img src>` verbatim, where the prefix belongs. `atob` on `"data:image/jpeg;base64,/9j/…"` throws, at module scope, before `render()` is ever reached — so the failure is total rather than a broken picture, which is why it read as "the harness is dead" rather than "the stub frame is wrong."
- **expected:** the harness page mounts, exactly as D-142 built it to, and its stub frame arrives as the bytes the real command now returns.
- **fix:** the constant is now the bare base64 payload its own name always claimed (prefix removed), with a comment at the declaration saying why it must stay that way. One line of real change.
- **verification:** the page mounts and `window.__chromaHarness` is live again — and it was then used for real, as D-218's whole real-browser verification tier (real layout, real `document.elementFromPoint` hit-testing, real `PointerEvent` drag, two screenshots). Only remaining console entry on the page is a `favicon.ico` 404, pre-existing and unrelated.
- **worth noting for next time:** the harness has no automated coverage of its own (it deliberately does not run in CI — `harness-main.tsx`'s own header says so), so nothing could have caught this except opening it. The cheap habit that would have: when a change alters a stub's own contract, open the harness once in the same pass rather than trusting the edit.

## B-101 — A panned MONO clip exports 3 dB quieter than it plays: the two paths adapt mono→stereo by different laws

status: open, deliberately (found and measured while building D-223; the fix belongs to the mono-adaptation layer, not to the pan feature) · severity: low (mono sources only, and only once a pan is set — stereo sources, the normal case, are bit-for-bit unaffected) · area: `crates/chroma-media/src/audio.rs` (`adapt_channels`) vs `packages/editor/src/timelineExportAudio.ts` (`buildAudioSourceChain`'s `aformat`)
- **found:** 2026-09-08, while building D-223's export half. Not a guess — measured directly with `ffmpeg -af volumedetect` on real files before the code was written, precisely because the mono case is where a channel-adaptation difference would hide.
- **repro:** `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i "sine=frequency=440:duration=2" -ac 1 mono.wav` measures `mean_volume: -21.1 dB`. Push it through the exporter's own upmix — `-filter_complex "[0:a]aformat=channel_layouts=stereo"` — and each channel measures `-24.1 dB`. The live mixer's `adapt_channels` instead does `out.extend(repeat_n(frame[0], dst_channels))`, i.e. each output channel carries the mono sample unchanged, `-21.1 dB`.
- **expected:** the same clip at the same pan sounds the same in the preview and in the exported file — the "same doc ⇒ same output" bar this repo holds every render/audio path to (the standard B-053/B-095 were fixed against).
- **actual:** an exported panned mono clip is 3.01 dB below what the preview played. Only reachable with `pan != 0`: with no pan the exporter never inserts the `aformat` node at all, so a mono clip stays mono end to end and nothing rematrixes.
- **cause:** two different, individually defensible mono→stereo laws. `adapt_channels` **duplicates at unity** (each speaker gets the full sample, so total power doubles — +3 dB). libswresample's default mono→stereo matrix **preserves power** (each channel at 1/√2). Neither is wrong on its own; they simply disagree, and D-223's pan is the first feature that makes both run over the same clip.
- **which one is right:** arguably the exporter's. Under it, a hard-panned mono clip lands in its destination channel at exactly the source's own level; under the live mixer's, it lands 3 dB above it. That is why this is filed as a divergence to resolve deliberately rather than "the export is broken."
- **the two real fixes**, neither taken here because both are wider than the feature that found this: (1) make `adapt_channels`' mono→stereo case power-preserving (`* FRAC_1_SQRT_2`), which changes the level of EVERY mono source in live playback — a real, audible change to existing behaviour that wants the owner's ear, not a subagent's judgement; or (2) plumb the probed `audio_channels` through `MediaVideoInfo` → the media pool DTO → `TimelineExportOptions`, and emit `pan=stereo|c0=c0|c1=c0` (a unity duplicate) for a known-mono source instead of `aformat` — exact, but it threads a new fact through the media pool and its Rust DTO for one edge case.
- **workaround meanwhile:** none needed for stereo sources. For a mono one, raise that clip's own `volume` by 3 dB (×1.41) if the export must match the preview exactly.
- **documented at the point of use:** `editor_get_capabilities`' `export.per_clip_level` entry states it, so an agent reading the capabilities before an export is told rather than left to measure it.

## B-102 — Total silence during live preview playback: `symphonia` was never built with MP3 decode support, so every `.mp3` clip fails to open and is swallowed silently

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: blocker (the owner's own real project has 4 audio tracks — SFX clicks/whooshes/chimes plus a full music bed — every one of them an `.mp3`; ALL of it was silent, on every playback) · area: `app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `crates/chroma-media/Cargo.toml` (symphonia feature flags)
- **found:** 2026-09-08, reported live by the owner ("BTW IN PREVIEW CANT HERE ANY AUDIO AT ALL") against their real `perf-comparison-reel-v3` project — 2 video tracks, 4 audio tracks (`click.mp3`, `whoosh.flac`, `chime.mp3`, `music.mp3`).
- **repro:** `CHROMA_TEST_AUDIO_VIDEO=<any .mp3 with real audio> cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib chroma::audio::tests::chroma_audio_play_produces_non_silent_pcm_end_to_end -- --exact --nocapture` — this integration test is gated on that env var and had evidently never actually been run with it set before tonight (a prior session summary's "28/28 passing, proves the mixer is correct" claim covered only the unconditional unit tests; every test that exercises a real file through the real `cpal` device — the one that would have caught this — was silently skipped for lack of the env var). Run for real: `peak=0`, and the swallowed error (temporarily surfaced via `eprintln!` in `chroma_media::audio::start`'s session thread, since `run_session` errors only ever reach a `log::warn!` with no logger backend installed) was `probe: unsupported feature: core (probe): no suitable format reader found`.
- **expected:** any audio-track clip with a real audio stream (mp3, flac, wav, aac, …) plays audibly during live timeline playback, matching the mixing math's own 28-test suite.
- **actual:** every `.mp3` source — clicks, whooshes, chimes, and the entire music bed, i.e. everything on the user's 4 audio tracks — failed to open in `chroma_media::open_source` (a `symphonia::default::get_probe().probe(...)` call), and that failure was swallowed on a background thread with no UI surface at all: `chroma_audio_play` itself still returns `Ok(())` (the spawn succeeded; the decode failure happens later, async, inside the spawned thread), so nothing in the frontend ever saw an error either.
- **cause:** `symphonia = { version = "0.6.1", features = [...] }` in both `app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` and `crates/chroma-media/Cargo.toml` listed `["isomp4", "aac", "alac", "aiff"]` — covering exactly video-embedded audio (mp4/mov containers, AAC/ALAC codecs) — with **no `mp3` feature**. `symphonia`'s own `default` features (`flac`, `wav`, `vorbis`, `pcm`, `mkv`, `ogg`, `adpcm`, `all-meta`) are additive on top of an explicit `features = [...]` list (Cargo only drops defaults with `default-features = false`, which this dependency never set) — which is why `whoosh.flac` was NOT affected, and why this bug was invisible to anyone testing with FLAC/WAV/embedded-video-audio and not specifically MP3. `mp3` (like `mp1`/`mp2`) is the one common format symphonia deliberately keeps OUT of `default` (it ships behind its own `symphonia-bundle-mp3` dependency), so it needs the same explicit opt-in `isomp4`/`aac`/etc. already got — it was simply never added.
- **fix:** added `"mp3"` to symphonia's feature list in both Cargo.toml files (kept in sync, as they already were). Verified via the real end-to-end test above: `peak=1.0267, rms=0.1889` against the project's own real `music.mp3` post-fix. Re-ran the FLAC case too (`whoosh.flac`, `peak=0.0790`) to confirm it was never actually broken, just already covered by `default`. Full `chroma::audio::` module (28 tests) still green.
- **worth noting for next time:** the real-device integration tests in this file (`CHROMA_TEST_AUDIO_VIDEO`/`CHROMA_TEST_SILENT_VIDEO`-gated) are the ONLY tests that would ever catch a codec-support gap like this — the unconditional unit tests all operate on synthetic in-memory buffers, never through `symphonia::probe` at all. They should be run with a real fixture set (one file per format actually used in this project — mp3, flac, wav, mp4/aac) at least once per session that touches the audio stack, not left to whichever file a past session happened to have on hand.
## B-103 — the ffmpeg export compiler never placed a clip in TIME: any clip at `start_frame > 0` rendered its last frame, frozen, for its whole window

**Status: fixed, 2026-09-08 (found while building D-226's transitions).**

**What was wrong.** `timelineExport.ts` gives every clip its own `-i` (with
`-ss`/`-t`) and composites it with
`overlay … enable='between(t, startSec, endSec)'` onto a shared `[base]`. Each
input decodes to a stream whose own timestamps start at ~0, and `overlay` pairs
its two inputs **by timestamp** (ffmpeg's `framesync`) — nothing ever shifted a
clip's stream to where that clip actually sits on the timeline. So a clip at
`start_frame > 0` had its real frames consumed against the base stream's opening
seconds, where its own `enable` gate was still shut, and then — once the gate
opened — showed nothing but its **last frame, repeated** for the rest of its
window (`overlay`'s default `eof_action=repeat`).

The second half of the same mistake: `overlay`'s `x`/`y` expressions run on the
main stream's clock (timeline seconds), but a clip's position keyframes were
emitted in **clip-relative** seconds, so a position animation on a clip at
`start_frame > 0` ran `start_frame / fps` seconds early.

**Why it survived this long.** The audio half of the same compiler always did
place its sources (`timelineExportAudio.ts`'s `adelay`, D-197) — only video
never grew the equivalent. And every existing real-ffmpeg test placed its clips
at frame 0, or used a flat colour, or a single clip: with either, a frozen frame
and the right frame are the same pixels. The pure argv tests string-match a
filtergraph and cannot see it at all. Confirmed live before fixing, not reasoned
about: a two-colour source (red for its first second, lime for its second)
placed at t=2 rendered **lime, frozen, for its whole window**.

**Fix.** `setpts=PTS[/speed]+<inputStart>/TB` at the **head** of every clip's
filter chain — timestamps only, no frames generated, no decode cost, the video
equivalent of `adelay`. Putting it first gives the whole chain one time base
(timeline seconds), which is what the two-time-bases confusion caused in the
first place; every expression authored against the clip's own in-point (its
keyframes, its D-147 fade, `overlay`'s `x`/`y`) is now explicitly re-based
through `(t − clipStartSec)` / `(T − clipStartSec)`, the exact re-basing
`buildTextDrawtextStep` already did for a title.

**Regression test.** `timelineExport.ffmpeg.test.ts` — "B-103: a clip at
start_frame > 0 renders its OWN REAL FRAMES at its own position". It builds the
two-colour source deliberately, because that is the only fixture shape that can
see this: it asserts red in the clip's own first second, lime in its second, and
black in the gap before it. The whole rest of the suite (856 TS tests) passes
unchanged, and the compiled argv is byte-identical for a clip at frame 0.

**Severity.** Real and silent for any sequential edit — which is the ordinary
use of an NLE. Masked in practice because the exporter's heaviest real use so
far has been stacked comparison reels, where both clips start at frame 0.

## B-104 — The on-canvas transform box detaches from the picture and drifts further the longer playback runs: three decoders each meant something different by "source frame N", and VFR footage made them disagree by up to 4 seconds

status: fixed (2026-09-08, D-228) · severity: high (the Edit tab's whole direct-manipulation surface — the transform box, and every position/scale keyframe authored through it — is wrong on any variable-frame-rate source, which means every macOS screen recording and most phone footage; the same fault silently mis-rendered exports and Colorist thumbnails) · area: `crates/chroma-media/src/{decode_pipe.rs,video.rs}`, `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/export.rs`
- **found:** 2026-09-08, reported live by the owner with a screenshot — "THE CANVAS BOX AND THE VIDEO CLIP IS DETACHED AND LAGGING :/" — against their real `perf-comparison-reel-v3` project (`b093-verify.chroma`), clip `e2192723-b56a-4bdc-8f1a-12a2b4f396c4` on track 1, source `scratch/reel-src/before-1.09.51pm.mov`, a heavily keyframed macOS screen recording.
- **repro:** put any **variable-frame-rate** source on the timeline (a macOS screen recording is the easy one — it only encodes a frame when the screen changes), give the clip position/scale keyframes, and play. Confirmed with the file's own numbers: `ffprobe` reports `avg_frame_rate` 126780/2873 ≈ 44.128, `r_frame_rate` 60/1, `nb_frames` 2112, `duration` 47.86 s — i.e. a real 1/60 s tick base with 47 holds of up to 0.567 s, not 60 fps CFR (which would have ~2872 frames).
- **expected:** the box tracks the picture. Frame `N` of a source means one thing to every part of the app.
- **actual:** the box and the picture separate by a growing amount for as long as playback continues, then snap back together on a scrub. Measured from the file's own PTS list: coded frame `k` and nominal frame `k` are up to **4.07 s apart** (≈180 frames, worst at k=866; +1.36 s the other way at k=1228).
- **cause:** not the probe, and not a single wrong line — three decode paths had each independently picked a different meaning for a frame index, and CFR footage had hidden the disagreement since D-015.
  - The **model** — `chroma_timeline::timeline_frames_to_source`, `Clip::source_start`/`duration`, `resolve_clip_transform`, the audio clock — says source frame `N` is *the picture at `N / source_fps` seconds*. That is the only meaning consistent with a CFR timeline, and it is what the keyframes the owner authored are indexed by.
  - `decode_pipe::FramePipe` and `export::spawn_decoder` instead seeked by time **once** and then counted **coded** frames — `-fps_mode passthrough` emits one output frame per coded frame — so after a respawn the pipe's index and the model's index walked apart at whatever rate the source's real frame timing differed from its average. This is why the drift *accumulated during playback* and *reset on a scrub*: a scrub forces a respawn, which re-synchronises the two at that instant.
  - `video::decode_frame` (the pipe's own error fallback) and the filmstrip chunk path seeked by time **per call**, so they were nearly right — but "nearly": checked against the file's PTS oracle at 49 grid indices, the bare `-ss N/fps` law returned a different frame from the true hold at **36** of them.
  - `video::extract_thumb_strip` selected on the filter graph's coded-frame counter (`select=not(mod(n,step))`) and returned those `n`s as frame indices, so every Colorist thumbnail was labelled with an index pointing somewhere else.
  - **`probe` preferring `avg_frame_rate` over `r_frame_rate` is NOT the bug**, though it looks like a suspect. The average is the only scalar that keeps `nb_frames`, `duration` and `Clip::duration` mutually consistent, and no scalar at all can describe unevenly-spaced frames. It was left alone deliberately — changing it would alter `source_fps` for every source and hence the meaning of every stored `source_start`/`duration`, i.e. a project migration, in exchange for nothing.
- **fix (D-228):** a new `crates/chroma-media/src/conform.rs` states the invariant once — *source frame `N` is the picture on screen at `N / source_fps` seconds, i.e. the last coded frame with PTS `<= N / source_fps`* — and builds the ffmpeg arguments that deliver it: `fps=<num>/<den>:start_time=0:round=up` under `-copyts`, with `-noaccurate_seek` and a half-frame-early `select=gte(t,…)`. All four decode paths above now build their commands through it, so they cannot disagree again. No transcode, no proxy, no cached artefact, no schema change, no migration — the conform is computed per decode, so `project.json` + the original file stay the only inputs to a render. `round=up` is the load-bearing part: measured against the real file's PTS oracle over 49 indices, `up` had 0 mismatches, ffmpeg's default `near` had 20, `down` had 29. Applied to CFR sources too, because it is free there (2.51 → 2.50 ms/frame measured) and one code path beats two.
- **regression tests:** `crates/chroma-media/src/decode_pipe.rs`, six new tests over an ffmpeg-synthesized VFR fixture with a known 2 s hold (`testsrc` at 30 fps, coded frames 30–89 dropped, timestamps preserved ⇒ `avg_frame_rate` exactly 15), plus `conform.rs`'s own 8 argument-construction tests. Three encode this exact fault and were **confirmed to fail against the pre-fix behaviour** (the old `-ss` + `passthrough` law temporarily restored inside `conform::from_frame`): `a_held_frame_is_held_for_every_grid_slot_it_covers` (mean|Δ| 0.47 where 0.0 is required), `a_frame_is_the_same_picture_wherever_the_pipe_was_opened` (1.69), `pipe_and_single_frame_decode_agree_on_a_vfr_source` (2.45). `a_cfr_source_is_unaffected_by_the_conform` passes both before and after, which is the point of having it.
- **verified against the real file:** `the_real_vfr_source_resolves_every_index_to_the_picture_on_screen` (env-gated on `CHROMA_TEST_VFR_VIDEO`) runs the actual `FramePipe` over the owner's own clip and checks each nominal index against `ffprobe`'s PTS list — an oracle that shares no code with the thing under test — asserting the argmin over a ±3 neighbourhood rather than a bare residual, since decoding forward from a seek and decoding from frame 0 legitimately differ in the last bit on non-IDR recovery points. All 8 probes correct, including the early frame 20 (→ coded 26) and the late frame 1600 (→ coded 1633) the report called for, and the worst-drift region (frame 866 → coded 969, 1020 → 1011).
- **verification gap (honest):** proven by test, by measurement against the real source, and by tracing — **not** by driving the real Tauri window and looking at the box. This pass ran in an isolated worktree while the owner's own `npm run tauri:dev` held the main checkout, and the transform box is drawn by the frontend overlay, so no backend-side capture can show box-vs-picture directly. What is proven is the half this bug actually lives in: the decoder now returns, for every index, the picture the box's own keyframe math was evaluated at. Same constraint D-125/D-130/B-058 recorded for their own fixes.
- **same family as B-075 / B-077 / B-079 / B-097, from the other side.** Those four are all "a frame number was interpreted in the wrong *unit*" — a SOURCE-frame count added to a TIMELINE-frame position without converting through `source_fps` (B-075 in the exporter, B-077 in the GUI, B-079 in the live engine), or off by one at a boundary (B-097 in `Track::clip_at`). Each was fixed by making a *consumer* of the model convert correctly. B-104 is the first one where the model and every consumer of it were already right and the **decoder** was the odd one out: the index was in the correct unit, and the picture fetched for it was not. Worth keeping the distinction, because the reflex those four trained — "check the `source_fps` conversion" — finds nothing here. The lesson B-104 adds is that a frame index is only meaningful if the thing that turns it back into pixels agrees about what it names, which is why `conform` exists as one module rather than a corrected line in each decoder.
- **adjacent finding, deliberately NOT fixed here:** `video::probe` records only a single scalar rate, so nothing in the app can currently *say* that a source is variable-rate — there is no `is_vfr` to show in the Inspector, warn on import, or expose over MCP. D-228 does not need it (the conform is unconditional and correct for both), so adding a field nothing reads would be dead code. Worth revisiting if a "this footage is VFR" affordance is ever wanted for the human/AI pair.

## B-105 — two `chroma::*` test modules raced over the process-global `current_video()`, so a relight test passed or failed purely on scheduling order

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: low (a test-suite isolation defect — no shipped behaviour is wrong; the cost is a `cargo test --workspace` that fails for a reason unrelated to whatever is being verified, which is exactly how a real regression gets waved through) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/state.rs` (`active_shot_change_busts_the_thumb_cache`) and `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/relight.rs` (`keyframed_light_without_a_loaded_video_falls_back_to_raw_fields`)

- **found:** 2026-09-08, during D-230's (adjustment clips) final verification. `cargo test --workspace` reported **two** failures where only the documented pre-existing B-097 was expected. The second was
  `chroma::relight::tests::keyframed_light_without_a_loaded_video_falls_back_to_raw_fields`, in a module D-230 never touched. Isolated before being explained rather than assumed pre-existing:
  - the relight test **passes alone** (`cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib <that test>` → ok);
  - the full suite **with D-230's new tests skipped** (`-- --skip preview_adjustment`) → only B-097 fails;
  - the full suite **with them** → B-097 *and* the relight test fail, reproducibly.
  That pattern says "order-dependent", not "broken by the new code".

- **root cause:** `current_video()` is process-global state (`chroma::state`), and it is read far outside the module that owns it — `chroma::keyframes::interpolated_parameters` consults it to decide whether to resolve a keyframed parameter against the current frame, and returns `None` (fall back to the raw fields) when no video is loaded. `chroma::relight`'s test asserts exactly that fallback, so its entire premise is `current_video() == None`. Meanwhile `chroma::state::tests::active_shot_change_busts_the_thumb_cache` called `set_current_video(Some(…))` **twice, took no lock, and never restored it** — leaving a video set for the remainder of the process. Whichever test won the race decided the outcome.

  **D-230 did not cause this; it exposed it.** Its new `preview_adjustment_tests` spawn real `ffmpeg` and decode real frames, which widened the scheduling window enough to turn a latent coin-flip into a deterministic failure. The defect had been present for as long as both tests have existed.

- **expected:** a test that depends on process-global state establishes that state; a test that mutates it restores it. Neither should be able to decide the other's result.

- **fix:** both halves, deliberately — fixing only the leaking side would leave the relight test passing by luck rather than by construction, and there are other `set_current_video` callers in the suite (`chroma::export`'s own tests, as `state.rs`'s existing comment already noted).
  - `active_shot_change_busts_the_thumb_cache` now takes `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` (the shared cross-module test mutex that exists for precisely this, `chroma/mod.rs`) and calls `set_current_video(None)` on the way out.
  - `keyframed_light_without_a_loaded_video_falls_back_to_raw_fields` now takes the same lock and sets `set_current_video(None)` itself, so its documented precondition is asserted rather than inherited.

- **verification:** `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib --no-fail-fast` run **three times**: 189 passed, 1 failed every time — and that one is B-097, the documented pre-existing failure, alone. Before the fix the same command gave 188 passed / 2 failed.

- **not fixed here, deliberately:** the underlying design smell — a process-global `current_video()` that a pure-parsing helper three modules away reads — is untouched. `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` is the existing, documented mitigation for exactly this class across `chroma::*` tests, and widening its use is the fix that matches the codebase as it is. Making `interpolated_parameters` take the frame as an argument instead of reaching for a global is the real repair and is a much wider change than a test-isolation bug warrants; noted here so it is on the record.

## B-106 — the three live-device audio playback tests read a once-per-second meter after 1.5 s, so they could fail for having nothing to read rather than nothing to hear

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: low (a test-robustness defect — no shipped behaviour is wrong; the cost is an env-gated audio test that fails intermittently for a reason unrelated to what it verifies, which is exactly how a real audio regression gets waved through) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs` (`chroma_audio_play_produces_non_silent_pcm_end_to_end`, `chroma_audio_play_mixes_a_genuine_audio_track_with_the_video_track`, `chroma_audio_play_with_a_muted_audio_track_still_plays_the_video`)

- **found:** 2026-09-08, while building D-232 (tape-style scrubbing). Running the `CHROMA_TEST_AUDIO_VIDEO`-gated suite for real against a real 44.1 kHz stereo file, `chroma_audio_play_mixes_a_genuine_audio_track_with_the_video_track` failed once with `rms=0.0000 peak=0.0000` while its two immediate siblings — playing the *same* file through the *same* engine — passed with real levels in the same process, seconds apart. Not reproducible on demand: 4 subsequent full runs of the same suite were clean. That pattern says "marginal timing", not "no sound".

- **root cause:** `chroma_audio_level` is not a live meter, it is a **once-per-window summary**. `chroma_media::audio`'s `build_typed` accumulates rms/peak over `out_rate × channels` interleaved samples — a full second of audio the device has actually written — and only then publishes to `LEVEL`, which `begin_request` resets to `(0.0, 0.0)` at the start of every session. Meanwhile a session's first few hundred milliseconds are device-open plus per-source probe/seek (D-125 measured that warm-up directly), during which the ring is legitimately silent and the callback is emitting `pull_or_silence` padding. So a test that sleeps 1500 ms is asking for a reading that lands, at best, a couple of hundred milliseconds after the single refresh becomes possible — and any scheduling hiccup, a slower disk, or a marginally longer probe pushes the refresh past the read. The test then measures the reset `(0.0, 0.0)` and reports it as silence.

  The two-source case is the one that showed it first for a mundane reason: it synthesizes its tone with a real `ffmpeg` invocation and opens **two** sources, so its warm-up is the longest of the three and it sits closest to the boundary. Nothing about the mixing is wrong — the same test measured `rms=0.0885 peak=0.6616` on the very next run.

- **expected:** a test whose assertion is "the device produced non-silent PCM" fails when the device produced silence, and never because the meter it reads had not ticked yet.

- **fix:** all three sleeps go 1500 ms → 2500 ms, with the reason stated at each. That is comfortably more than one full meter window past the end of any realistic warm-up, and it costs three seconds on a suite that only runs when the fixture env var is set at all. D-232's own sibling test (`chroma_audio_scrub_produces_non_silent_pcm_end_to_end`) was written against this finding from the start — a 2.4 s drag rather than the 1.2 s first drafted, for exactly this reason, which is how the defect in these three came to be understood.

- **not fixed, deliberately:** `chroma_audio_level`'s once-per-second cadence itself. It exists to be a cheap, low-frequency verification hook and a future meter feed (D-049), not a test instrument, and making it publish more often would put more work in a real-time audio callback to suit a test. The test adapting to the instrument is the right way round here.
## B-108 — the export interpolates a keyframed `rotation` the LONG way round while the live preview takes the shortest arc, so a clip animated 350° → 10° spins backwards through 180° in the rendered file

status: **open** (found 2026-09-08 during D-233; not fixed there — see below) · severity: medium (a real preview/export divergence on animation data, the B-090/B-094/B-095/B-098 family; narrow, because it needs a rotation animation that crosses the 0°/360° seam, but when it hits, the exported motion is not merely off by a little — it travels 340° in the opposite direction to what the user watched) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`keyframeExprAt`) vs. `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/keyframes.rs` (`lerp_value`)

- **found:** 2026-09-08, while adding per-segment easing (D-233) to both interpolators and checking that easing composed correctly with every existing rule on each side. It composes correctly with this one on the preview side; on the export side there was no rule to compose with.

- **the divergence.** The live preview's `lerp_value` special-cases `rotation`: it takes the **shortest signed arc** between the two keys, so `350 → 10` moves `+20°` forward through `0°` (its own doc, and `rotation_takes_the_shortest_arc` / `rotation_negative_arc` pin it). The export's `keyframeExprAt` has no such case — it is generic over the param name and emits a plain `y0 + (y1-y0)*…` ramp for every property alike, so the same two keys compile to a `-340°` sweep backwards through `180°`. Concretely, at the midpoint of that animation the preview shows `0°` and the export renders `180°` — the clip is upside down in the file and upright on screen.

- **why it was invisible.** Both sides are individually self-consistent and individually tested, and every existing rotation test on either side uses values that do not cross the seam (`0 → 90`, `0 → 45`), where linear and shortest-arc are the same answer. Nothing compared the two implementations *for rotation specifically*; D-208 established the filter-then-bracket order that made preview and export agree, and the shortest-arc rule sat inside `lerp_value` where the export path never went.

- **not fixed in D-233, deliberately.** D-233's subject is the ease curve, and easing is orthogonal to this: it warps the *rate* along whichever arc each side picks, so it neither causes nor worsens the divergence and the fix does not depend on it. Fixing it properly means teaching the export compiler the arc rule — `keyframeExprAt` would have to special-case `rotation` and emit the *unwrapped* target (`y0 + shortestArc(y0, y1)`) as each segment's endpoint rather than the stored `y1`, which is a real change to the compiler's per-param genericity and wants its own tests, including a real-ffmpeg one measuring the rendered angle mid-sweep. Landing that inside a curve-editor change would have made both harder to review and would have put an unrelated render-path change under D-233's verification. Filed here instead of fixed quietly, per this file's own purpose.

- **workaround until then:** key a seam-crossing rotation as a continuous, unwrapped number — `350 → 370` rather than `350 → 10`. Both renderers agree exactly on that, because there is no seam to disagree about; the preview's shortest arc from `350` to `370` *is* `+20`. `editor_set_clip_keyframes` stores whatever number it is given, so this is available to a human and an agent alike.

## B-107 — `chroma::audio`'s tests guarded the process-global open project with a mutex of their OWN, so they cleared it out from under `chroma::edit`'s adjustment-preview tests, which guard the same global with `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK`

status: fixed (2026-09-08) · severity: low (a test-suite isolation defect — no shipped behaviour is wrong; the cost is a `cargo test --workspace` that fails for a reason unrelated to whatever is being verified, which is exactly how a real regression gets waved through) · area: `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/audio.rs` (`session_test_guard`) and `app/src-tauri/src/chroma/edit.rs` (`preview_adjustment_tests::open_project`)

- **found:** 2026-09-08, during D-233's (timeline curve editor) final verification. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reported **three** failures where only the documented pre-existing B-097 was expected: `chroma::edit::preview_adjustment_tests::an_adjustment_clip_does_not_reach_a_track_above_it` and `::an_identity_adjustment_clip_changes_nothing` both panicked with `preview frame: "no project open — open one in the Colorist tab"`. Both pass in isolation, and the whole binary passes under `--test-threads=1` (221 passed / 1 failed, that one being B-097) — the signature of a parallel-scheduling race on a process-global, not a real defect in either feature. Nothing in D-233 touches project state; it was pre-existing and simply had not been hit in this scheduling order before.

- **cause — two different mutexes over one global, which is not mutual exclusion.** `chroma::edit`'s adjustment-preview tests, and every project-state test in `chroma::project` and `chroma::state`, serialise on the shared `chroma::PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` (the codebase's documented answer for this class — see B-105, which fixed the same shape one file over). `chroma::audio`'s `session_test_guard` instead locked a `static LOCK: Mutex<()>` **local to that function**. So the audio tests serialised against each other and against nothing else — while eleven of them call `state::set_project(None)` on their way out. That `None` could land in the middle of an adjustment test that was holding `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` and had already opened its own project, so the adjustment test's next `timeline_frame` found no project and panicked. The panic surfaced three layers from the cause, inside the JPEG decode helper, and reads at first glance like a fault in the adjustment-clip feature.

- **fix, both halves:**
  - **The leak.** `session_test_guard` now returns a guard on the shared `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` instead of its own private static. Every one of its eleven call sites is unchanged — they already said `let _guard = session_test_guard();` — so this is one lock over one global, not eleven new call-site edits to keep in step. It costs some test parallelism (these now serialise against every project-state test in the binary rather than only each other), which is the right trade and the same one B-105 made.
  - **The dependent test's own precondition.** `preview_adjustment_tests::open_project` now asserts `state::current_project().is_some()` immediately after setting it, with a message naming this bug. Holding `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` only excludes other holders of that same lock, so "I took the lock" is not by itself proof the global is what this helper just made it; if a *future* second lock ever appears, it fails loudly at the leak instead of three layers away.

- **verification:** `cargo test -p RapidRAW --lib --no-fail-fast` (parallel, the failing configuration) run **four times** after the fix: 221 passed, 1 failed every time — and that one is B-097, the documented pre-existing failure, alone. Before the fix the same command gave 219 passed / 3 failed. `--test-threads=1` gave 221/1 both before and after, which is what identified the race in the first place.

- **not fixed here, deliberately:** the underlying design smell — a process-global open project that tests must hand-serialise around at all — is untouched, exactly as B-105 left it. `PROJECT_STATE_LOCK` is the existing, documented mitigation for this class, and making every one of its readers take the project as an argument is a far wider change than a test-isolation bug warrants. What this bug adds to B-105's own note is the sharper lesson: the mitigation only works if there is exactly **one** lock. A second one beside it looks like protection and provides none, and is harder to spot than no lock at all.

## B-112 — a sped-up clip's PICTURE fades for `speed`× longer than its own sound and than the clip itself: the export divided the clip's length by the speed but not its fade windows

status: fixed (2026-09-08, in D-236) · severity: medium (a real, visible export defect on any clip that combines a `speedOverrides` entry with a D-147 fade; silent, because each half looks plausible on its own) · area: `packages/editor/src/timelineExport.ts` (`buildClipFilterChain`'s alpha/fade expression) vs. `packages/editor/src/timelineExportAudio.ts` (`buildAudioSourceChain`)

- **found:** 2026-09-08, while generalising the flat `speedOverrides` multiplier into the D-236 speed ramp. Routing every time quantity in the two chains through one remap function made the two spellings sit side by side, and they did not match.

- **the divergence.** Both chains run *after* the `setpts`/`atempo` node, so the `t`/`T` they see is already POST-speed output seconds. The audio chain converted every quantity into that axis — `lenSec`, `fadeInSec` and `fadeOutSec` were all divided by `speed`. The video chain divided only `lenSec` (`clip.duration / speedValue / clipFps`) and left the two fade windows as raw `frames / clipFps`, un-divided. So on a clip at 2x with a 24-frame fade-in, the sound faded up over 12 output frames and the picture over 24 — and since the whole clip is only half as long now, a fade that was a quarter of the clip became half of it.

- **why it was invisible.** Each side is individually self-consistent and individually tested, and every existing fade test uses an un-sped clip, where `speed == 1` makes the two spellings identical. Nothing exercised a fade and a speed override on the *same* clip. The two conversions also live in different files, one of which (`timelineExportAudio.ts`) documents its division explicitly as "what keeps a duck or fade CORRECTLY timed on a clip that also has a `speedOverrides` entry" — the video side simply never grew the equivalent, the same shape as B-103's own "the audio half always placed its sources; the video half never grew the equivalent."

- **fix:** all three quantities now go through one function — `outputAtSourceFrame(speedSegments, sourceFrame) / clipFps`, the D-236 ramp's own forward map — so the clip's length, its fade-in and its fade-out are obtained the same way and cannot drift again. For an un-sped clip that map is the identity and the emitted expression is byte-identical to before, which is why the whole existing export suite passes unchanged; for a flat-sped clip it is the division that was missing; and for a D-236 ramp it is correct by construction rather than by a second special case. A fade is authored as a number of SOURCE frames from the clip's in/out point, so under a ramp its real on-screen length is however long those frames take to play — a 12-frame fade-in on a 0.5x head is a full second.

- **why it is a real bug and not just a D-236 design note:** it is wrong today, on the pre-D-236 flat `speedOverrides` path, in a shipped export, with no ramp involved. D-236 is what surfaced it, not what caused it.
